They might invite her on board, and have tea and perhaps a run up the river. He seemed to visualise the launch moving easily between the tree-clad banks, Hilliard attending to the engine and steering, he and the brown-eyed girl in the taffrail, or the cockpit, or the well, or whatever you sat in on a motor boat. He pictured a gloriously sunny afternoon, warm and delightful, with just enough air made by the movement to prevent it being too hot. It would﻿ ﻿…
Hilliard’s voice broke in on his thoughts, and he realised his friend had been speaking for some time.

===Chinese===
“你真是太好心了，希利亚德。你要是觉得受得了一个无可救药的旱鸭子，那我干定了。”
这个提议竟让梅里曼如此心驰神往，连他自己都觉得意外。他喜欢划船出游，不过兴头很淡；吸引他的，当然不会是沿法国的运河与河流没完没了的航行，更不会是海——海的颠簸他深恶痛绝。也不是为了卡车号码那桩事。那桩事让他困惑，也让他感兴趣，他愿意知道谜底，可他的好奇心远没有热切到那个地步，不肯为满足它大费周章。无论如何，倘使希利亚德想要的是刺探，他可不干，因为他压根儿不信那套走私理论。不过等到了那一带，他想，顺路拜访一下科伯恩父女，总还是使得的。科伯恩小姐看上去挺孤单。能设法让她高兴高兴，倒也是件美事。他们可以请她上船，喝喝茶，或许再溯河兜上一圈。他仿佛已经望见那艘小艇悠然滑行在绿荫夹岸的水面上，希利亚德照管着机器、把着舵，他和那位褐色眼睛的姑娘坐在船尾栏杆边——或是舵舱里、井舱里，或者机动船上随便什么可坐的地方。他描绘出一幅阳光灿烂的午后图景：暖融融的，说不出的惬意，船行带起的风恰到好处，不至于热得难受。那该会是……
希利亚德的声音打断了他的遐想，他这才发觉，朋友已经说了一会儿话了。

===Original===
“She’s over-engined, if anything,” he was saying, “but that’s all to the good for emergencies. I got fifteen knots out of her once, but she averages about twelve. And good in a seaway, too. For her size, as dry a boat as ever I was in.”
“What size is she?” asked Merriman.
“Thirty feet, eight feet beam, draws two feet ten. She’ll go down any of the French canals. Two four-cylinder engines, either of which will run her. Engines and wheel amidships, cabin aft, decked over. Oh, she’s a beauty. You’ll like her, I can tell you.”

===Chinese===
“要挑毛病的话，就是机器功率太大，”他正说着，“不过遇上急事倒全是好处。有一回我从她身上逼出过十五节的速度，不过平均大约十二节。遇上风浪也稳当。就她这个尺寸，我平生坐过的船里，没有一条比她更干爽。”
“她多大尺寸？”梅里曼问。
“三十英尺长，八英尺宽，吃水两英尺十英寸。法国的运河她条条都过得去。两台四缸发动机，随便哪一台都能开动她。发动机和舵轮设在船腰，舱房在船尾，上面封着甲板。噢，她是个美人儿。我告诉你，你准会喜欢她。”

===Original===
“But do you mean to tell me you would cross the Bay of Biscay in a boat that size?”
“The Bay’s maligned. I’ve been across it six times and it was only rough once. Of course, I’d keep near the coast and run for shelter if it came on to blow. You need not worry. She’s as safe as a house.”
“I’m not worrying about her going to the bottom,” Merriman answered. “It’s much worse than that. The fact is,” he went on in a burst of confidence, “I can’t stand the motion. I’m ill all the time. Couldn’t I join you later?”

===Chinese===
“可你总不至于要告诉我，你打算驾着这么大点儿的船横渡比斯开湾（the Bay of Biscay）吧？”
“比斯开湾的名声是冤枉的。我横渡过六次，只有一回遇上风浪。当然，我会贴着海岸走，一起风就进港躲避。你尽管放心。她稳当得像座房子。”
“我担心的倒不是她沉底，”梅里曼答道，“比那糟糕得多。实话说了吧，”他忽然掏心窝子似的接着说，“我受不了船的颠簸。一上船我就一路难受。我不能晚点儿再跟你会合吗？”

===Original===
Hilliard nodded.
“I had that in my mind, but I didn’t like to suggest it. As a matter of fact it would suit me better. You see, I go on my holidays a week earlier than you. I don’t want to hang about all that time waiting for you. I’ll get a man and take the boat over to Bordeaux, send the man home, and you can come overland and join me there. How would that suit you?”
“A1, Hilliard. Nothing could be better.”
They continued discussing details for the best part of an hour, and when Merriman left for home it had been arranged that he should follow Hilliard by the night train from Charing Cross on the following Monday week.

===Chinese===
希利亚德点点头。
“我也有这个意思，只是不好意思先提。说实话，这样对我更合适。你知道，我比你早一个星期休假。我不想干等那么久等你来。我雇上一个人，把船开去波尔多，把那人打发回家，你再从陆路过来，在那儿跟我会合。这样安排，你觉得如何？”
“好极了，希利亚德。再好也没有了。”
两人又把细节商量了将近一个钟头。梅里曼动身回家时，一切都已商定：下下周一，他将从查令十字车站（Charing Cross）乘夜车动身，随后赶去与希利亚德会合。

## XI An Unexpected Ally / 十一、意外的盟友
===Original===
For several days Merriman, sick at heart and shaken in body, remained on at Bordeaux, too numbed by the blow which had fallen on him to take any decisive action. He now understood that Madeleine Coburn had refused him because she loved him, and he vowed he would rest neither day nor night till he had seen her and obtained a reversal of her decision. But for the moment his energy had departed, and he spent his time smoking in the Jardin and brooding over his troubles.
It was true that on three separate occasions he had called at the manager’s house, only to be told that Mr. and Miss Coburn were still from home, and neither there nor from the foreman at the works could he learn their addresses or the date of their return. He had also written a couple of scrappy notes to Hilliard, merely saying he was on a fresh scent, and to make no move in the matter until he heard further. Of the Pit-Prop Syndicate as apart from Madeleine he was now profoundly wearied, and he wished for nothing more than never again to hear its name mentioned.
But after a week of depression and self-pity his natural good sense reasserted itself, and he began seriously to consider his position. He honestly believed that Madeleine’s happiness could best be brought about by the fulfilment of his own, in other words by their marriage. He appreciated the motives which had caused her to refuse him, but he hoped that by his continued persuasion he might be able, as he put it to himself, to talk her round. Her very flight from him, for such he believed her absence to be, seemed to indicate that she herself was doubtful of her power to hold out against him, and to this extent he drew comfort from his immediate difficulty.
===Chinese===
接连好几天，梅里曼心病沉沉，身子也垮了，滞留在波尔多；落在头上的那一击把他击懵了，一时无力采取任何决断的行动。他如今明白了，玛德琳·科伯恩之所以回绝他，正是因为她爱他；他发誓，不亲眼见着她、求她收回成命，他昼夜都不得安息。可眼下一股锐气已经离他而去，他把时光都消磨在公共花园里，抽着烟，闷想自己的烦恼。
的确，他先后三次登门经理宅上，回回只听得一句：科伯恩先生和小姐仍旧离家未归；无论在宅上，还是从厂里的工头口中，他都打听不到他们的去处和归期。他也给希利亚德写过两张三言两语的便条，只说自己有了新的线索，在再听到消息之前，此事万万不可轻举妄动。至于撇开玛德琳不谈的坑木辛迪加，他如今厌烦透顶，别无所愿，只盼此生再别听见有人提起它的名字。
可是消沉自怜了一个星期之后，他天生的识见又占了上风，他开始郑重掂量自己的处境。他真心相信，玛德琳的幸福，最好莫如由他自己幸福的实现来成就——换句话说，就是由两人的婚姻来成就。她回绝他的那些缘由，他都体谅，但他指望凭着再接再厉的劝说，能够——照他对自己讲的那样——把她说转。就连她对他的躲避——他认定她的不告而别正是躲避——似乎也表明，她自己都拿不准能不能顶得住他；单凭这一点，他便从眼前的困境里多少汲取了一丝安慰。

===Original===
He concluded before trying any new plan to call once again at the clearing, in the hope that Mr. Coburn at least might have returned. The next afternoon, therefore, saw him driving out along the now familiar road. It was still hot, with the heavy enervating heat of air held stagnant by the trees. The freshness of early summer had gone, and there was a hint of approaching autumn in the darker greenery of the firs, and the overmaturity of such shrubs and wild flowers as could find along the edge of the road a precarious roothold on the patches of ground not covered by pine needles. Merriman gazed unceasingly ahead at the straight white ribbon of the road, as he pondered the problem of what he should do if once again he should be disappointed in his quest. Madeleine could not, he thought, remain indefinitely away. Mr. Coburn at all events would have to return to his work, and it would be a strange thing if he could not obtain from the father some indication of his daughter’s whereabouts.
But his call at the manager’s house was as fruitless on this occasion as on those preceding. The woman from whom he had received the note opened the door and repeated her former statement. Mr. and Miss Coburn were still from home.
Merriman turned away disconsolately, and walked slowly back across the clearing and down the lane. Though he told himself he had expected nothing from the visit, he was nevertheless bitterly disappointed with its result. And worse than his disappointment was his inability to see his next step, or even to think of any scheme which might lead him to the object of his hopes.
===Chinese===
他打定主意，在另试新计之前，先再到林中空地走一趟，指望至少科伯恩先生已经回来。于是第二天下午，他又坐车驶上那条如今已熟稔的路。天气依旧炎热，是那种被树木锁得滞闷、叫人乏力的热。初夏的清新已经消逝：冷杉的绿意深了一层，透出几分秋意将临的气息；沿路边的灌木野花——凡是在松针未覆的斑驳土地上勉强寻得一处立锥之地的——也都显出了熟透的疲态。梅里曼一面寻思着倘若此行再度扑空、自己该当如何，一面目不转睛地望着前方那条笔直的白色路带。他想，玛德琳总不能无限期地漂泊在外；科伯恩先生无论如何总得回来料理他的营生，倘若连从做父亲的那里都探不出女儿行踪的一点端倪，那可就怪了。
可是这一回登门，同前几回一样徒劳。接过信的那位妇人开了门，把先前的话又复述了一遍：科伯恩先生和小姐仍旧离家未归。
梅里曼怏怏地转身走开，缓步穿过林中空地，沿小径往回走。他虽对自己说，这趟来访本不指望什么，结果却仍旧让他失望到了极点。比失望更糟的是，他看不清下一步迈向何处，甚至连一条能引他通向心中所愿的路数也想不出来。

===Original===
He trudged on down the lane, his head sunk and his brows knitted, only half conscious of his surroundings. Looking up listlessly as he rounded a bend, he stopped suddenly as if turned to stone, while his heart first stood still, then began thumping wildly as if to choke him. A few yards away and coming to meet him was Madeleine!
She caught sight of him at the same instant and stopped with a low cry, while an expression of dread came over her face. So for an appreciable time they stood looking at one another, then Merriman, regaining the power of motion, sprang forward and seized her hands.
“Madeleine! Madeleine!” he cried brokenly. “My own one! My beloved!” He almost sobbed as he attempted to strain her to his heart.
But she wrenched herself from him.
“No, no!” she gasped. “You must not! I told you. It cannot be.”
He pleaded with her, fiercely, passionately, and at last despairingly. But he could not move her. Always she repeated that it could not be.
===Chinese===
他一路踽踽前行，头也低着，眉也锁着，对四下的境物只剩一半知觉。转过一道弯时，他恹恹地抬头一望，蓦地僵在原地，仿佛化作石头；他的心先是停了一拍，跟着狂跳起来，跳得几乎要把他噎住。几步开外，迎面走来的正是玛德琳！
同一瞬间她也望见了他，低呼一声收住脚步，脸上掠过一阵惊惧。两人就这样对望了好一会儿，随后梅里曼恢复了动弹的能力，抢上前去，一把攥住她的双手。
“玛德琳！玛德琳！”他语不成声地喊道，“我的人儿！我的爱人！”他把她往心口搂去，几乎要呜咽出来。
可她从他怀里挣脱了出去。
“不，不！”她喘着气说，“你不能这样！我说过的。这是万万不能的。”
他向她苦苦恳求，先是激烈，继而炽热，末了只剩下绝望。可他打动不了她。她翻来覆去只一句：万万不能。

===Original===
“At least tell me this,” he begged at last. “Would you marry me if this syndicate did not exist; I mean if Mr. Coburn was not mixed up with it?”
At first she would not answer, but presently, overcome by his persistence, she burst once again into tears and admitted that her fear of disgrace arising through discovery of the syndicate’s activities was her only reason for refusal.
“Then,” said Merriman resolutely, “I will go back with you now and see Mr. Coburn, and we will talk over what is to be done.”
At this her eyes dilated with terror.
“No, no!” she cried again. “He would be in danger. He would try something that might offend the others, and his life might not be safe. I tell you I don’t trust Captain Beamish and Mr. Bulla. I don’t think they would stop at anything to keep their secret. He is trying to get out of it, and he must not be hurried. He will do what he can.”
===Chinese===
“至少告诉我一件事，”他终于央求道，“倘若没有这个辛迪加，你肯嫁给我吗？我是说，倘若科伯恩先生没有搅在里头？”
起先她不肯回答，可不多时，被他的纠缠不过，又哭了出来，承认她唯一回绝的缘由，就是害怕辛迪加的勾当一旦败露、随之而来的耻辱。
“那么，”梅里曼斩钉截铁地说，“我现在就同你回去见科伯恩先生，咱们好好商量商量该怎么办。”
一听这话，她的眼睛惊恐地睁大了。
“不，不！”她又喊道，“他会身处险境的。他会做出什么开罪旁人的事来，性命说不定都保不住。我告诉你，我信不过比米什船长和布拉先生。为着保住他们的秘密，我看他们什么事都干得出来。他正设法脱身，万万催不得。他能做的，自会去做。”

===Original===
“But, my dearest,” Merriman remonstrated, “it could do no harm, to talk the matter over with him. That would commit him to nothing.”
But she would not hear of it.
“If he thought my happiness depended on it,” she declared, “he would break with them at all costs. I could not risk it. You must go away. Oh, my dear, you must go. Go, go!” she entreated almost hysterically, “it will be best for us both.”
Merriman, though beside himself with suffering, felt he could no longer disregard her.
“I shall go,” he answered sadly, “since you require it, but I will never give you up. Not until one of us is dead or you marry someone else﻿—I will never give you up. Oh, Madeleine, have pity and give me some hope; something to keep me alive till this trouble is over.”
===Chinese===
“可是，我最亲爱的，”梅里曼劝道，“同他把这件事商谈商谈，总不会有什么害处，又不至于把他套进半点约束里。”
可她说什么也不肯依。
“他要是想到我的幸福系于此事，”她断言道，“就会不惜一切代价同他们决裂。这个险我冒不起。你必须走。哦，亲爱的，你必须走。走吧，走吧！”她近乎失态地哀求道，“这样对咱们两个都最好。”
梅里曼虽说痛得几乎忘乎所以，却觉得再不能违拗她了。
“我走，”他凄然答道，“既然你这样要求。可我永远不会放弃你。除非我们两个有一个死了，或者你嫁了别人——我永远不会放弃你。哦，玛德琳，发发慈悲，给我一点指望吧；给一点东西，让我撑着活到这场祸事过去。”

===Original===
She was beginning to reply when she stopped suddenly and stood listening.
“The lorry!” she cried. “Go! Go!” Then pointing wildly in the direction of the road, she turned and fled rapidly back towards the clearing.
Merriman gazed after her until she passed round a corner of the lane and was lost to sight among the trees. Then, with a weight of hopeless despair on his heart, he began to walk towards the road. The lorry, driven by Henri, passed him at the next bend, and Henri, though he saluted with a show of respect, smiled sardonically as he noted the other’s woebegone appearance.
But Merriman neither knew nor cared what the driver thought. Almost physically sick with misery and disappointment, he regained his taxi and was driven back to Bordeaux.
===Chinese===
她正要作答，忽然收住话头，站着侧耳细听。
“卡车！”她失声道，“快走！快走！”说着朝大路的方向胡乱一指，转身朝林中空地飞奔而去。
梅里曼目送着她，直到她转过小径的一角，消失在树木深处。随后，他心头压着一团无望的绝望，朝大路走去。亨利驾驶的卡车在下一道弯处从他身边驶过；亨利虽然装出恭敬的样子行了个礼，可瞧见对方那副愁苦潦倒的模样，嘴角浮起一丝讥诮的笑。
至于司机心里想什么，梅里曼既不知道，也不在乎。他伤心失望得几乎要呕出来，回到出租汽车上，被载回了波尔多。

===Original===
The next few days seemed to him like a nightmare of hideous reality and permanence. He moved as a man in a dream, living under a shadow of almost tangible weight, as a criminal must do who has been sentenced to early execution. The longing to see Madeleine again, to hear the sound of her voice, to feel her presence, was so intense as to be almost unendurable. Again and again he said to himself that had she cared for another, had she even told him that she could not care for him, he would have taken his dismissal as irrevocable and gone to try and drag out the remainder of his life elsewhere as best he could. But he was maddened to think that the major difficulty﻿—the overwhelming, insuperable difficulty﻿—of his suit had been overcome. She loved him! Miraculous and incredible though it might seem﻿—though it was﻿—it was the amazing truth. And that being so, it was beyond bearing that a mere truckling to convention should be allowed to step in and snatch away the ecstasy of happiness that was within his grasp. And worse still, this truckling to convention was to save him! What, he asked himself, did it matter about him? Even if the worst happened and she suffered shame through her father, wasn’t all he wanted to be allowed to share it with her? And if narrow, stupid fools did talk, what matter? They could do without their companionship.
Fits of wild rage alternated with periods of cold and numbing despair, but as day succeeded day the desire to be near her grew until it could no longer be denied. He dared not again attempt to force himself into her presence, lest she should be angry and shatter irrevocably the hope to which he still clung with desperation. But he might without fear of disaster be nearer to her for a time. He hired a bicycle, and after dark had fallen that evening he rode out to the lane, and leaving his machine on the road, walked to the edge of the clearing. It was a perfect night, calm and silent, though with a slight touch of chill in the air. A crescent moon shone soft and silvery, lighting up pallidly the open space, gleaming on the white wood of the freshly cut stumps, and throwing black shadows from the ghostly looking buildings. It was close on midnight, and Merriman looked eagerly across the clearing to the manager’s house. He was not disappointed. There, in the window that he knew belonged to her room, shone a light.
He slowly approached, keeping on the fringe of the clearing and beneath the shadow of the trees. Some shrubs had taken root on the open ground, and behind a clump of these, not far from the door, he lay down, filled his pipe, and gave himself up to his dreams. The light still showed in the window, but even as he looked it went out, leaving the front of the house dark and, as it seemed to him, unfriendly and forbidding. “Perhaps she’ll look out before going to bed,” he thought, as he gazed disconsolately at the blank, unsympathetic opening. But he could see no movement therein.
===Chinese===
随后的几天，在他恍如一场噩梦，一场狰狞而真切、仿佛永无尽头的噩梦。他行动如同梦游，活在一片几乎压得出分量的阴影底下，就像被判了近期处决的罪犯必然度过的那种日子。想再见玛德琳一面，再听听她的声音，再感受她在身旁——这渴望炽烈得几乎无法忍受。他一遍又一遍对自己说：倘使她心属他人，哪怕她明白告诉他，她对他无意，他也会把这场回绝当作不可挽回，远走他乡，尽力把余生捱过去也就罢了。可一想到他求爱的那道大难关——那道压倒一切、无可逾越的大难关——竟然已经闯过，他就五内如焚。她爱他！这话无论显得多么神奇、多么难以置信——它偏偏是真的——这便是那惊人的事实。既然如此，竟容一纸向流俗屈从的苟且横插进来，把已到他掌心的幸福至乐生生夺去，这实在忍无可忍。更糟的是，这份向流俗的屈从，打的还是保全他的旗号！他问自己：他这个人又算得了什么？即便最坏的事发生，她因父亲而蒙羞受辱，他要的不正是获准同她分担吗？至于那些心地狭窄的蠢材要嚼舌头，嚼去好了，有什么要紧？没有这班人的交游，日子照样过得下去。
狂怒的阵阵发作，同冰冷麻木的绝望交替袭来；可日子一天天过去，靠近她的欲望有增无减，终于再压抑不住。他不敢再强行闯到她面前，生怕惹恼了她，把他仍死死攥着的那点指望无可挽回地击碎。但暂且离她近些，却不至招灾惹祸。他雇了一辆自行车，当晚天黑以后骑到小径口，把车留在大路上，步行到林中空地的边缘。这是一个完美无瑕的夜晚，安宁而静谧，只是空气里带一丝薄薄的凉意。一弯新月泻下柔和的银辉，惨淡地照亮空地，映着新伐树桩的白茬，又从那几幢鬼影幢幢的房子后面投下浓黑的影子。将近午夜，梅里曼热切地隔着空地望向经理住宅。他没有失望。在那扇他知道属于她房间的窗户里，正亮着一盏灯。
他慢慢挨近过去，贴着空地的边缘，藏在树影底下。空地上生出了几丛灌木，他躲在其中一丛后面——离宅门不远——躺了下来，装上烟斗，任由自己沉入梦想。窗里的灯还亮着，可就在他望着的当口，灯灭了，房子正面一片漆黑，在他眼里显得冷漠而拒人。“也许她睡前会朝外望望呢，”他郁郁地望着那个黑洞洞、无动于衷的窗口，暗自想道。可他看不出里面有半点动静。

===Original===
He lost count of time as he lay dreaming of the girl whose existence had become more to him than his very life, and it was not until he suddenly realised that he had become stiff and cramped from the cold that he looked at his watch. Nearly two! Once more he glanced sorrowfully at the window, realizing that no comfort was to be obtained therefrom, and decided he might as well make his way back, for all the ease of mind he was getting.
He turned slowly to get up, but just as he did so he noticed a slight movement at the side of the house before him, and he remained motionless, gazing intently forward. Then, spellbound, he watched Mr. Coburn leave by the side door, walk quickly to the shed, unlock a door, and disappear within.
There was something so secretive in the way the manager looked around before venturing into the open, and so stealthy about his whole walk and bearing, that Merriman’s heart beat more quickly as he wondered if he was now on the threshold of some revelation of the mystery of that outwardly innocent place. Obeying a sudden instinct, he rose from his hiding-place in the bushes and crept silently across the sward to the door by which the other had entered.
It was locked, and the whole place was dark and silent. Were it not for what he had just seen, Merriman would have believed it deserted. But it was evident that some secret and perhaps sinister activity was in progress within, and for the moment he forgot even Madeleine in his anxiety to learn its nature.
===Chinese===
他躺着梦想那个姑娘——她的存在，于他已重过自己的性命——把时间都忘了，直到猛然觉出自己冻得僵直痉挛，才看了看表。快两点了！他又怅然望了一眼那扇窗，明白从那里得不到半点慰藉，心想反正心里也安顿不下来，不如回去算了。
他缓缓转身要起身，正在这时，瞥见前面的房子侧面有一阵轻微的动静，他便一动不动，凝神望去。随后，他像被施了定身法，眼看着科伯恩先生从边门出来，快步走向棚屋，打开一扇门上的锁，闪身进去了。
经理在冒险现身空地之前先四下张望，那副鬼祟模样，连同他整个步态举止的悄然，让梅里曼的心跳快了起来：他寻思，此刻自己莫非正站在那处表面清白之地重重谜团的门槛上。他凭着一阵突发的本能，从灌木藏身处站起，蹑手蹑脚地穿过草地，摸到对方进去的那扇门前。
门锁着，整座棚屋一片漆黑寂静。若不是亲眼看见了方才那一幕，梅里曼准会当它是座空屋。可显然，里面正进行着某种隐秘、兴许还是凶险的活动；此刻他急于弄清它的底细，连玛德琳都暂且忘了。

===Original===
He crept silently round the shed, trying each door and peering into each window, but without result. All remained fast and in darkness, and though he listened with the utmost intentness of which he was capable, he could not catch any sound.
His round of the building completed, he paused in doubt. Should he retire while there was time, and watch for Mr. Coburn’s reappearance with perhaps some of his accomplices, or should he wait at the door and tackle him on the matter when he came out? His first preference was for the latter course, but as he thought it over he felt it would be better to reserve his knowledge, and he turned to make for cover.
But even as he did so he heard the manager say in low harsh tones: “Hands up now, or I fire!” and swinging round, he found himself gazing into the bore of a small deadly-looking repeating pistol.
Automatically he raised his arms, and for a few moments both men stood motionless, staring perplexedly at one another. Then Mr. Coburn lowered the pistol and attempted a laugh, a laugh nervous, shaky, and without merriment. His lips smiled, but his eyes remained cold and venomous.
===Chinese===
他绕着棚屋蹑足潜行，每扇门都试了试，每扇窗都凑近张望，却一无所获。处处紧闭、黑洞洞的；他侧耳细听，凝神到无以复加的地步，也捕捉不到一丝声息。
绕屋一周已毕，他停下来踌躇。是趁还来得及先撤下去，守着等科伯恩先生——也许还带着几个同伙——重新露面呢，还是守在这扇门前，等他出来时当面盘诘？他起初偏向后一条路，可再一掂量，觉得还是把自己知道的底牌捂住为好，便转身朝隐蔽处走去。
可就在转身的一刹那，他听见经理低沉凶狠地喝道：“举起手来，不然我开枪了！”他猛地回身，发现自己正对着一支小巧而杀气腾腾的连发手枪的枪口。
他机械地举起双臂，两个人有一小会儿都僵立不动，面面相觑，满脸错愕。随后科伯恩先生放低了手枪，勉强挤出一声笑——一声紧张、发颤、毫无欢意的笑。他唇边挂着笑，眼睛却仍旧又冷又毒。

===Original===
“Good heavens, Merriman, but you did give me a start,” he cried, making an evident effort to be jocular. “What in all the world are you doing here at this hour? Sorry for my greeting, but one has to be careful here. You know the district is notorious for brigands.”
Merriman was not usually very prompt to meet emergencies. He generally realised when it was too late what he ought to have said or done in any given circumstances. But on this occasion a flash of veritable inspiration revealed a way by which he might at one and the same time account for his presence, disarm the manager’s suspicions, and perhaps even gain his point with regard to Madeleine. He smiled back at the other.
“Sorry for startling you. Mr. Coburn. I have been looking for you for some days to discuss a very delicate matter, and I came out late this evening in the hope of attracting your attention after Miss Coburn had retired, so that our chat could be quite confidential. But in the darkness I fell and hurt my knee, and I spent so much time in waiting for it to get better that I was ashamed to go to the house. Imagine my delight when, just as I was turning to leave, I saw you coming down to the shed, and I followed with the object of trying to attract your attention.”
He hardly expected that Mr. Coburn would have accepted his statement, but whatever the manager believed privately, he gave no sign of suspicion.
===Chinese===
“天哪，梅里曼，你可真把我吓了一大跳，”他喊道，明显费着劲想显出打趣的样子，“深更半夜的，你到底在这儿干什么？方才多有得罪，可这地方不得不防。你是知道的，这地区是出了名的盗匪横行之地。”
梅里曼临机应变向来不算敏捷。任何一个特定场合里该说什么、该做什么，他一般是事后才明白，而那时已经太迟。可这一回，一道真正的灵光闪过，给他指出了一条同时办成三件事的路子：既能解释自己何以在此，又能卸去经理的疑心，说不定还能在玛德琳的事上如愿以偿。他朝对方报以微笑。
“对不起，吓着您了。科伯恩先生。我有一桩极难启齿的事，找您好几天了。今晚我深夜出来，是盼着等科伯恩小姐歇下之后再引起您的注意，好让咱们的谈话绝无人知。可黑暗里我摔了一跤，伤了膝盖，等它好些等了那么久，实在不好意思再登门。您想我该多么高兴：正要转身离开的时候，恰好看见您朝棚屋走来，我便跟了上来，想设法引起您的注意。”
他并不指望科伯恩先生会全盘相信这套说辞，可无论经理心里作何想法，他脸上没有露出半点疑色。

===Original===
“I’m glad your journey was not fruitless,” he answered courteously. “As a matter of fact, my neuralgia kept me from sleeping, and I found I had forgotten my bottle of aspirin down here, where I had brought it for the same purpose this morning. It seemed worth the trouble of coming for it, and I came.”
As he spoke Mr. Coburn took from his pocket and held up for Merriman’s inspection a tiny phial half full of white tablets.
It was now Merriman’s turn to be sceptical, but he murmured polite regrets in as convincing a way as he was able. “Let us go back into my office,” the manager continued. “If you want a private chat you can have it there.”
He unlocked the door, and passing in first, lit a reading lamp on his desk. Then relocking the door behind his visitor and unostentatiously slipping the key into his pocket, he sat down at the desk, waved Merriman to a chair, and producing a box of cigars, passed it across.
===Chinese===
“很高兴你这趟没有白跑，”他客客气气地答道，“不瞒你说，我的神经痛叫我睡不着，我想起那瓶阿司匹林（aspirin）忘在了这边——今天早晨也是为着同样的毛病把它带来的。值得为它跑一趟，我就来了。”
说话间，科伯恩先生从口袋里掏出一个小药瓶，举起来给梅里曼过目：半瓶白色药片。
这一回轮到梅里曼心里犯疑了，可他只低声说了几句得体的惋惜，尽可能说得恳切。“到我的办公室来吧，”经理接着说，“你想密谈，那边尽可畅所欲言。”
他开了锁，先进去，点亮书桌上的台灯。随后又在来客身后把门重新锁上，不动声色地把钥匙滑进衣袋，这才在桌后坐下，摆手请梅里曼就座，又取出一盒雪茄，递了过去。

===Original===
The windows, Merriman noticed, were covered by heavy blinds, and it was evident that no one could see into the room, nor could the light be observed from without. The door behind him was locked, and in Mr. Coburn’s pocket was the key as well as a revolver, while Merriman was unarmed. Moreover, Mr. Coburn was the larger and heavier, if not the stronger man of the two. It was true his words and manner were those of a friend, but the cold hatred in his eyes revealed his purpose. Merriman instantly realised he was in very real personal danger, and it was borne in on him that if he was to get out of that room alive, it was to his own wits he must trust.
But he was no coward, and he did not forget to limp as he crossed the room, nor did his hand shake as he stretched it out to take a cigar. When he came within the radius of the lamp he noticed with satisfaction that his coat was covered with fragments of moss and leaves, and he rather ostentatiously brushed these away, partly to prove to the other his calmness, and partly to draw attention to them in the hope that they would be accepted as evidence of his fall.
Fearing lest if they began a desultory conversation he might be tricked by his astute opponent into giving himself away, he left the latter no opportunity to make a remark, but plunged at once into his subject.
“I feel myself, Mr. Coburn,” he began, “not a little in your debt for granting me this interview. But the matter on which I wish to speak to you is so delicate and confidential, that I think you will agree that any precautions against eavesdroppers are justifiable.”
===Chinese===
梅里曼注意到，窗上都遮着厚厚的帘子，显然谁也望不进这间屋子，灯光也透不到外面。他身后的门锁着，科伯恩先生的口袋里既有钥匙又有左轮枪，而梅里曼手无寸铁。何况，即便算不上更强壮，科伯恩先生也是两人中块头更大、分量更沉的一个。他的言语举止固然像位朋友，可他眼里那股冷冷的恨意泄露了他的用心。梅里曼登时明白，自己正处在实实在在的人身危险之中；他也深切意识到，要想活着走出这间屋子，能靠的只有自己的急智。
可他不是懦夫。穿过房间时他没有忘了装瘸，伸手去接雪茄时，手也不曾发颤。走进灯光照到的范围，他满意地注意到外套上沾满了碎苔藓和树叶，便有意张扬地掸了掸：一半是向对方显示自己的镇定，一半也是引人留意它们，指望它们被认作自己摔过一跤的凭据。
他生怕闲扯起来，自己会被这个精明的对手诱出破绽，便不给对方插话的机会，开门见山径入正题。
“科伯恩先生，”他开口道，“蒙您赐见，我自觉欠情不浅。只是我要同您谈的事情，实在太过微妙、太过机密，我想您也会承认，防人偷听的种种布置都情有可原。”

===Original===
He spoke at first somewhat formally, but as interest in his subject quickened, he gradually became more conversational.
“The first thing I have to tell you,” he went on, “may not be very pleasant hearing to you, but it is a matter of almost life and death importance to me. I have come, Mr. Coburn, very deeply and sincerely to love your daughter.”
Mr. Coburn frowned slightly, but he did not seem surprised, nor did he reply except by a slight bow. Merriman continued:
“That in itself need not necessarily be of interest to you, but there is more to tell, and it is in this second point that the real importance of my statement lies, and on it hinges everything that I have to say to you. Madeleine, sir, has given me a definite assurance that my love for her is returned.”
===Chinese===
起初他说得有些拘谨，可随着谈兴渐浓，语气也慢慢随意起来。
“我要告诉你的头一件事，”他接着说，“您听来也许不太受用，于我却是几乎性命交关的大事。科伯恩先生，我已经深深地、真心实意地爱上了您的女儿。”
科伯恩先生微微皱了皱眉，但似乎并不惊讶，也没有答话，只略一欠身。梅里曼接着说：
“这件事本身未必与您相干，可还有下文；我这番话真正的分量正在第二点上，我要对您说的一切，也系于它。先生，玛德琳已经给了我一个明白的许诺：我对她的爱，得到了回报。”

===Original===
Still Mr. Coburn made no answer, save then by another slight inclination of his head, but his eyes had grown anxious and troubled.
“Not unnaturally,” Merriman resumed, “I begged her to marry me, but she saw fit to decline. In view of the admission she had just made, I was somewhat surprised that her refusal was so vehement. I pressed her for the reason, but she utterly declined to give it. Then an idea struck me, and I asked her if it was because she feared that your connection with this syndicate might lead to unhappiness. At first she would not reply nor give me any satisfaction, but at last by persistent questioning, and only when she saw I knew a great deal more about the business than she did herself, she admitted that that was indeed the barrier. Not to put too fine a point on it﻿—it is better, is it not, sir, to be perfectly candid﻿—she is living in terror and dread of your arrest, and she won’t marry me for fear that if it were to happen she might bring disgrace on me.”
Mr. Coburn had not moved during this speech, except that his face had become paler and the look of cold menace in his eyes seemed charged with a still more vindictive hatred. Then he answered slowly:
“I can only assume, Mr. Merriman, that your mind has become temporarily unhinged, but even with such an excuse, you cannot really believe that I am going to wait here and listen to you making such statements.”
===Chinese===
科伯恩先生仍旧没有答话，只是又微微颔首，可他的眼里已添了忧色与不安。
“不足为奇，”梅里曼续道，“我随即求她嫁给我，她却执意回绝。鉴于她方才已亲口承认了那一层，她回绝得那样决绝，我不免有些诧异。我追问缘由，她死活不肯吐口。后来我灵机一动，问她是不是害怕您同这个辛迪加的干系会招来祸殃。起先她也不答，也不给我半点准话，可经不住我刨根问底，直到她看出这桩营生的内情我知道的远比她本人多，她才承认那确是阻碍。恕我直言——把话说透总是好的，是吧，先生？——她如今就活在对您被捕的恐惧和惊惶里；她不肯嫁我，是怕一旦事败，会连累我蒙受耻辱。”
这一席话里，科伯恩先生一动未动，只是脸色愈见苍白，眼中的冷冷威胁仿佛充盈着一种愈发狠毒的仇恨。随后他缓缓答道：
“我只能认定，梅里曼先生，您的脑子是暂时出了毛病；可即便有这个说辞，您也断不会真以为，我会守在这儿听您说出这等话来。”

===Original===
Merriman bent forward.
“Sir,” he said earnestly, “I give you my word of honour and earnestly ask you to believe that I am approaching you as a friend. I am myself an interested party. I have sought this interview for Madeleine’s sake. For her sake, and for her sake only, I have come to ask you to discuss with me the best way out of the difficulty.”
Mr. Coburn rose abruptly.
“The best way out of the difficulty,” he declared, no longer attempting to disguise the hatred he felt, “is for you to take yourself off and never to show your face here again. I am amazed at you.” He took his automatic pistol out of his pocket. “Don’t you know that you are completely in my power? If I chose I could shoot you like a dog and sink your body in the river, and no one would ever know what had become of you.”
Merriman’s heart was beating rapidly. He had the uncomfortable suspicion that he had only to turn his back to get a bullet into it. He assumed a confidence he was far from feeling.
===Chinese===
梅里曼俯身向前。
“先生，”他恳切地说，“我以名誉向您担保，也恳请您相信，我是作为一个朋友来见您的。我自己便是利害攸关的一造。我求这次会面，是为了玛德琳。只为着她——单单为着她——我才来请您同我商量一条摆脱困境的最好出路。”
科伯恩先生霍地站起身来。
“摆脱困境最好的出路，”他声明道，再不打算掩饰心头的仇恨，“就是你趁早滚蛋，永远别再在这儿露面。你真叫我不知道说什么好。”他从口袋里掏出自动手枪。“你难道不知道，你完全捏在我手心里？只要我乐意，我可以像打一条狗一样崩了你，把你的尸首沉进河里，谁也不会知道你的下落。”
梅里曼的心怦怦急跳。他隐隐不安地疑心，自己只要一转身，背上就会挨进一颗子弹。他装出一副远非实感的镇定。

===Original===
“On the contrary, Mr. Coburn,” he said quietly, “it is you who are in our power. I’m afraid you don’t quite appreciate the situation. It is true you could shoot me now, but if you did, nothing could save you. It would be the rope for you and prison for your confederates, and what about your daughter then? I tell you, sir, I’m not such a fool as you take me for. Knowing what I do, do you think it likely I should put myself in your power unless I knew I was safe?”
His assurance was not without its effect. The other’s face grew paler and he sat heavily down in his chair.
“I’ll hear what you have to say,” he said harshly, though without letting go his weapon.
“Then let me begin at the beginning. You remember that first evening I was here, when you so kindly supplied me with petrol? Sir, you were correct when you told Captain Beamish and Mr. Bulla that I had noticed the changing of the lorry number plate. I had.”
===Chinese===
“恰恰相反，科伯恩先生，”他平静地说，“捏在人家手心里的，是您。恐怕您没有十分看清眼下的局面。您现在固然可以一枪打死我，可您要是真打了，就再没有什么能救您：绞索会套上您的脖子，您的同伙会锒铛入狱，到那时您的女儿又怎么办？我告诉您，先生，我不是您以为的那种傻子。我既知道我所知道的这些，您想，若非确知自己万无一失，我会把性命送到您手上吗？”
他这份笃定并非全无效果。对方的脸色更白了，颓然跌坐回椅子里。
“你说吧，我听着。”他哑声道，手却没有放开武器。
“那就容我从头说起。您总记得我初到此地的那个傍晚吧，承您好意，给我加了汽油。先生，您对比米什船长和布拉先生说，我注意到了换卡车车牌的事——您说得不错，我确实注意到了。”

===Original===
Mr. Coburn started slightly, but he did not speak, and Merriman went on:
“I was interested, though the thing conveyed nothing to me. But some time later I mentioned it casually, and Hilliard, who has a mania for puzzles, overheard. He suggested my joining him on his trip, and calling to see if we could solve it. You, Mr. Coburn, said another thing to your friends﻿—that though I might have noticed about the lorry, you were certain neither Hilliard nor I had seen anything suspicious at the clearing. There, sir, you were wrong. Though at that time we could not tell what was going on, we knew it was something illegal.”
Coburn was impressed at last. He sat motionless, staring at the speaker. As Merriman remained silent, he moved.
“Go on,” he said hoarsely, licking his dry lips.
===Chinese===
科伯恩先生微微一震，却没有说话，梅里曼继续道：
“我当时上了心，虽说那件事本身于我毫无意义。可后来不久，我随口提起了它，被对谜题素有癖好的希利亚德听了去。他提议我随他同游，顺路来此看看能否解破此谜。而您，科伯恩先生，对您的朋友们说了另一句话——您说，尽管我也许注意到了卡车的事，您却敢断定，希利亚德同我谁也没有在林中空地看出任何可疑之处。先生，这一点您说错了。我们当时虽说不出正在进行的是什么，却知道那是非法的勾当。”
科伯恩这回终于被镇住了。他一动不动地坐着，直勾勾盯着说话的人。见梅里曼不作声，他动了动。
“往下说。”他舔着干燥的嘴唇，哑声道。

===Original===
“I would ask you please to visualise the situation when we left. Hilliard believed he was on the track of a criminal organisation, carrying on illicit operations on a large scale. He believed that by lodging with the police the information he had gained, the breakup of the organisation and the capture of its members would be assured, and that he would stand to gain much kudos. But he did not know what the operations were, and he hesitated to come forward, lest by not waiting and investigating further he should destroy his chance of handing over to the authorities a complete case. He was therefore exceedingly keen that we should carry on inquiries at what I may call the English end of the business. Such was Hilliard’s attitude. I trust I make myself clear.”
Again Coburn nodded without speaking.
“My position was different. I had by that time come to care for Madeleine, and I saw the effect any disclosure must have on her. I therefore wished things kept secret, and I urged Hilliard to carry out his second idea and investigate further so as to make his case complete. He made my assistance a condition of agreement, and I therefore consented to help him.”
Mr. Coburn was now ghastly, and was listening with breathless earnestness to his visitor. Merriman realised what he had always suspected, that the man was weak and a bit of a coward, and he began to believe his bluff would carry him through.
===Chinese===
“请您设想一下我们离开时的局面。希利亚德相信，自己咬住了一个大规模从事非法勾当的犯罪组织的行踪。他相信，只要把到手的情报报送警方，这个组织的覆灭、党羽的落网便十拿九稳，他还能就此博得大大的名声。只是他不知道那勾当究竟为何，又迟迟不肯出头，生怕不等一等、再深挖一步，会毁掉他向当局交出一件全案的机会。因此他极力主张，由我们在——容我这么说——这桩生意的英国一端继续查访。这便是希利亚德的态度。我想我说得够明白了。”
科伯恩又只点了点头，没有出声。
“我的立场却不同。那时我已对玛德琳上了心，我看得见任何败露对她的打击。因此我但愿一切保密，还力劝希利亚德实行他的第二个主意，继续深查，把案子办成铁案。他以我出力相助为应允的条件，我便答应帮他。”
科伯恩先生此刻面如死灰，屏息凝神地听来客说话。梅里曼印证了他素来的怀疑：此人软弱，还有几分怯懦。他开始相信，自己这手虚张声势能撑到底了。

===Original===
“I need not trouble you,” he went on, “with all the details of our search. It is enough to say that we found out what we wanted. We went to Hull, discovered the wharf at Ferriby, made the acquaintance of Benson, and witnessed what went on there. We know all about Archer and how he plants your stuff, and Morton, who had us under observation and whom we properly tricked. I don’t claim any credit for it; all that belongs to Hilliard. And I admit we did not learn certain small details of your scheme. But the main points are clear﻿—clear enough to get convictions anyway.”
After a pause to let his words create their full effect, Merriman continued:
“Then arose the problem that had bothered us before. Hilliard was wild to go to the authorities with his story; on Madeleine’s account I still wanted it kept quiet. I needn’t recount our argument. Suffice it to say that at last we compromised. Hilliard agreed to wait for a month. For the sake of our friendship and the help I had given him, he undertook to give me a month to settle something about Madeleine. Mr. Coburn, nearly half that month is gone and I am not one step farther on.”
The manager wiped the drops of sweat from his pallid brow. Merriman’s quiet, confident manner, with its apparent absence of bluff or threat, had had its effect on him. He was evidently thoroughly frightened, and seemed to think it no longer worthwhile to plead ignorance. As Merriman had hoped and intended, he appeared to conclude that conciliation would be his best chance.
===Chinese===
“我们查访的种种细节，就不必絮叨给您听了。只需说一句：我们查明了想查明的。我们去了赫尔，寻到了费里比的码头，结识了本森，也亲眼目睹了那里干的勾当。阿彻的底细我们一清二楚——他怎样替你们撒货；莫顿也是——他把我们置于监视之下，倒叫我们好好戏耍了一场。这份功劳我不冒领，全都归于希利亚德。我也承认，你们方案中某些细枝末节，我们未能尽知。但大关节是清楚的——至少清楚得足以定罪了。”
梅里曼顿了一顿，让这番话充分发挥效力，然后接着说：
“于是又冒出了先前困扰我们的那个难题。希利亚德恨不得立刻把这一切报官；我为着玛德琳，仍想压下不声张。我们的争执不必细表，只说结果：我们各让一步，希利亚德答应等一个月。看在我们交情的份上，也看我帮过他的忙，他许给我一个月，料理玛德琳这边的事情。科伯恩先生，这一个月快过去一半了，我却寸步未进。”
经理抹去了灰白额头上的汗珠。梅里曼那份安静、笃定的神气——看似不带半点讹诈与恫吓——在他身上起了作用。他显然吓破了胆，似乎觉得再装糊涂也已无谓。正如梅里曼指望并筹算的那样，他看来得出了结论：讲和才是最好的生路。

===Original===
“Then no one but you two know so far?” he asked, a shifty, sly look passing over his face.
Merriman read his thoughts and bluffed again.
“Yes and no,” he answered. “No one but we two know at present. On the other hand, we have naturally taken all reasonable precautions. Hilliard prepared a full statement of the matter which we both signed, and this he sent to his banker with a request that unless he claimed it in person before the given date, the banker was to convey it to Scotland Yard. If anything happens to me here, Hilliard will go at once to the Yard, and if anything happens to him our document will be sent there. And in it we have suggested that if either of us disappear, it will be equivalent to adding murder to the other charges made.”
It was enough. Mr. Coburn sat, broken and completely cowed. To Merriman he seemed suddenly to have become an old man. For several minutes silence reigned, and then at last the other spoke.
“What do you want me to do?” he asked, in a tremulous voice, hardly louder than a whisper.
===Chinese===
“那么说，到目前为止，知道的只有你们两个？”他问，脸上掠过一丝狡黠阴滑的神色。
梅里曼看穿了他的心思，又虚晃了一枪。
“是，也不是，”他答道，“眼下知道的，确实只有我们两个。可另一方面，我们自然也做了该做的一切防备。希利亚德把全部案情写成了一份陈书，我们两个都签了名；他把这份东西交给了他的银行家，并嘱托说：除非他本人赶在指定期限之前亲自取回，银行家须把它转交苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）。我若在此地出了事，希利亚德会立刻去苏格兰场；他若出了事，我们那份文书就会被送到那里。而且我们在里面写明：我们两人无论哪个失踪，都等于在其余罪名之外，再加一条谋杀。”
这就够了。科伯恩先生瘫坐着，精神垮了，彻底被制服了。在梅里曼眼里，他仿佛霎时变成了一个老人。有好几分钟一片寂静，末了对方终于开口。
“你要我做什么？”他问，声音发颤，比耳语高不了多少。

===Original===
Merriman’s heart leaped.
“To consider your daughter, Mr. Coburn,” he answered promptly. “All I want is to marry Madeleine, and for her sake I want you to get out of this thing before the crash comes.”
Mr. Coburn once more wiped the drops of sweat from his forehead.
“Good lord!” he cried hoarsely. “Ever since it started I have been trying to get out of it. I was forced into it against my will and I would give my soul if I could do as you say and get free. But I can’t﻿—I can’t.”
He buried his head in his hands and sat motionless, leaning on his desk.
“But your daughter, Mr. Coburn,” Merriman persisted. “For her sake something must be done.”
===Chinese===
梅里曼的心猛地一跳。
“想想您的女儿，科伯恩先生，”他应声答道，“我别无所求，只求娶到玛德琳；为着她，我求您赶在大祸临头之前，从这桩事里脱出身来。”
科伯恩先生又一次抹去额头上的汗珠。
“老天爷！”他嘶哑地喊道，“打这事开头起，我就一直在设法脱身。我是被硬违背本心拽进来的，倘若能照你说的那样得到自由，把魂灵交给魔鬼我都愿意。可是不成——不成啊。”
他把头埋进双手，伏在书桌上，一动不动地坐着。
“可是您的女儿，科伯恩先生，”梅里曼不肯放松，“为着她，总得想个办法。”

===Original===
Mr. Coburn shook his clenched fists in the air.
“Damnation take you!” he cried, with a sudden access of rage, “do you think I care about myself? Do you think I’d sit here and listen to you talking as you’ve done if it wasn’t for her? I tell you I’d shoot you as you sit, if I didn’t know from my own observation that she is fond of you. I swear it’s the only thing that has saved you.” He rose to his feet and began pacing jerkily to and fro. “See here,” he continued wildly, “go away from here before I do it. I can’t stand any more of you at present. Go now and come back on Friday night at the same time, and I’ll tell you of my decision. Here’s the key,” he threw it down on the desk. “Get out quick before I do for you!”
Merriman was for a moment inclined to stand his ground, but, realizing that not only had he carried his point as far as he could have expected, but also that his companion was in so excited a condition as hardly to be accountable for his actions, he decided discretion was the better part, and merely saying: “Very well, Friday night,” he unlocked the door and took his leave.
===Chinese===
科伯恩先生把攥紧的双拳在空中乱挥。
“叫天谴劈了你！”他勃然大怒，狂喊道，“你当我心疼的是我自己？要不是为她，你以为我肯坐在这儿，听你说这一车的话？告诉你，要不是我从自己眼里看出她心里有你，你这么坐着，我早一枪崩了你了。我起誓，就这一件事救了你的命。”他站起身，抽搐似的来回疾走。“你听着，”他狂乱地接着说，“趁我还没动手，快从这儿滚。眼下你这个人在我跟前多待一刻我也受不住。现在就走，星期五夜里还是这个时辰再来，我把我的决定告诉你。钥匙在这儿，”他把它摔在桌上，“趁我没结果你，快滚！”
有一瞬间，梅里曼想坚持不走；可转念一想，此行所求不但达到了所能期望的极限，而且对方正处在那样亢奋的状态里，行事全然做不得准，便觉得还是谨慎为上。他只说了一句：“好吧，星期五夜里。”便开了门锁，告辞而去。

===Original===
On the whole he was well pleased with his interview. In the first place, he had by his readiness escaped an imminent personal danger. What was almost as important, he had broken the ice with Mr. Coburn about Madeleine, and the former had not only declared that he was aware of the state of his daughter’s feelings, but he had expressed no objection to the proposed match. Further, an understanding as to Mr. Coburn’s own position had been come to. He had practically admitted that the syndicate was a felonious conspiracy, and had stated that he would do almost anything to get out of it. Finally he had promised a decision on the whole question in three days’ time. Quite a triumph, Merriman thought.
On the other hand he had given the manager a warning of the danger which the latter might communicate to his fellow-conspirators, with the result that all of them might escape from the net in which Hilliard, at any rate, wished to enmesh them. And just to this extent he had become a copartner in their crime. And though it was true that he had escaped from his immediate peril, he had undoubtedly placed himself and Hilliard in very real danger. It was by no means impossible that the gang would decide to murder both of the men whose knowledge threatened them, in the hope of bluffing the bank manager out of the letter which they would believe he held. Merriman had invented this letter on the spur of the moment and he would have felt a good deal happier if he knew that it really existed. He decided that he would write to Hilliard immediately and get him to make it a reality.
A great deal, he thought, depended on the character of Coburn. If he was weak and cowardly he would try to save his own skin and let the others walk into the net. Particularly might he do this if he had suffered at their hands in the way he suggested. On the other hand, a strong man would undoubtedly consult his fellow-conspirators and see that a pretty determined fight was made for their liberty and their source of gain.
===Chinese===
总的说来，他对这次会谈相当满意。第一，凭着眼疾手快，他躲过了一场迫在眉睫的人身危险。几乎同样要紧的是，他就玛德琳的事同科伯恩先生把话挑明了；后者不但表明他知道女儿的心事，对这门亲事也未置一词反对。再者，关于科伯恩先生自身的处境，两人也达成了谅解：他实际上已经承认，这个辛迪加是一桩重罪阴谋，也表明只要能脱身，他几乎什么都肯做。末了，他还答应三天之内就整个问题给出决定。梅里曼想，这算得上一场大捷了。
可另一方面，他也向经理泄露了危险的警示；后者很可能把它传递给同伙，结果便是：那一伙人或许尽数从网里溜走——这张网，至少希利亚德是想把他们一网兜进的。而恰恰到此地步，他已成了他们罪案的同谋。虽说躲过眼下的急难不假，他无疑也把自己和希利亚德推进了实实在在的危险之中。那伙人完全有可能决定，把两个知情之人一并杀掉灭口，指望从银行经理那里诈出那封他们认定他握着的信——那封信原是梅里曼情急之下信口编出来的，倘若它当真存在，他心里本会踏实得多。他决定立刻写信给希利亚德，让它变成现实。
他想，往后种种，大半要看科伯恩的品性。他若软弱怯懦，便会设法自保皮肉，听任其余的人踏进网里；倘若他真如自己话里暗示的那样，在他们手上吃过苦头，就尤其会如此。反过来，一个硬气的人，必定会去同党羽们商议，务必为他们的自由和财源放手拼上一场。

===Original===
He had thought of all this when it suddenly flashed into his mind that Mr. Coburn’s presence in the shed at two in the morning in itself required a lot of explanation. He did not for a moment believe the aspirin story. The man had looked so shifty while he was speaking, that even at the time Merriman had decided he was lying. What then could he have been doing?
He puzzled over the questions but without result. Then it occurred to him that as he was doing nothing that evening he might as well ride out again to the clearing and see if any nocturnal activities were undertaken.
Midnight therefore found him once more ensconced behind a group of shrubs in full view of both the house and the shed. It was again a perfect night, and again he lay dreaming of the girl who was so near in body and in spirit, and yet so infinitely far beyond his reach.
===Chinese===
这些他都想过了，忽然又灵光一闪：科伯恩先生凌晨两点身在棚屋，这件事本身便很需要一番解释。那套阿司匹林的说辞，他一刻也不曾相信。那人说话时神情那样闪烁，当时梅里曼便断定他在撒谎。那么，他究竟可能在干什么呢？
他绕着这些问题百思不解，却毫无结果。后来他想到，反正当晚无事，不如再骑车到林中空地去一趟，看看夜里会不会有什么活动。
于是午夜时分，他又一次安顿在一丛灌木后面，住宅和棚屋都尽收眼底。又是一个完美的夜晚，他又躺着梦想那个姑娘——身与心都这样近，却又远在无穷、不可企及的天边。

===Original===
Time passed slowly, but the hours wore gradually round until his watch showed two o’clock. Then, just as he was thinking that he need hardly wait much longer, he was considerably thrilled to see Mr. Coburn once more appear at the side door of the house, and in the same stealthy, secretive way as on the previous night, walk hurriedly to the shed and let himself in by the office door.
At first Merriman thought of following him again in the hope of learning the nature of these strange proceedings, but a moment’s thought showed him he must run no risk of discovery. If Coburn learned that he was being spied on he would at once doubt Merriman’s statement that he knew the syndicate’s secret. It would be better, therefore, to lie low and await events.
But the only other interesting event that happened was that some fifteen minutes later the manager left the shed, and with the same show of secrecy returned to his house, disappearing into the side door.
===Chinese===
时间过得很慢，钟点却渐渐磨到了表针指向两点。正当他想差不多不必再等下去的时候，忽见科伯恩先生又一次出现在住宅边门口，不由得心头猛地一紧；同前一夜一样，那人鬼鬼祟祟、遮遮掩掩地疾步走向棚屋，从办公室那扇门进去了。
起初梅里曼想再跟上去，指望探明这些古怪行径的究竟，可略一转念便明白，万万冒不得被发觉的风险。科伯恩若察觉自己被人盯梢，立刻就会怀疑梅里曼那套“已尽知辛迪加秘密”的说辞。因此，还是按兵不动、静观其变为上。
可接下来值得一提的事只有一件：约莫一刻钟后，经理出了棚屋，又摆出同样遮掩的做派回到住宅，闪身进了边门。

===Original===
So intrigued was Merriman by the whole business that he determined to repeat his visit the following night also. He did so, and once again witnessed Mr. Coburn’s stealthy walk to the shed at two a.m., and his equally stealthy return at two-fifteen.
Rack his brains as he would over the problem of these nocturnal visits, Merriman could think of no explanation. What for three consecutive nights could bring the manager down to the sawmill? He could not imagine, but he was clear it was not the pit-prop industry.
If the *Girondin* had been in he would have once more suspected smuggling, but she was then at Ferriby. No, it certainly did not work in with smuggling. Still less did it suggest false note printing, unless﻿—Merriman’s heart beat more quickly as a new idea entered his mind. Suppose the notes were printed there, at the mill! Suppose there was a cellar under the engine house, and suppose the work was done at night? It was true they had not seen signs of a cellar, but if this surmise was correct it was not likely they would.
===Chinese===
这整桩事把梅里曼的心思吊得死死的，他决意第二天夜里再照去一趟。他去了，又一次亲眼看见科伯恩先生凌晨两点蹑足走向棚屋，两点一刻又同样蹑足而归。
这几次夜访的难题，梅里曼绞尽脑汁，也想不出个所以然。接连三夜，究竟能有什么事把经理引到锯木厂来？他想象不出，但他敢断定，那绝非坑木营生。
倘若*吉伦丁号*在港，他会再度疑心走私；可她那时正在费里比。不，这同走私怎么也对不上。它更不像假钞印制的迹象——除非——一个新念头钻进脑海，梅里曼的心跳加快了。倘若钞票就是在那儿、在厂里印的呢！倘若机房底下有一间地窖，活计都在夜里干呢？他们确实没见过地窖的迹象，可这个揣度若真，人家本也就不会让他们看见。

===Original===
At first sight this theory seemed a real advance, but a little further thought showed it had serious objections. Firstly, it did not explain Coburn’s nightly visits. If the manager had spent some hours in the works it might have indicated the working of a press, but what in that way could be done in fifteen minutes? Further, and this seemed to put the idea quite out of court, if the notes were being produced at the clearing, why the changing of the lorry numbers? That would then be a part of the business quite unconnected with the illicit traffic. After much thought, Merriman had to admit to himself that here was one more of the series of insoluble puzzles with which they found themselves faced.
The next night was Friday, and in accordance with the arrangement made with Mr. Coburn, Merriman once again went out to the clearing, presenting himself at the works door at two in the morning. Mr. Coburn at once opened to his knock, and after locking the door, led the way to his office. There he wasted no time in preliminaries.
“I’ve thought this over, Merriman,” he said, and his manner was very different from that of the previous interview, “and I’m bound to say that I’ve realised that, though interested, your action towards me has been correct not to say generous. Now I’ve made up my mind what to do, and I trust you will see your way to fall in with my ideas. There is a meeting of the syndicate on Thursday week. I should have been present in any case, and I have decided that, whatever may be the result, I will tell them I am going to break with them. I will give ill-health as my reason for this step, and fortunately or unfortunately I can do this with truth, as my heart is seriously diseased. I can easily provide the necessary doctor’s certificates. If they accept my resignation, well and good﻿—I will emigrate to my brother in South America, and you and Madeleine can be married. If they decline, well”﻿—Mr. Coburn shrugged his shoulders﻿—“your embarrassment will be otherwise removed.”
He paused. Merriman would have spoken, but Mr. Coburn held up his hand for silence and went on:
===Chinese===
乍看之下，这个说法似乎大有进展，可略再深思，便显出严重的破绽。其一，它解释不了科伯恩的每夜来访：倘若经理在厂里一待几个钟头，倒可能是在开动印机；可一刻钟能干得了什么？再者——这一点似乎足以把这个念头整个否掉——倘若钞票当真产自林中空地，又何必换卡车的车牌？那样它就成了同非法贩运全不相干的一截闲棋。苦苦思索之后，梅里曼只得承认：这又是横在他们面前那一系列无解难题中的一道。
下一夜便是星期五。依照同科伯恩先生的约订，梅里曼再次前往林中空地，凌晨两点到厂门上应约。科伯恩先生应声开门，锁上门，领他到办公室去。到了那里，他不赘一句虚文。
“这事我反复想过了，梅里曼，”他说，神态同上一次会谈已判若两人，“我不得不说，我意识到：尽管你自有利害在其中，你对我的行事却堪称得体，甚至可说宽厚。如今我已拿定主意要怎么做了，也相信你会乐意成全我的安排。下星期四，辛迪加有一次聚会。无论如何我本来就得到场，我已决意，无论结果如何，都要当场宣布同他们决裂。这一步，我将以健康不济为由——幸或不幸，这倒并非虚言，我的心脏确实害了重病。必要的医生证明，我轻而易举就能弄到。他们若接受我的退出，那就再好不过——我就移居南美洲（South America）去投奔我兄弟，你和玛德琳便可以成婚。他们若不答应，唉——”科伯恩先生耸了耸肩——“你的难题自会另有解除之法。”
他停了停。梅里曼正要开口，科伯恩先生却抬手示意噤声，继续说道：

===Original===
“I confess I have been terribly upset for the last three days to discover my wisest course, and even now I am far from certain that my decision is best. I do not want to go back on my former friends, and on account of Madeleine I cannot go back on you. Therefore, I cannot warn the others of their danger, but on the other hand I won’t give your life into their hands. For if they knew what I know now, you and Hilliard would be dead men inside twenty-four hours.”
Mr. Coburn spoke simply and with a certain dignity, and Merriman found himself disposed not only to believe what he had heard, but even to understand and sympathise with the man in the embarrassing circumstances in which he found himself. That his difficulties were of his own making there could be but little doubt, but how far he had put himself in the power of his associates through deliberate evildoing, and how far through mistakes or weakness, there was of course no way of learning.
At the end of an hour’s discussion, Mr. Coburn had agreed at all costs to sever his connection with the syndicate, to emigrate to his brother in Chile, and to do his utmost to induce his daughter to remain in England to marry Merriman. On his side, Merriman undertook to hold back the lodging of information at Scotland Yard for one more week, to enable the other’s arrangements to be carried out.
There being nothing to keep him in Bordeaux, Merriman left for London that day, and the next evening he was closeted with Hilliard in the latter’s rooms, discussing the affair. Hilliard at first was most unwilling to postpone their visit to the Yard but he agreed on Merriman’s explaining that he had pledged himself to the delay.
===Chinese===
“我承认，这三天来，为了找出最稳妥的一步棋，我心乱得厉害；即便此刻，我也远不敢说自己的决定就是上策。我不愿背弃旧日的朋友，而为了玛德琳，我又不能背弃你。所以，我无法向其余的人警示他们的危险；可另一方面，我也决不会把你的性命交到他们手里。因为，他们若知道了我如今知道的这一切，不出二十四小时，你同希利亚德就得变成死人。”
科伯恩先生说得很朴实，也自有一种尊严。梅里曼发觉，自己不但倾向于相信听到的一切，甚至还能体谅并同情这个人身处的那份尴尬境地。他的困境多半是自作自受，这几乎没有疑问；可他在多大程度上是因蓄意为恶而受制于同伙，又有几分是出于失误或软弱，当然就无从究诘了。
商谈了一个钟头之后，科伯恩先生终于应承：不惜一切代价同辛迪加一刀两断，移居智利（Chile）投奔他的兄弟，并竭力劝说女儿留在英国，同梅里曼成婚。梅里曼这方面则承诺：把向苏格兰场报案之事再压上一个星期，好让对方从容布置。
波尔多既无事可留，梅里曼当天便动身去伦敦；第二天晚上，他同希利亚德关在后者的寓所里密谈此事。希利亚德起先极不情愿推迟去苏格兰场，可听梅里曼说明他已作过承诺，也就应允了。

===Original===
So the days, for Merriman heavily weighted with anxiety and suspense, began slowly to drag by. His fate and the fate of the girl he loved hung in the balance, and not the least irksome feature of his position was his own utter impotence. There was nothing that he could do﻿—no action which would take him out of himself and ease the tension of his thoughts. As day succeeded day and the silence remained unbroken, he became more and more upset. At the end of a week he was almost beside himself with worry and chagrin, so much so that he gave up attending his office altogether, and was only restrained from rushing back to Bordeaux by the knowledge that to force himself once more on Madeleine might be to destroy, once and for ever, any hopes he might otherwise have had.
It was now four days since the Thursday on which Mr. Coburn had stated that the meeting of the syndicate was to have been held, and only three days to the date on which the friends had agreed to tell their story at Scotland Yard. What if he received no news during those three days? Would Hilliard agree to a further postponement? He feared not, and he was racked with anxiety as to whether he should cross that day to France and seek another interview with Mr. Coburn.
But, even as he sat with the morning paper in his hand, news was nearer than he imagined. Listlessly he turned over the sheets, glancing with but scant attention to the headlines, automatically running his eyes over the paragraphs. And when he came to one headed “Mystery of a Taxicab,” he absentmindedly began to read it also.
But he had not gone very far when his manner changed. Starting to his feet, he stared at the column with horror-stricken eyes, while his face grew pallid and his pipe dropped to the floor from his open mouth. With the newspaper still tightly grasped in his hand, he ran three steps at a time down the stairs of his flat, and calling a taxi, was driven to Scotland Yard.
===Chinese===
于是日子一天天捱了过去；对梅里曼来说，每一天都压满了焦灼与悬望。他的命运，连同他所爱的姑娘的命运，都悬而未决；而他处境中最难堪的一点，便是自己全然的无能为力。他无事可做——没有任何行动能让他暂忘自身、松一松绷紧的思绪。日复一日，音信杳然，他越来越坐立不安。一个星期下来，他忧烦懊恼得几乎失了方寸，索性连商行都不去了；若不是想到再强行纠缠玛德琳，也许会把他仅存的一点指望永远毁掉，他早克制不住冲回波尔多了。
科伯恩先生说过，辛迪加聚会在那个星期四召开，如今已过去四天；离两位朋友约定的向苏格兰场和盘托出的日子，也只剩三天。倘若这三天里他得不到半点消息可怎么办？希利亚德肯再宽限吗？他怕是不肯。他焦虑如焚，拿不定主意当天要不要渡海去法国，再求见科伯恩先生一面。
可是，正当他手捧晨报坐着的时候，消息比他想象的更近。他有气无力地翻着报纸，大标题只草草扫过，各段文字机械地顺目而读。读到一篇题为“出租汽车之谜”（Mystery of a Taxicab）的报道时，他心不在焉地也读了下去。
可没读几行，他的神色就变了。他霍地站起，睁着惊骇的眼睛死盯着那栏报道，脸色煞白，烟斗从张开的嘴里跌落在地。他一手仍死死攥着报纸，一步三级地奔下公寓的楼梯，叫了一辆出租汽车，直驰苏格兰场。

## XVII “Archer Plants Stuff” / 十七、“阿彻塞货”
===Original===
Willis’s chief at the Yard was not a little impressed by his subordinate’s story. He congratulated the inspector on his discovery, commended him for his restraint in withholding action against Archer until he had identified his accomplices, and approved his proposals for the further conduct of the case. Fortified by this somewhat unexpected approbation, Willis betook himself forthwith to the headquarters of the Customs Department and asked to see Hilliard.
The two men were already acquainted. As has been stated, the inspector had early called at Hilliard’s rooms and learned all that the other could tell him of the case. But for prudential reasons they had not met since.
Hilliard was tremendously excited by the inspector’s news, and eagerly arranged the interview with his chief which Willis sought. The great man was not engaged, and in a few minutes the others were shown into his presence.
===Chinese===
威利斯在苏格兰场的上司听了下属的讲述，印象颇深。他祝贺探长的这一发现，称赞他在查明阿彻的同伙之前按兵不动、不贸然出手的克制，并批准了他就本案后续办理提出的方案。得了这份多少出乎意料的嘉许，威利斯平添底气，当即前往海关总署总部，求见希利亚德。
两人原本就相识。如前所述，探长早先曾登门希利亚德的寓所，把对方所知的案情听了个遍。但出于审慎起见，此后两人再未见过面。
希利亚德听了探长带来的消息，激动不已，立即殷勤安排威利斯所求的那场与其上司的会面。那位大人物正好没有客，几分钟后，两人便被引到了他的面前。

===Original===
“We are here, sir,” Willis began, when the necessary introductions had been made, “to tell you jointly a very remarkable story. Mr. Hilliard would doubtless have told you his part long before this, had I not specially asked him not to. Now, sir, the time has come to put the facts before you. Perhaps as Mr. Hilliard’s story comes before mine in point of time, he should begin.”
Hilliard thereupon began. He told of Merriman’s story in the Rovers’ Club, his own idea of smuggling based on the absence of return cargoes, his proposition to Merriman, their trip to France and what they learned at the clearing. Then he described their visit to Hull, their observations at the Ferriby wharf, the experiment carried out with the help of Leatham, and, finally, what Merriman had told him of his second visit to Bordeaux.
Willis next took up the tale and described the murder of Coburn, his inquiries thereinto and the identification of the assassin, and his subsequent discoveries at Ferriby, ending up by stating the problem which still confronted him, and expressing the hope that the chief in dealing with the smuggling conspiracy would cooperate with him in connection with the murder.
The latter had listened with an expression of amazement, which towards the end of the inspector’s statement changed to one of the liveliest satisfaction. He gracefully congratulated both men on their achievements, and expressed his gratification at what had been discovered and his desire to cooperate to the full with the inspector in the settling up of the case.
The three men then turned to details. To Hilliard’s bitter disappointment it was ruled that, owing to his being known to at least three members of the gang, he could take no part in the final scenes, and he had to be content with the honour of, as it were, a seat on the council of war. For nearly an hour they deliberated, at the end of which time it had been decided that Stopford Hunt, one of the Customs Department’s most skillful investigators, should proceed to Hull and tackle the question of the distribution of the brandy. Willis was to go to Paris, interest the French authorities in the Bordeaux end of the affair, and then join Hunt in Hull.
===Chinese===
“先生，我们是两人一道来的，”必要的引见寒暄过后，威利斯开口道，“要向您共同禀报一桩极不寻常的事。倘若不是我有意请他暂缓，希利亚德先生想必早就把他那一段讲给您听了。如今，先生，把事实摆到您面前的时机到了。既然按时间先后，希利亚德先生的经历在我之前，那就请他先讲吧。”
希利亚德随即讲了起来。他讲了梅里曼在罗弗俱乐部讲的那件事，讲了他自己由回程无货这一点生出的走私猜想，讲了他向梅里曼提的那个提议，讲了他们的法国之行以及在林中空地上的所见所闻。接着，他又描述了他们的赫尔之行、在费里比码头的观察、借助利瑟姆做的那次试验，最后讲到梅里曼第二次去波尔多的种种经历。
接着威利斯接过话头，讲了科伯恩被杀的案情、他对此案的调查和凶手的认定，以及他随后在费里比的种种发现，末了道出仍横在他面前的难题，并表示希望这位长官在处理走私阴谋案时，就凶案一节与他通力合作。
长官一直听着，脸上先是讶异，待探长陈述将毕，已转为一种极其生动的满意神色。他雍容得体地祝贺两人建立的功绩，对已然查明的种种表示欣慰，并表示愿与探长竭诚合作，了结此案。
随后三人转入具体细节。裁定下来了，令希利亚德大失所望：由于团伙中至少三名成员认得他，收官的几场戏他一概不能登场；他也只好满足于一份荣名——好比在军事会议上占了一个席位。三人商议了将近一个钟头，最后议定：由海关总署最干练的调查员之一斯托普福德·亨特（Stopford Hunt）前往赫尔，专攻白兰地的分销问题；威利斯则赴巴黎，促成法国当局过问本案的波尔多一头，然后到赫尔与亨特会合。

===Original===
Stopford Hunt was an insignificant-looking man of about forty. All his characteristics might be described as being of medium quality. He was five feet nine in height, his brown hair was neither fair nor dark, his dress suggested neither poverty nor opulence, and his features were of the type known as ordinary. In a word, he was not one whose appearance would provoke a second glance or who would be credited with taking an important part in anything that might be in progress.
But for his job these very peculiarities were among his chief assets. When he hung about in an aimless, loafing way, as he very often did, he was overlooked by those whose actions he was so discreetly watching, and where mere loafing would look suspicious, he had the inestimable gift of being able to waste time in an *affairé* and preoccupied manner.
That night Willis crossed to Paris, and next day he told his story to the polite chief of the French Excise. M. Max was almost as interested as his English *confrère*, and readily promised to have the French end of the affair investigated. That same evening the inspector left for London, going on in the morning to Hull.
He found Hunt a shrewd and capable man of the world, as well as a pleasant and interesting companion.
===Chinese===
斯托普福德·亨特是个四十岁上下、相貌毫不起眼的人。他的一切特征都可以用“中等”二字概括：身高五英尺九，棕色头发不深不浅，衣着不见寒酸也不显阔气，五官属于所谓寻常一路。总而言之，他这副长相断不会惹人多看一眼，也没有谁会相信，正在进行的任何事情里会有他充当要角。
可对他这份差事来说，这些特质恰恰是他顶要紧的本钱。他常常漫无目的地闲荡游逛，而他正悄悄监视的那些人，对这样一个闲汉全不在意；在光是闲荡便难免惹眼的场合，他又有一种无价的天赋——能装出一副公事缠身、心不在焉的模样，把时间消磨过去。
当天夜里，威利斯渡海去了巴黎，第二天把事情原委讲给法国消费税局（Excise）那位客气周到的局长听。马克斯先生（M. Max）几乎同他的英国同行一样兴致勃勃，爽快地答应对本案法国一端着手调查。当天傍晚，探长动身回伦敦，次日早晨再赴赫尔。
他发现亨特为人精明干练、深谙世故，又是位叫人愉快、很有趣味的同伴。

===Original===
They had engaged a private sitting-room at their hotel, and after dinner they retired thither to discuss their plan of campaign.
“I wish,” said Willis, when they had talked for some moments, “that you would tell me something about how this liquor distribution business is worked. It’s outside my job, and I’m not clear on the details. If I understood I could perhaps help you better.”
Hunt nodded and drew slowly at his pipe.
“The principle of the thing,” he answered, “is simple enough, though in detail it becomes a bit complicated. The first thing we have to remember is that in this case we’re dealing, not with distillers, but with rectifiers. Though in loose popular phraseology both businesses are classed under the term ‘distilling,’ in reality there is a considerable difference between them. Distillers actually produce the spirit in their buildings, rectifiers do not. Rectifiers import the spirit produced by distillers, and refine or prepare it for various specified purposes. The check required by the Excise authorities is therefore different in each case. With rectifiers it is only necessary to measure the stuff that goes into and comes out of the works. Making due allowance for variation during treatment, these two figures will balance if all is right.”
Willis nodded, and Hunt resumed.
“Now, the essence of all fraud is that more stuff goes out of the works than is shown on the returns. That is, of course, another way of saying that stuff is sold upon which duty has not been paid. In the case of a rectifying house, where there is no illicit still, more also comes in than is shown. In the present instance you yourself have shown how the extra brandy enters. Our job is to find out how it leaves.”
===Chinese===
两人已在旅馆里包下一间私人起居室，晚饭后便退到那里，商讨起作战方略来。
谈了一阵之后，威利斯说：“我想请你给我讲讲，这酒类分销的营生是怎么个运转法。这不在我的职责范围，个中细节我不清楚。我要是弄懂了，或许能更好地帮上你。”
亨特点点头，慢慢吸着烟斗。
“这件事的原理，”他答道，“够简单的，只是细究起来有点复杂。首先得记住：我们这回打交道的，不是蒸馏酒商，而是精馏商。虽说在老百姓随口的说法里，两种营生都归在“蒸馏酿酒”一个名目底下，实际上两者大有分别。蒸馏酒商当真在自己的厂房里产出酒来，精馏商却不产。精馏商把蒸馏酒商产出的酒贩运进来，再加精炼，或调配作各种指定用途。因此，消费税局当局对两者所需的稽查各不相同。对精馏商，只消把进厂和出厂的酒计量清楚。对处理过程中的损耗作了应有的折算之后，这两个数目若能相抵，便一切无恙。”
威利斯点点头，亨特接着说下去。
“要知道，一切舞弊的实质，就是出厂的酒比申报册上所载的多。这当然也就是换个说法：有未纳税的酒卖掉了。至于精馏作坊，既然没有私设蒸馏器，进厂的酒也必定比申报册所载的多。眼前这桩案子，多出来的白兰地是怎么进来的，你已经亲自查明了。我们的差事，是查出它是怎么出去的。”

===Original===
“That part of it is clear enough anyway,” Willis said with a smile. “But brandy smuggling is not new. There must surely be recognised ways of evading the law?”
“Quite. There are. But to follow them you must understand how the output is measured. For every consignment of stuff that leaves the works a permit or certificate is issued and handed to the carrier who removes it. This is a kind of waybill, and of course a block is kept for the inspection of the surveying officer. It contains a note of the quantity of stuff, date and hour of starting, consignee’s name and other information, and it is the authority for the carrier to have the liquor in his possession. An Excise officer may stop and examine any dray or lorry carrying liquor, or railway wagon, and the driver or other official must produce his certificate so that his load may be checked by it. All such what I may call surprise examinations, together with the signature of the officer making them, are recorded on the back of the certificate. When the stuff is delivered, the certificate is handed over with it to the consignee. He signs it on receipt. It then becomes his authority for having the stuff on his premises, and he must keep it for the Excise officer’s inspection. Do you follow me so far?”
“Perfectly.”
“The fraud, then, consists in getting more liquor away from the works than is shown on the certificates, and I must confess it is not easy. The commonest method, I should think, is to fill the kegs or receptacles slightly fuller than the certificate shows. This is sometimes done simply by putting extra stuff in the ordinary kegs. It is argued that an Excise officer cannot by his eye tell a difference of five or six percent; that, for example, twenty-six gallons might be supplied on a twenty-five gallon certificate without anyone being much the wiser. Variants of this method are to use slightly larger kegs, or, more subtly, to use the normal sized kegs of which the wood at the ends has been thinned down, and which therefore when filled to the same level hold more, while showing the same measure with a dipping rod. But all these methods are risky. On the suspicion the contents of the kegs are measured and the fraud becomes revealed.”
===Chinese===
“反正这一头是够清楚的了，”威利斯含笑道。“不过白兰地走私不是什么新鲜事。逃避法网的门路，想必早有公认的吧？”
“不错。是有的。可要顺着门路查下去，你得先明白出产量是怎么计量的。每一批酒出厂，都要签发一张许可证、也就是凭单，交与承运人随货带走。这有点像一张货运路单，厂方当然还要留存一份存根，备监察官查验。凭单上载明酒的数量、启运的日期和钟点、收货人的姓名以及其他事项，它就是承运人持有这批酒的凭证。消费税局官员可以拦下任何载酒的货运马车或卡车、铁路货车加以查验，司机或其他经手人必须出示凭单，好让人按单核对所载的货。凡是这一类我姑且称之为突击检查的查验，连同执行官员的签名，都记录在凭单背面。货一送到，凭单便随货一并交与收货人。收货人在收货时签字。此后这张凭单就成了他在店内存放这批酒的正凭据，他必须留着备消费税局官员查验。到这里你都听明白了吗？”
“完全明白。”
“那么，舞弊的手法就在于：从厂里运出的酒，比凭单上所载的要多。我必须承认，这可不容易。照我想，最普通的法子，是把小桶或容器灌得比凭单所载略满一点。有时无非就是往寻常的小桶里多灌些酒。他们的道理是：消费税局官员凭肉眼分不出百分之五六的出入；比如说，凭一张二十五加仑的凭单装出二十六加仑来，谁也看不出多大破绽。这个法子的变体，是用略大一号的小桶，或者更巧妙些，用寻常尺寸、但两端桶板刨薄了的小桶——这样灌到同一高度时实际盛得更多，量酒尺插下去却显出同样的刻度。可这些法子都有风险。一旦起了疑，桶里的东西一过计量，舞弊立刻败露。”

===Original===
Willis, much interested, bent forward eagerly as the other, after a pause to relight his pipe, continued:
“Another common method is to send out liquor secretly, without a permit at all. This may be done at night, or the stuff may go through an underground pipe, or be hidden in innocent looking articles such as suitcases or petrol tins. The pipe is the best scheme from the operator’s point of view, and one may remain undiscovered for months, but the difficulty usually is to lay it in the first instance.
“A third method can be used only in the case of rectifiers and it illustrates one of the differences between rectifiers and distillers. Every permit for the removal of liquor from a distillery must be issued by the excise surveyor of the district, whereas rectifiers can issue their own certificates. Therefore in the case of rectifiers there is the possibility of the issuing of forged or fraudulent certificates. Of course this is not so easy as it sounds. The certificates are supplied in books of two hundred by the Excise authorities, and the blocks must be kept available for the supervisor’s scrutiny. Any certificates can be obtained from the receivers of the spirit and compared with the blocks. Forged permits are very risky things to work with, as all genuine ones bear the government watermark, which is not easy to reproduce. In fact, I may say about this whole question of liquor distribution generally, that fraud has been made so difficult that the only hope of those committing it is to avoid arousing suspicion. Once suspicion is aroused, discovery follows almost as a matter of course.”
“That’s hopeful for us,” Willis smiled.
“Yes,” the other answered, “though I fancy this case will be more difficult than most. There is another point to be taken into consideration which I have not mentioned, and that is, how the perpetrators of the frauds are going to get their money. In the last resort it can only come in from the public over the counters of the licensed premises which sell the smuggled spirits. But just as the smuggled liquor cannot be put through the books of the house selling it, so the money received for it cannot be entered either. This means that someone in authority in each licensed house must be involved. It also carries with it a suggestion, though only a suggestion, that the houses in question are tied houses. The director of a distillery company would have more hold on the manager of their own tied houses than over an outsider.”
===Chinese===
对方停下来重新点烟斗，接着往下讲；威利斯听得入神，急切地俯身向前：
“另一个常见的法子，是根本不带凭单，偷偷把酒运出去。可以夜里干，也可以让酒走一根地下管道，或者藏进手提箱、汽油桶之类貌不惊人的物件里。从干这行的人的角度看，管道是顶好的法子，一支管子可以一连几个月不被人发觉；难就难在头一回把它铺设起来。”
“第三种法子只有对精馏商才行得通，这也正好显出精馏商与蒸馏酒商的又一层分别。凡从蒸馏酒厂运酒，每一张许可证都必须由该地区的消费税局监察官签发；精馏商却可以自开凭单。因此对精馏商来说，就有了开出伪造或作假凭单的可能。当然，这事不像听起来那么容易。凭单由消费税局当局以二百张一册的簿册发放，各册存根都必须随时备着供监督官稽核。随便哪张凭单，都能从收酒人那里调来，同存根比对。伪造凭单是极冒险的营生，因为所有真凭单都带政府水印，那东西不易仿制。说实在的，关于整个酒类分销的问题，我可以这样讲：舞弊已被防范得如此之难，舞弊者唯一的指望，就是别惹人起疑。疑心一起，败露几乎顺理成章。”
“这对咱们是桩好事，”威利斯笑道。
“是啊，”对方答道，“不过我看这桩案子会比大多数案子更难办。还有一层我方才没提、却必须考虑在内的：那班作弊的人打算怎么把到手的钱变现。说到底，钱只能从公众那里来，从售卖走私酒的持牌酒馆的柜台上流进来。可是，正如走私的酒不能记进卖酒的酒馆的账簿，卖私酒得来的钱同样入不了账。这就意味着，每家持牌酒馆里都得有一个掌权的人入伙。这还附带一点暗示——仅仅是暗示——涉案的这几家是联号酒馆（tied house）。一家蒸馏酒公司的董事，对自家联号酒馆的经理，总比对一个外人更有约束力。”

===Original===
Again Willis nodded without replying, and Hunt went on:
“Now it happens that these Ackroyd & Holt people own some very large licensed houses in Hull, and it is to them, I imagine, that we should first direct our attention.”
“How do you propose to begin?”
“I think we must first find out how the Ferriby liquor is sent to these houses. By the way, you probably know that already. You watched the distillery during working hours, didn’t you?”
The inspector admitted it.
===Chinese===
威利斯又是只点头、不接话，亨特接着说：
“恰好，阿克罗伊德—霍尔特这班人在赫尔拥有几家很大的持牌酒馆。依我看，咱们的注意力就该先往他们身上使。”
“你打算从哪里入手？”
“我想，咱们得先查明费里比的酒是怎么送到这几家酒馆去的。对了，这个你多半已经知道了。你在上班钟点里监视过那家蒸馏酒厂，不是吗？”
探长承认了。

===Original===
“Did you see any lorries?”
“Any number; large blue machines. I noticed them going and coming in the Hull direction loaded up with barrels.”
Hunt seemed pleased.
“Good,” he commented. “That’s a beginning anyway. Our next step must be to make sure that all these lorries carry certificates. We had better begin tomorrow.”
Willis did not quite see how the business was to be done, but he forbore to ask questions, agreeing to fall in with his companion’s arrangements.
===Chinese===
“见过卡车吗？”
“见得多了；都是些大型蓝色机器。我留意到它们装着满车的大桶，朝着赫尔方向来来去去。”
亨特像是听了很高兴。
“好，”他说。“好歹算有个开头。下一步，咱们务必查清这些卡车是不是车车都带凭单。最好明天就动手。”
威利斯不很明白这件事该怎么办，但他忍住不问，同意一切照同伴的安排来。

===Original===
These arrangements involved the departure from their hotel by taxi at six o’clock the next morning. It was not fully light as they whirled out along the Ferriby road, but the sky was clear and all the indications pointed to a fine day.
They dismounted at the end of the lane leading to the works, and struck off across the fields, finally taking up their position behind the same thick hedge from which Willis had previously kept watch.
They spent the whole of that day, as well as of the next two, in their hiding-place, and at the end of that time they had a complete list of all lorries that entered or left the establishment during that period. No vehicles other than blue lorries appeared, and Hunt expressed himself as satisfied that if the smuggled brandy was not carried by them it must go either by rail or at night.
“We can go into those other contingencies later if necessary,” he said, “but on the face of it I am inclined to back the lorries. They supply the tied houses in Hull, which would seem the obvious places for the brandy to go, and, besides, railway transit is too well looked after to attract the gang. I think we’ll follow this lorry business through first on spec.”
“I suppose you’ll compare the certificate blocks with the list I made?” Willis asked.
“Of course. That will show if all carry certificates. But I don’t want to do that yet. Before alarming them I want to examine the contents of a few of the lorries. I think we might do that tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
照这些安排，第二天早晨六点，两人便乘出租车离开了旅馆。车子沿费里比大道飞驰而出时，天还没有大亮，但天空晴朗，种种迹象都预示着一个好天。
他们在通往厂区那条小巷的巷口下了车，斜插过田野，最后占据了原先那道厚树篱后面的位置——威利斯上回就是打那里守望的。
他们在藏身之处蹲了整整一天，外加随后的两天，到头来列出了一份完整的清单，记下那三天里进出厂区的所有卡车。出现的车辆除蓝色卡车外别无其他，亨特表示满意：走私的白兰地若不由这些卡车运载，那就必然走铁路，或者趁夜运出。
“那几种万一，必要的话咱们往后再查，”他说，“不过照表面看，我倾向于押卡车这一头。这些卡车给赫尔的联号酒馆送货，白兰地要脱手，最顺理成章的去处似乎就是那儿；再说，铁路运输看守得太严，那伙人不会动这个念头。我想，咱们不妨先顺着卡车这条线碰碰运气，一查到底。”
“我猜，你会拿凭单存根同我列的清单比对吧？”威利斯问。
“当然。一对就知道是不是车车有凭单。可我还不想这么快就动手。在惊动他们之前，我要先查验几辆卡车装载的东西。我想咱们明天就可以办这件事。”

===Original===
The next morning, therefore, the two detectives again engaged a taxi and ran out along the Ferriby road until they met a large blue lorry loaded with barrels and bearing on its side the legend “Ackroyd & Holt Ltd., Licensed Rectifiers.” When it had lumbered past on its way to the city, Hunt called to the driver and ordered him to follow it.
The chase led to the heart of the town, ending in a street which ran parallel to the Humber Dock. There the big machine turned in to an entry.
“The Anchor Bar,” Hunt said, in satisfied tones. “We’re in luck. It’s one of the largest licensed houses in Hull.”
He jumped out and disappeared after the lorry, Willis following. The vehicle had stopped in a yard at the back of the great public house, where were more barrels than the inspector ever remembered having seen together, while the smell of various liquors hung heavy in the air. Hunt, having shown his credentials, demanded the certificate for the consignment. This was immediately produced by the driver, scrutinised, and found in order. Hunt then proceeded to examine the consignment itself, and Willis was lost in admiration at the rapidity as well as the thoroughness of his inspection. He tested the nature of the various liquids, measured their receptacles, took drippings in each cask, and otherwise satisfied himself as to the quality and quantity. Finally he had a look over the lorry, then expressing himself satisfied, he endorsed the certificate, and with a few civil words to the men in charge, the two detectives took their leave.
“That’s all square anyway,” Hunt remarked, as they reentered their taxi. “I suppose we may go and do the same thing again.”
===Chinese===
于是第二天早晨，两位侦探又雇了一辆出租车，沿费里比大道一路驶出去，直到迎面遇上那辆大型蓝色卡车——满车装着大桶，车身一侧漆着“阿克罗伊德—霍尔特有限公司，持牌精馏商”的字样。等它朝城里轰隆隆驶了过去，亨特招呼司机，叫他跟上去。
一路追踪到了市中心，最后停在一条与亨伯码头平行的街上。那辆大车拐进了一处巷口。
“‘铁锚’酒吧（the Anchor Bar），”亨特用满意的声调说。“咱们走运了。这是赫尔最大的几家持牌酒馆之一。”
他跳下车，跟着卡车走了进去，威利斯随在后面。车子停在那家大酒馆后面的一个院子里。院里的大桶之多，探长记忆里从未见过聚在一起这么多，各色酒类的气味浓浓地悬在半空。亨特亮过证件，索要这批货的凭单。司机立刻取出，他细细审看，确认无误。接着亨特动手查验货物本身，那查验之快之细，令威利斯叹服不已。他检验各色酒液的品类，量度容器，从每只桶里取酒样，就这样把质地和数量一应核实。最后他把卡车前后看了一遍，表示满意，在凭单上作了签注，又同经手的人客气了几句，两位侦探便告辞了。
“反正这一趟是规规矩矩、查无破绽，”重新坐回出租车时，亨特说。“我看，咱们可以再去照样来一遍。”

===Original===
They did. Three times more on that day, and four times on the next day they followed Messrs. Ackroyd & Holt’s lorries, in every instance with the same result. All eight consignments were examined with the utmost care, and all were found to be accurately described on the accompanying certificate. The certificates themselves were obviously genuine, and everything about them, so far as Hunt could see, was in order.
“Doesn’t look as if we are going to get it that way,” he commented, as late that second evening they sat once more discussing matters in their private sitting-room.
“Don’t you think you have frightened them into honesty by our persistence?” Willis queried.
“No doubt,” the other returned. “But that couldn’t apply to the first few trips. They couldn’t possibly have foretold that we should examine those consignments yesterday, and today I expect they thought their visitation was over. But we have worked it as far as it will go. We shall have to change our methods.”
===Chinese===
他们果真照办了。当天又跟了三回，第二天跟了四回，回回结果相同。八批货一律经过最仔细的查验，批批都同随货凭单所载丝毫不差。凭单本身一望便知是真件，就亨特所能看出的而言，处处规整，无可挑剔。
“看这样子，这条道是查不出名堂了，”第二天夜里很晚的时候，两人又坐在私人起居室里议事，他这样评论道。
“你不觉得，是咱们这样穷追不舍，把他们吓得不敢作弊了？”威利斯问道。
“毫无疑问，”对方答道。“可这话套不到头几趟上。他们决计料不到咱们昨天会查验那几批货；到了今天，我料想他们还当这场查访已经过去了。不过这一手咱们已经使到头了。得换个法子。”

===Original===
The inspector looked his question and Hunt continued:
“I think tomorrow I had better go out to the works and have a look over these certificate blocks. But I wonder if it would be well for you to come? Archer has seen you in that hotel lounge, and at all events he has your description.”
“I shall not go,” Willis decided. “See you when you get back.”
===Chinese===
探长把疑问写在了脸上，亨特接着说：
“我想明天我最好到厂里去一趟，把这些凭单存根翻检一遍。只是你跟着去好不好呢？阿彻在那家旅馆的休息厅里见过你，无论如何，他手里有你的相貌描述。”
“那我就不去了，”威利斯拿定主意。“等你回来见。”

===Original===
Hunt, after showing his credentials, was received with civility at Messrs. Ackroyd & Holt’s. When he had completed the usual examination of their various apparatus he asked for certain books. He took them to a desk, and sitting down, began to study the certificate blocks.
His first care was to compare the list of outward lorries which he and Willis had made with the blocks for the same period. A short investigation convinced him that here also everything was in order. There was a certificate for every lorry which had passed out, and not only so, but the number of the lorry, the day and hour at which it left and the load were all correct so far as his observations had enabled him to check them. It was clear that here also he had drawn blank, and for the fiftieth time he wondered with a sort of rueful admiration how the fraud was being worked.
He was idly turning over the leaves of the blocks, gazing vacantly at the lines of writing while he pondered his problem when his attention was attracted to a slight difference of colour in the ink of an entry on one of the blocks. The consignment was a mixed one, containing different kinds of spirituous liquors. The lowest entry was for three twenty-five gallon kegs of French brandy. This entry was slightly paler than the remainder.
===Chinese===
亨特亮出证件，在阿克罗伊德—霍尔特商行里受到了礼遇。他把厂里各项设备照例查验完毕，便索取了几本簿册。他把它们拿到一张书桌前，坐下来，开始研读那些凭单存根。
他首先办的，是把他和威利斯列的那份出厂卡车清单，同同期各册存根一一比对。稍稍一查，他便确信这里也是一切无误：出厂的每一辆卡车，都有相应的凭单；不仅如此，卡车的编号、出厂的日期钟点和载货，凡他的观察所及、能够核对的，都准确无误。看来这里又是一无所获。他第五十次带着几分懊恼的钦佩暗自纳闷：这舞弊究竟是怎么运作的。
他正无心地翻着存根簿，对着一行行字迹出神，脑子里盘算着难题，忽然，其中一册上一条记录的墨色深浅引起了他的注意。那是一批混合货，装有各色酒类。最末一条是三只二十五加仑装的法国白兰地小桶。这一条的墨色比其余各条略淡。

===Original===
At first Hunt did not give the matter serious thought. The page had evidently been blotted while the ink was wet, and the lower items should therefore naturally be the fainter. But as he looked more closely he saw that this explanation would not quite meet the case. It was true that the lower two or three items above that of the brandy grew gradually paler in proportion to their position down the sheet, and to this rule Archer’s signature at the bottom was no exception. In these Hunt could trace the gradual fading of colour due to the use of blotting paper. But he now saw that this did not apply to the brandy entry. It was the palest of all﻿—paler even than Archer’s name, which was below it.
He sat staring at the sheet, whistling softly through his teeth and with his brow puckered into a frown, as he wondered whether the obvious suggestion that the brandy item had been added after the sheet had been completed, was a sound deduction. He could think of no other explanation, but he was loath to form a definite opinion on such slight evidence.
He turned back through the blocks to see if they contained other similar instances, and as he did so his interest grew. Quite a number of the pages referring to mixed consignment had for their last item kegs of French brandy. He scrutinised these entries with the utmost care. A few seemed normal enough, but others showed indications which strengthened his suspicions. In three more the ink was undoubtedly paler than the remainder of the sheet, in five it was darker, while in several others the handwriting appeared slightly different﻿—more upright, more sloping, more heavily or more lightly leaned on. When Hunt had examined all the instances he could find stretching over a period of three months, he was convinced that his deduction was correct. The brandy items had been written at a different time from the remainder, and this could only mean that they had been added after the certificate was complete.
His interest at last keenly aroused, he began to make an analysis of the blocks in question in the hope of finding some other peculiarity common to them which might indicate the direction in which the solution might lie.
===Chinese===
起初亨特没把这事放在心上。这一页分明是趁墨迹未干时用吸墨纸吸过，靠下的几条自然理应淡些。可他再细看，就发现这个解释不完全说得通。诚然，白兰地那一条上面的两三条，越靠纸的下方越淡，与位置成比例，页底阿彻的签名也逃不出这个规律。在那几行里，亨特看得出吸墨纸造成的墨色渐褪。但他此刻看出，这个道理套不到白兰地那条上：它是全页最淡的一条——比位置在它下面的阿彻的签名还要淡。
他坐在那里盯着那页纸，从齿缝里轻轻吹着口哨，眉头锁成疙瘩，心里捉摸：白兰地这一条是全页写完之后补上去的——这个最现成的猜测，算不算一个可靠的推论？他想不出别的解释，可又不愿凭这么一点蛛丝马迹就下定论。
他往回翻检各册存根，看有没有别的类似例子，越翻兴趣越浓。凡涉及混合货的各页，颇有几页的末一条是法国白兰地小桶。他对这些条目做了最仔细的审看。有几条似乎够正常，另一些却露出了加重疑心的迹象：又有三条的墨色无疑比同页其余各条淡，五条反而更深，还有几条的笔迹看着略有不同——有的更端正，有的更倾斜，有的落笔更重，有的更轻。等亨特把前后跨着三个月、能寻见的实例一一查完，他已确信自己的推论不错：白兰地各条与其余字迹书写的时间不同，而这只能意味着，它们是凭单写就之后补添上去的。
他的兴趣终于被强烈地激发起来，着手对这几册可疑的存根做一番分析，指望从中找出它们共有的别的什么特点，好指明破解的门路所在。

===Original===
And first as to the consignees. Ackroyd & Holt evidently supplied a very large number of licensed houses, but of these the names of only five appeared on the doubtful blocks. But these five were confined to houses in Hull, and each was a large and important concern.
“So far, so good,” thought Hunt, with satisfaction. “If they’re not planting their stuff in those five houses, I’m a Dutchman!”
He turned back to the blocks and once again went through them. This time he made an even more suggestive discovery. Only one lorry-man was concerned in the transport of the doubtful consignments. All the lorries in question had been in charge of a driver called Charles Fox.
Hunt remembered the man. He had driven three of the eight lorries Hunt himself had examined, and he had been most civil when stopped, giving the investigator all possible assistance in making his inspection. Nor had he at any time betrayed embarrassment. And now it seemed not improbable that this same man was one of those concerned in the fraud.
===Chinese===
先看收货人。阿克罗伊德—霍尔特显然给为数众多的持牌酒馆供货，但名字出现在可疑存根上的只有五家。这五家清一色都在赫尔，且家家都是买卖大、地位要紧的商号。
“到目前为止，一切顺利，”亨特心满意足地想。“要是他们没把私货塞进那五家酒馆，我就不姓亨特！”
