他回过头去，把各册存根又过了一遍。这一回，他有了一个更能说明问题的发现：经手运输那几批可疑货物的，只有一名卡车司机。涉案的各辆卡车，全归一个名叫查尔斯·福克斯（Charles Fox）的司机掌管。
亨特记得这个人。亨特亲自查验过的八辆卡车里，有三辆就是他开的；被拦下时他客客气气，在查验中给查案人帮了所有帮得上的忙，自始至终不曾露出半点窘迫。而现在看来，此人竟未必不是舞弊的同伙之一。

===Original===
Hunt applied himself once again to a study of the blocks, and then he made a third discovery, which, though he could not at first see its drift, struck him nevertheless as being of importance. He found that the faked block was always one of a pair. Within a few pages either in front of or behind it was another block containing particulars of a similar consignment, identical, in fact, except that the brandy item was missing.
Hunt was puzzled. That he was on the track of the fraud he could not but believe, but he could form no idea as to how it was worked. If he were right so far, the blocks had been made out in facsimile in the first instance, and later the brandy item had been added to one of each pair. Why? He could not guess.
He continued his examination, and soon another interesting fact became apparent. Though consignments left the works at all hours of the day, those referred to by the first one of each pair of blocks had all been sent out between the hours of one and two in the afternoon, and those referred to by the second between the hours of four and five. Further, the number of minutes past one and past four were always identical on each pair. That showed the brandy item was nearly always the later of the two, but occasionally the stuff had gone with the one o’clock trip.
Hunt sat in the small office, of which he had been given undisturbed possession, pondering over his problem and trying to marshal the facts that he had learned in such a way as to extract their inner meaning. As far as he could follow them they seemed to show that three times each day driver Charles Fox took a lorry of various liquors into Hull. The first trip was irregular, that is, he left at anything between seven-thirty and ten-thirty a.m., and his objective extended over the entire city. The remaining two trips were regular. Of these the first always left between one and two and the second the same number of minutes past four; both were invariably to the same one of the five large tied houses already mentioned; the load of each was always identical except that one﻿—generally the second﻿—had some kegs of brandy additional, and, lastly, the note of this extra brandy appeared always to have been added to the certificate after the latter had been made out.
===Chinese===
亨特又一次埋头研究起存根来，接着他有了第三个发现。这个发现的用意他一时还看不出来，却让他直觉事关紧要。他发现，动了手脚的那份存根总是成对出现：在它前头或后头几页之内，必另有一份存根，记着一批类似的货，事实上笔笔相同，唯独少了白兰地那一条。
亨特给难住了。他不能不相信自己已经踏在舞弊的踪迹上，可这舞弊如何运作，他却理不出半点头绪。倘若他到目前为止所料不差，那么这些存根起初是照样誊写、一式两份，事后才在每对之中的一份上补添了白兰地一条。为什么？他猜不出。
他继续查验，不久又显出一件耐人寻味的事实。虽说厂里从早到晚随时有货出厂，可每对存根里第一份所对应的那批，一律是下午一点到两点之间发出的；第二份所对应的，则在四点到五点之间。更有甚者，每对存根上，“一点过几分”与“四点过几分”的分数，回回相同。这说明，白兰地那条几乎总是落在两趟里的后一趟，但偶尔那批货也随一点钟的那趟走了。
亨特坐在那间供他独用、无人打扰的小办公室里，反复思量着他的难题，设法把已查明的种种事实排布成阵，好榨出它们内里的含义。就他理出来的线索看，事情似乎是：司机查尔斯·福克斯每天三趟开卡车载各色酒进赫尔。头一趟不定时，也就是说，动身时刻在上午七点半到十点半之间出入，送货地点遍及全城。余下两趟则定时定点：第一趟总在一点到两点之间出发，第二趟恰在四点过同样的分数动身；两趟回回都送往前述五家大联号酒馆中的同一家；两趟载货一律相同，唯独其中一趟——一般是第二趟——多出几小桶白兰地；最后，这多出的白兰地的记录，看着总是凭单开好之后补添上去的。

===Original===
Hunt could make nothing of it. In the evening he described his discoveries to Willis, and the two men discussed the affair exhaustively, though still without result.
That night Hunt could not sleep. He lay tossing from side to side and racking his brains to find a solution. He felt subconsciously that it was within his reach, and yet he could not grasp it.
It was not far from dawn when a sudden idea flashed into his mind, and he lay thrilled with excitement as he wondered if at last he held the clue to the mystery. He went over the details in his mind, and the more he thought over his theory the more likely it seemed to grow.
But how was he to test it? Daylight had come before he saw his way; but at last he was satisfied, and at breakfast he told Willis his idea and asked his help to carry out his plan.
===Chinese===
亨特还是理不出个所以然。晚上，他把自己的种种发现讲给威利斯听，两人把这桩事翻来覆去讨论了个透，仍然没有结果。
那天夜里亨特睡不着。他躺在床上翻来覆去，绞尽脑汁想找出一个答案。他隐隐觉得答案近在手边，偏偏又抓它不住。
天快破晓的时候，一个念头忽然闪进他的脑海。他躺着，兴奋得心头怦怦直跳，捉摸自己这回是否总算抓住了谜底。他在心里把细节过了一遍又一遍，越想越觉得这套推断站得住。
可是，怎么验证它呢？等他看出门道来，天已经亮了；不过他终于拿定了主意，早饭时把自己的想法告诉威利斯，请他帮忙实施这个计划。

===Original===
“You’re not a photographer, by any chance?” he asked.
“I’m not A1, but I dabble a bit at it.”
“Good. That will save some trouble.”
They called at a photographic outfitter’s, and there, after making a deposit, succeeded in hiring two large-size Kodaks for the day. With these and a set of climbing irons they drove out along the Ferriby road, arriving at the end of the lane to the works shortly after midday. There they dismissed their taxi.
As soon as they were alone their actions became somewhat bewildering to the uninitiated. Along one side of the road ran a seven-foot wall bounding the plantation of a large villa. Over this Willis, with the help of his friend, clambered. With some loose stones he built himself a footing at the back, so that he could just look over the top. Then having focused his camera for the middle of the road, he retired into obscurity behind his defences.
===Chinese===
“你该不会恰好会照相吧？”他问。
“算不上一流，不过也摆弄过一阵。”
“好。那就省事了。”
他们到一家照相器材行去了一趟，付下押金，成功租下两架大号柯达相机，租期一天。带着这两架相机和一副登杆脚扣，他们驱车沿费里比大道出城，刚过中午便到了通往厂区那条小巷的巷口。到了那里，他们打发出租车走了。
只剩他们两个之后，他们的举动在外行看来就有些莫名其妙了。大路一侧有一道七英尺高的围墙，围着一座大别墅的园林。威利斯在朋友帮衬下翻墙而过，用几块散碎石头在墙里侧给自己垒了个垫脚，刚好能从墙头探出眼睛。然后他把相机对准大路正中调好焦距，便退进屏障后面，隐没不见了。

===Original===
His friend settled to his satisfaction, Hunt buckled on the climbing irons, and crossing the road, proceeded to climb a telegraph pole which stood opposite the lane. He fixed his camera to the lower wires﻿—carefully avoiding possible short-circuitings﻿—and having focused it for the center of the road, pulled a pair of pliers from his pocket and endeavored to simulate the actions of a lineman at work. By the time these preparations were complete it was close on one o’clock.
Some half-hour later a large blue lorry came in sight bearing down along the lane. Presently Hunt was able to see that the driver was Fox. He made a prearranged sign to his accomplice behind the wall, and the latter, camera in hand, stood up and peeped over. As the big vehicle swung slowly round into the main road both men from their respective positions photographed it. Hunt, indeed, rapidly changing the film, took a second view as the machine retreated down the road towards Hull.
When it was out of sight, Hunt descended and with some difficulty climbed the wall to his colleague. There in the shade of the thick belt of trees both men lay down and smoked peacefully until nearly four o’clock. Then once more they took up their respective positions, watched until about half an hour later the lorry again passed out and photographed it precisely as before. That done, they walked to Hessle station, and took the first train to Hull.
By dint of baksheesh they persuaded the photographer to develop their films there and then, and that same evening they had six prints.
===Chinese===
朋友安顿妥帖，令他满意，亨特便绑上登杆脚扣，穿过大路，爬上小巷正对面的一根电线杆。他把相机固定在下层的线路上——小心翼翼，唯恐造成短路——对准大路正中调好焦，从口袋里摸出一把钳子，装出线路工正在作业的样子。这些准备完事的时候，已快到一点钟了。
约莫半个钟头后，一辆大型蓝色卡车顺着小巷压了过来，进了视野。不一会儿，亨特看清司机正是福克斯。他朝墙后那位同伙打了个约好的暗号，后者握着相机站起身，从墙头探望出去。等那辆大车缓缓拐上大路，两人各自从自己的位置上给它拍了照。亨特还飞快地换了胶卷，趁那车沿大路朝赫尔退走时，又补拍了一张。
卡车看不见了，亨特下了杆，又颇费了些力气爬上墙，到同事那边去。两人在那一圈浓密树荫里躺下，安安静静抽烟抽到将近四点。然后各自重新就位，守到约莫半个钟头后卡车再次驶出，照先前那样分毫不差地又拍了照。拍完，两人步行到赫斯尔车站，搭上了去赫尔的第一班火车。
靠着一点小费，他们说动照相师傅当场把胶卷冲了出来。当天晚上，两人手里就有了六张相片。

===Original===
As it happened they turned out exceedingly good photographs. Their definition was excellent, and each view included the whole of the lorry. The friends found, as Hunt had hoped and intended, that owing to the height from which the views had been taken, each several keg of the load showed out distinctly. They counted them. Each picture showed seventeen.
“You see?” cried Hunt triumphantly. “The same amount of stuff went out on each load! We shall have them now, Willis!”
Next day Hunt returned to Ferriby works ostensibly to continue his routine inspection. But in three minutes he had seen what he wanted. Taking the certificate book, he looked up the blocks of the two consignments they had photographed, and he could have laughed aloud in his exultation as he saw that what he had suspected was indeed the fact. The two certificates were identical except that to the second an item of four kegs of French brandy had been added! Hunt counted the barrels. The first certificate showed thirteen and the last seventeen.
===Chinese===
恰巧，这些照片冲出来出奇地好。清晰度极佳，每张都收进了整辆卡车。两人发现——正如亨特所指望、所安排的——由于拍摄的位置高，车上的每一只小桶都显豁分明。他们数了数，每张照片上都是十七只。
“看见没有？”亨特得意地喊道。“每趟车运出去的货一般多！这回他们逃不出咱们的手心了，威利斯！”
第二天，亨特回到费里比厂里，表面上说是继续例行查验。可只三分钟，他就看到了要看的东西。他取来凭单簿，翻出他们拍过照的那两批货的存根，一看之下，疑心果然是事实，他得意得几乎要放声大笑。两张凭单笔笔相同，唯独第二张多添了一条：法国白兰地四小桶！亨特数了数桶数。第一张凭单上是十三只，后一张是十七只。

===Original===
“Four kegs of brandy smuggled out under our noses yesterday,” he thought delightedly. “By Jove! but it’s a clever trick. Now to test the next point.”
He made an excuse for leaving the works, and returning to Hull, called at the licensed house to which the previous afternoon’s consignment had been dispatched. There he asked to see the certificates of the two trips. On seeing his credentials these were handed up without demur, and he withdrew with them to his hotel.
“Come,” he cried to Willis, who was reading in the lounge, “and see the final act in the drama.”
They retired to their private room, and there Hunt spread the two certificates on the table. Both men stared at them, and Hunt gave vent to a grunt of satisfaction.
“I was right,” he cried delightedly. “Look here! Why I can see it with the naked eye!”
===Chinese===
“昨天当着咱们的面，走私运出去了四小桶白兰地，”他喜滋滋地想。“天哪！这把戏玩得真妙。接下来验证下一环。”
他寻了个由头离厂，回到赫尔，登门拜访了前一天下午那批货送达的那家持牌酒馆。在那里，他要求查看那两趟送货的凭单。对方一见他的证件，二话不说便把凭单呈了上来，他揣着凭单退回了旅馆。
“来，”他朝正在休息厅里看书的威利斯喊道，“来看这出戏的最后一幕。”
两人退进他们的私人房间，亨特把两张凭单摊在桌上。两人盯着看去，亨特满意地哼了一声。
“我说对了，”他喜滋滋地喊道。“瞧这儿！这不，肉眼都看得出来！”

===Original===
The two certificates were an accurate copy of their blocks. They were dated correctly, both bore Fox’s name as driver, and both showed consignments of liquor, identical except for the additional four kegs of brandy on the second. There was, furthermore, no sign that this had been added after the remainder. The slight lightening in the colour towards the bottom of the sheet, due to the use of blotting paper, was so progressive as almost to prove the whole had been written at the same time.
The first certificate was timed 1:15 p.m., the second 4:15 p.m., and it was to the 4 of this second hour that Hunt’s eager finger pointed. As Willis examined it he saw that the lower strokes were fainter than the remainder. Further, the beginning of the horizontal stroke did not quite join the first vertical stroke.
“You see?” Hunt cried excitedly. “That figure is a forgery. It was originally a 1, and the two lower strokes have been added to make it a 4. The case is finished!”
Willis was less enthusiastic.
===Chinese===
两张凭单与各自的存根一模一样。日期填写无误，都署着司机福克斯的名字，所载货物也相同，只是第二张多了四小桶白兰地。而且，看不出这四桶是在其余各项之后补添的迹象。纸页下端因吸墨纸造成的墨色轻微转淡，过渡得那样均匀渐进，几乎足以证明整张凭单是一气写成的。
第一张凭单上填的时刻是下午 1 点 15 分，第二张是下午 4 点 15 分，亨特急切的手指正点在第二个钟点的那个 4 字上。威利斯凑近细看，只见那数字下端的笔画比其余的淡；再者，横笔的起笔处同第一笔竖笔衔接得不甚严密。
“看见没有？”亨特激动地喊道。“那个数字是伪造的。它原本是个 1，后来添了两笔下端的笔画，才改成 4。这案子结了！”
威利斯却没有这般热心。

===Original===
“I’m not so sure of that,” he returned cautiously. “I don’t see light all the way through. Just go over it again, will you?”
“Why to me it’s as clear as daylight,” the other asserted impatiently. “See here. Archer decides, let us suppose, that he will send out four kegs, or one hundred gallons, of the smuggled brandy to the Anchor Bar. What does he do? He fills out certificates for two consignments each of which contains an identical assortment of various liquors. The brandy he shows on one certificate only. The blocks are true copies of the certificates except that the brandy is not entered on either. The two blocks he times for a quarter past one and past four respectively, but both certificates he times for a quarter past one. He hands the two certificates to Fox. Then he sends out on the one o’clock lorry the amount of brandy shown on one of the certificates.”
Hunt paused and looked interrogatively at his friend, then, the latter not replying, he resumed:
“You follow now the position of affairs? In the office is Archer with his blocks, correctly filled out as to time but neither showing the brandy. On the one o’clock lorry is Fox, with one hundred gallons of brandy among his load. In his pocket are the two certificates, both timed for one o’clock, one showing the brandy and the other not.”
===Chinese===
“这一点我拿不准，”他谨慎地答道。“我没法从头到尾看得透亮。你把整件事再从头说一遍，好不好？”
“在我看来，这再清楚不过，跟白昼一样，”对方不耐烦地坚持道。“你听着。咱们假定，阿彻决意把四小桶、也就是一百加仑走私白兰地送到‘铁锚’酒吧去。他怎么办？他开出两张凭单，各载一批货，两批货色搭配完全相同。白兰地只写在其中一张凭单上。两份存根如实照录凭单，唯独白兰地哪份都不记。两份存根上，他把时间分别填作一点一刻和四点一刻，两张凭单却都填作一点一刻。他把两张凭单交给福克斯。然后，他让一点钟那趟卡车，把一张凭单上所载的那批白兰地运出去。”
亨特顿了顿，用探询的眼光望着朋友；见对方不作声，他便继续说道：
“现在的局面你清楚了吧？办公室里坐着阿彻，守着他的存根，时间填得规规矩矩，可哪份都不见白兰地。一点钟的卡车上是福克斯，载货里夹着一百加仑白兰地。他口袋里揣着两张凭单，都写着一点钟出发，一张载明白兰地，一张不载。”

===Original===
The inspector nodded as Hunt again looked at him.
“Now suppose,” the latter went on, “that the one o’clock lorry gets through to its destination unchallenged, and the stuff is unloaded. The manager arranges that the four kegs of brandy will disappear. He takes over the certificate which does not show brandy, signs it, and the transaction is complete. Everything is in order, and he has got four kegs smuggled in.”
“Good,” Willis interjected.
“On the other hand, suppose the one o’clock trip is held up by an exciseman. This time Fox produces the other certificate, the one which shows the brandy. Once again everything is in order, and the Excise officer satisfied. It is true that on this occasion Fox has been unable to smuggle out his brandy, and on that which he carries duty must be paid, but this rare contingency will not matter to him as long as his method of fraud remains concealed.”
===Chinese===
亨特又朝他看过来时，探长点了点头。
“再设想，”后者接着说，“一点钟那趟卡车一路无人盘查，到了地头，货卸了下来。经理安排那四小桶白兰地凭空消失。他收下那张不载白兰地的凭单，签了字，这笔交易就算完结。样样手续齐全，四小桶私货就这样走私进了门。”
“好，”威利斯插了一句。
“反过来，假定一点钟那趟半路被消费税局稽查员拦下。这一回，福克斯掏出另一张凭单，就是载明白兰地的那张。这一来又是样样齐全，消费税局官员无话可说。诚然，这一趟福克斯的白兰地走私不成了，车上这批货也得照章纳税；可只要舞弊的路数不露馅，这点万一的损失，他是不会放在心上的。”

===Original===
“Seems very sound so far.”
“I think so. Let us now consider the four o’clock trip. Fox arrives back at the works with one of the two certificates still in his pocket, and the make up of his four o’clock load depends on which it is. He attempts no more smuggling that day. If his remaining certificate shows brandy he carries brandy, if not, he leaves it behind. In either case his certificate is in order if an Excise officer holds him up. That is, when he has attended to one little point. He has to add two strokes to the 1 of the hour to make it into a 4. The ease of doing this explains why these two hours were chosen. Is that all clear?”
“Clear, indeed, except for the one point of how the brandy item is added to the correct block.”
“Obviously Archer does that as soon as he learns how the first trip has got on. If the brandy was smuggled out on the first trip, it means that Fox is holding the brandy-bearing certificate for the second, and Archer enters brandy on his second block. If, on the contrary, Fox has had his first load examined, Archer will make his entry on the first block.”
===Chinese===
“到这里为止，听上去很严密。”
“我也这么想。现在来看四点钟那趟。福克斯回到厂里时，两张凭单还有一张揣在口袋里，而他四点钟那趟装什么货，就看揣着的是哪一张。当天他不再走私。倘若留下的凭单载有白兰地，他就装白兰地；若没有，就把白兰地留下。无论哪种情形，消费税局官员再拦他，凭单都是齐全的——只消他办好一件小事：把钟点的那个 1 添上两笔，改成 4。改动起来轻而易举，这正解释了为何偏偏挑中这两个钟头。都清楚了吗？”
“清楚是清楚，只差一点：白兰地那条，是怎么补到该补的那份存根上的。”
“很明白，阿彻一打听到头一趟的结局，就立刻补记。倘若白兰地随头一趟走私出去了，那就意味着福克斯把载明白兰地的凭单留给了第二趟，阿彻便在第二份存根上记下白兰地。反之，倘若福克斯的第一车货受了查验，阿彻就把那条记在第一份存根上。”

===Original===
“The scheme,” Willis declared, “really means this. If Archer wants to smuggle out one hundred gallons of brandy, he has to send out another hundred legitimately on the same day? If he can manage to send out two hundred altogether then one hundred will be duty clear, but in any case he must pay on one hundred?”
“That’s right. It works out like that.”
“It’s a great scheme. The only weak point that I can see is that an Excise officer who has held up one of the trips might visit the works and look at the certificate block before Archer gets it altered.”
===Chinese===
“这套方案，”威利斯断言道，“实际的意味是：阿彻每想走私出一百加仑白兰地，当天就得另外正经运出一百加仑，对不对？倘若他总共能运出两百加仑，其中一百加仑便净落了免税；可无论如何，总有一百加仑的税他躲不掉，对不对？”
“正是如此。账就是这么算的。”
“真是个高明的方案。依我看，唯一的破绽是：拦过其中一趟的消费税局官员，也许会在阿彻来得及改动之前，跑到厂里查看凭单存根。”

===Original===
Hunt nodded.
“I thought of that,” he said, “and it can be met quite easily. I bet the manager telephones Archer on receipt of the stuff. I am going into that now. I shall have a note kept at the Central of conversations to Ferriby. If Archer doesn’t get a message by a certain time, I bet he assumes the plan has miscarried for that day and fills in the brandy on the first block.”
During the next two days Hunt was able to establish the truth of his surmise. At the same time Willis decided that his cooperation in the work at Hull was no longer needed. For Hunt there was still plenty to be done. He had to get direct evidence against each severally of the managers of the five tied houses in question, as well as to ascertain how and to whom they were passing on the “stuff,” for that they were receiving more brandy than could be sold over their own counters was unquestionable. But he agreed with Willis that these five men were more than likely in ignorance of the main conspiracy, each having only a private understanding with Archer. But whether or not this was so, Willis did not believe he could get any evidence that they were implicated in the murder of Coburn.
The French end of the affair, he thought, the supply of the brandy in the first instance, was more promising from this point of view, and the next morning he took an early train to London as a preliminary to starting work in France.
===Chinese===
亨特点了点头。
“这一层我想到了，”他说，“而且不难应付。我敢打赌，经理一收到货就会打电话给阿彻。我眼下就去查证这个。我要让电话总局留一份打到费里比的通话记录。倘若到了一定时刻阿彻还没得到音信，我敢说他就会认定当天的计划落了空，把白兰地填到第一份存根上。”
随后两天，亨特证实了自己的猜测。与此同时，威利斯认定，自己在赫尔的这摊工作已无须他再出力。亨特那边还有的是事要做：他必须逐一拿到涉案五家联号酒馆经理的直接罪证，还要查明他们把“货”是怎么转手、又转给了什么人——他们收进的白兰地多到自家的柜台卖不完，这一点毋庸置疑。不过他同意威利斯的看法：这五个人十有八九对主谋的阴谋一无所知，各自只同阿彻有私下的默契。但无论是否如此，威利斯都不相信，能找到他们与科伯恩凶案有牵连的任何证据。
他想，就这一层而论，本案的法国一头——白兰地最初的货源——更有可为。于是第二天早晨，他乘早班车去了伦敦，作为赴法开展工作的前站。

## XV Inspector Willis Listens In / 十五、威利斯探长窃听
===Original===
Inspector Willis was a good deal exercised by the question of whether or not he should have Archer shadowed. If the managing director conceived the slightest suspicion of his danger he would undoubtedly disappear, and a man of his ability would not be likely to leave many traces. On the other hand Willis wondered whether even Scotland Yard men could shadow him sufficiently continuously to be a real safeguard, without giving themselves away. And if that happened he might indeed arrest Archer, but it would be goodbye to any chance of getting his confederates.
After anxious thought he decided to take the lesser risk. He would not bring assistants into the matter, but would trust to his own skill to carry on the investigation unnoticed by the distiller.
Though the discovery of Archer’s identity seemed greatly to strengthen the probability that the secret telephone led to him, Willis could not state this positively, and he felt it was the next point to be ascertained. The same argument that he had used before seemed to apply﻿—that owing to the difficulty of wiring, the point of connection must be close to the depot. Archer’s office was not more than three hundred yards away, while his house, The Elms, was over a mile. The chances were therefore in favour of the former.
It followed that he must begin by searching Archer’s office for the other receiver, and he turned his attention to the problem of how this could best be done.
===Chinese===
威利斯探长为一件事大伤脑筋：要不要派人盯阿彻的梢。这位总经理只要对自己的危险起了些许疑心，无疑就会远走高飞，而以他的本事，走时多半不会留下多少痕迹。可另一方面，威利斯又怀疑，就算是苏格兰场的人，盯梢也未必盯得够连续、真能起到保障作用，而不暴露自己。倘若暴露了，他固然可以将阿彻逮捕归案，但再想捞到他的同伙，就休想了。
焦虑地思量一番之后，他决定冒较小的那个险。他不把助手们牵扯进来，而要全凭自己的本事继续侦查，不叫那位蒸馏酒商察觉。
虽然查明阿彻的身份，似乎大大加强了那条秘密电话线通到他那里的可能性，威利斯却无法把这一点说死，他觉得这是下一个必须查实的要点。他先前用过的那套论证看来依然适用——由于布线不易，接线点必定紧挨着货栈。阿彻的办公室离货栈不过三百码，而他的宅邸榆树宅则在一英里开外。因此多半是前者。
由此得出的结论是：他必须从搜查阿彻的办公室、寻找那另一只听筒入手。于是他把心思转到怎样才能把这件事办得最为妥帖的问题上来。

===Original===
And first, as to the lie of the offices. He called at the Electric Generating Station, and having introduced himself confidentially to the manager in his official capacity, asked to see the man whose business it was to inspect the lights of the distillery. From him he had no difficulty in obtaining a rough plan of the place.
It appeared that the offices were on the first floor, fronting along the line, Archer’s private office occupying the end of the suite and the corner of the building nearest to the syndicate’s wharf, and therefore to Ferriby. The supervisor believed that it had two windows looking to the front and side respectively, but was not sure.
That afternoon Inspector Willis returned to the distillery, and secreting himself in the same hiding place as before, watched until the staff had left the building. Then strolling casually along the lane, he observed that the two telephone wires which approached across the fields led to the third window from the Ferriby end of the first floor row.
===Chinese===
首先说说办公室的方位格局。他登门拜访了发电站，以自己的官家身份向经理作了不宜声张的自我介绍，然后求见那个专管查验蒸馏酒厂照明的人。他没有费什么周折，就从那人手里拿到了那地方的一张简图。
从简图上看，办公室设在二层，沿铁路一线排开，阿彻的私人办公室占着这排屋子的一头、也就是大楼最靠近辛迪加码头、因而也最靠近费里比的一角。那位管理员相信它有两扇窗，分别朝前、朝侧，但拿不准。
当天下午，威利斯探长回到蒸馏酒厂，像先前一样藏进原来的藏身之处，一直望着全体员工离开那座楼。随后他信步沿小路溜达过去，看出那两路横穿田野而来的电话线，通到了二层那排屋子从费里比一端数的第三扇窗。

===Original===
“That’ll be the main office,” he said to himself, “but there will probably be an extension to Archer’s own room. Now I wonder﻿—”
He looked about him. The hedge bounding the river side of the lane ran up to the corner of the building. After another hasty glance round Willis squeezed through and from immediately below scrutinised the side window of the managing director’s room. And then he saw something which made him chuckle with pleasure.
Within a few inches of the architrave of the window there was a down-spout, and from the top of the window to the spout he saw stretching what looked like a double cord. It was painted the same colour as the walls, and had he not been looking out specially he would not have seen it. A moment’s glance at the foot of the spout showed him his surmise was correct. Pushed in behind it and normally concealed by it were two insulated wires, which ran down the wall from the window and disappeared into the ground with the spout.
“Got it first shot,” thought the inspector delightedly, as he moved away so as not to attract the attention of any chance onlooker.
===Chinese===
“那该是总办公室，”他自言自语道，“不过多半有一路分机通到阿彻自己的房间。我倒想知道——”
他朝四下里望了望。小路临河一侧的树篱一直延伸到大楼的墙角。威利斯又匆匆扫视一圈，随即从树篱间挤了过去，站在正下方端详起总经理房间那扇侧窗来。这一看，他看见了一样叫他乐得暗笑出声的东西。
离窗框线脚只有几英寸的地方有一根落水管，从窗顶到管子之间，他看见绷着一样像是双股绳的东西。它漆得同墙壁一个颜色，若不是特地留心寻找，根本看不出来。朝管子脚下扫一眼，他便知道自己猜对了。塞在管子背后、平时被它遮得严严实实的，是两根绝缘线，从窗边沿墙而下，同落水管一道没入地下。
“一击即中，”探长喜不自胜地想道，随即移开身子，以免惹来偶然路过的旁观者注意。

===Original===
Another idea suddenly occurred to him and, after estimating the height and position of the window, he turned and ran his eye once more over his surroundings. About fifty yards from the distillery, and behind the hedge fronting the lane, stood the cottage which Hilliard and Merriman had noticed. It was in a bad state of repair, having evidently been unoccupied for a long time. In the gable directly opposite the managing director’s office was a broken window. Willis moved round behind the house, and once again producing his bent wire, in a few moments had the back door open. Slipping inside, he passed through the damp-smelling rooms and up the decaying staircase until he reached the broken window. From it, as he had hoped, he found he had a good view into the office.
He glanced at his watch. It was ten minutes past seven.
“I’ll do it tonight,” he murmured, and quietly leaving the house, he hurried to Ferriby Station and so to Hull.
===Chinese===
他忽然又想起一个主意。估量过那扇窗的高度和位置之后，他转身把周围环境又打量了一遍。离蒸馏酒厂约五十码、在小路一侧树篱的后面，立着希利亚德和梅里曼当时留意到的那座村舍。它失修得厉害，显然久已无人居住。正对着总经理办公室的山墙上有一扇破窗。威利斯绕到屋后，又掏出那根弯铁丝，不多时便弄开了后门。他侧身进屋，穿过几间散发着潮气的屋子，登上朽坏的楼梯，来到那扇破窗跟前。正如他所期望的，从那里可以把办公室里面的情形看个清楚。
他看了看表。七点十分。
“今晚就干，”他低声道，随即悄悄离开那座房子，匆匆赶往费里比车站，由那里去赫尔。

===Original===
Some five hours later he left the city again, this time by motor. He stopped at the end of the lane which ran past the distillery, dismissed the vehicle, and passed down the lane. He was carrying a light, folding ladder, a spade, a field telephone, a coil of insulated wire, and some small tools.
The night was very dark. The crescent moon would not rise for another couple of hours, and a thick pall of cloud cut off all light from the stars. A faint wind stirred the branches of the few trees in the neighbourhood and sighed across the wide spaces of open country. The inspector walked slowly, being barely able to see against the sky the tops of the hedges which bounded the lane. Except for himself no living creature seemed to be abroad.
Arrived at his destination, Willis felt his way to the gap in the hedge which he had used before, passed through, and with infinite care raised his ladder to the window of Archer’s office. He could not see the window, but he checked the position of the ladder by the measurements from the hedge. Then he slowly ascended.
===Chinese===
约莫五个钟头之后，他又出了城，这一回是坐汽车。他在那条经过蒸馏酒厂的小路尽头停了车，打发走车子，顺着小路走下去。他随身带着一架轻便折梯、一把铁锹、一部战地电话、一圈绝缘线，还有几件小工具。
夜黑得很。一弯新月还要过两个钟头才会升起，厚厚一重乌云把星光遮了个严实。微风轻轻摇动附近稀疏几棵树的枝条，叹息似的掠过开阔的旷野。探长慢慢走着，勉强能凭天色辨出夹住小路的两侧树篱的顶。除了他自己，似乎再没有活物出门。
到了目的地，威利斯摸索着找到他先前用过的那道树篱豁口，钻了过去，又万般小心地把折梯抬起来搭向阿彻办公室的窗口。他看不见窗子，便凭从树篱量出的尺寸校准梯子的位置。然后他慢慢往上爬。

===Original===
He found he had gauged his situation correctly, and he was soon on the sill of the window, trying with his knife to push back the hasp. This he presently accomplished, and then, after an effort so great that he thought he would be beaten, he succeeded in raising the sash. A minute later he was in the room.
His first care was to pull down the thick blinds of blue holland with which the windows were fitted. Then tiptoeing to the door, he noiselessly shot the bolt in the lock.
Having thus provided against surprise, he began his investigation. There in the top corner of the side window were the wires. They followed the miter of the window architrave﻿—white-enameled to match﻿—and then, passing down for a few inches at the outside of the moldings, ran along the picture rail round the room, concealed in the groove behind it. Following in the same way the miter of the architrave, they disappeared though a door in the back wall of the office.
Willis softly opened the door, which was not locked, and peered into a small store, evidently used for filing. The wires were carried down the back of the architrave molding and along the top of the wainscoting, until finally they disappeared into the side of one of a series of cupboards which lined the wall opposite the door. The cupboard was locked, but with the help of the bent wire it soon stood open and Willis, flashing in a beam from his electric torch, saw with satisfaction that he had attained at least one of his objects. A telephone receiver similar to that at the syndicate’s depot was within.
===Chinese===
他发现自己估得没错，不多时已立上窗台，用刀去拨窗扣。这活儿很快得手，接着他又费了好大的劲、几乎以为自己要败下阵来，才把窗扇推了上去。一分钟后，他已在屋里。
他头一件操心的，是把窗上装配的蓝色荷兰麻布厚窗帘统统拉严。随后踮着脚走到门边，无声无息地把锁里的门闩推上了。
防了这一手不虞之变，他开始查勘。侧窗上角正挂着那两根电线。它们顺着窗框线脚的斜接角走——漆了相配的白瓷漆——然后在饰线条外侧向下走几英寸，再沿着绕屋一周的挂画线板延伸，藏在线板背后的凹槽里。它们又顺着线脚的斜接角以同样的方式转过去，从办公室后墙的一道门里消失了。
威利斯轻轻推开那道没上锁的门，朝一间小屋里窥看，那显然是作档案室用的。电线沿线脚饰条的背面下来，再顺着护壁板的顶沿走，末了没入门对面墙上一排柜子中一个柜子的侧壁。柜子上了锁，但借弯铁丝之助，不多时它便敞开了。威利斯用手电往里一照，满意地看到自己至少达成了一项目标：里面有一只电话听筒，同辛迪加货栈里的那只相仿。

===Original===
He examined the remaining contents of the room, but found nothing of interest until he came to the door. This was solidly made and edged with rubber, and he felt sure that it would be almost completely soundproof. It was, moreover, furnished with a well-oiled lock.
“Pretty complete arrangement,” Willis thought as he turned back to the outer office. Here he conducted another of his meticulous examinations, but unfortunately with a negative result.
Having silently unlocked the door and pulled up the blinds, he climbed out on the window sill and closed the window. He was unable to refasten the hasp, and had therefore to leave this evidence of his visit, though he hoped and believed it would not be noticed.
===Chinese===
他把屋里其余的东西查看了一遍，直到那道门跟前才见到值得留意的东西。那门做得厚实，四边镶着橡胶，他确信它几乎能完全隔音。何况门上还装着一把上足了油的锁。
“布置得真够周全，”威利斯一边转回外间办公室一边想。他在这里又做了一回他那丝丝入扣的检查，可惜一无所获。
他无声地开了锁，拉起窗帘，爬出窗外立上窗台，把窗子放了下来。窗扣没法重新扣上，他只得把这份来过的痕迹留在那里，不过他盼望并且相信它不会被人留意。

===Original===
Lifting down the ladder, he carried it to the cottage and hid it therein. Part of his task was done, and he must wait for daylight to complete the remainder.
When some three hours later the coming dawn had made objects visible, he again emerged armed with his tools and coil of insulated wire. Digging a hole at the bottom of the down-pipe, he connected his wires just below the ground level to those of the telephone. Then inserting his spade along the face of the wall from the pipe to the hedge, he pushed back the adjoining soil, placed the wires in the narrow trench thus made, and trod the earth back into place. When the hole at the down-spout had been filled, practically no trace remained of the disturbance.
The ground along the inside of the hedge being thickly grown over with weeds and grass, he did not think it necessary to dig a trench for the wire, simply bedding it beneath the foliage. But he made a spade cut across the sward from the hedge to the cottage door, sank in the wire and trod out the cut. Once he had passed the tiny cable beneath the front door he no longer troubled to hide it but laid it across the floors and up the stairs to the broken window. There he attached the field receiver, affixing it to his ear so as to be ready for eventualities.
It was by this time half past six and broad daylight, but Willis had seen no sign of life and he believed his actions had been unobserved. He ate a few sandwiches, then lighting his pipe, lay down on the floor and smoked contentedly.
===Chinese===
他把梯子取下来，搬到村舍里藏好。任务完成了一部分，余下的要等天亮再办。
约莫三个钟头后，将至的黎明已能照见物件，他再次现身，携带着工具和那圈绝缘线。他在落水管脚下挖了个洞，把自带的电线在地面之下同那部电话的线接通。然后把铁锹贴着墙面自落水管一路插到树篱，把两侧的土推开，将电线纳入这样开出的一道窄沟，再把土踩回原处。落水管脚下的洞一填平，动土的痕迹几乎荡然无存。
树篱内侧一带杂草丛生，他觉得不必为电线开沟，只把它压在草叶底下便是。但他从树篱到村舍前门的草皮上用锹开了一道口子，把线埋进去，又把口子踩平。这条细缆一从前门底下穿过，他便不再费事掩藏，径直让它越过一间间地板、爬上楼梯，直牵到那扇破窗跟前。他在那里接上战地听筒，扣在耳朵上，以备不时之需。
这时已是六点半，天光大亮，但威利斯没有见到一点人烟，他相信自己的举动不曾落在任何人眼里。他吃了几块三明治，然后点上烟斗，往地板上一躺，悠悠然抽起烟来。

===Original===
His case at last was beginning to prosper. The finding of Coburn’s murderer was of course an event of outstanding importance, and now the discovery of the telephone was not only valuable for its own sake, but was likely to bring in a rich harvest of information from the messages he hoped to intercept. Indeed he believed he could hardly fail to obtain from this source a definite indication of the nature and scope of the conspiracy.
About eight o’clock he could see from his window a number of workmen arrive at the distillery, followed an hour later by a clerical staff. After them came Archer, passing from his car to the building with his purposeful stride. Almost immediately he appeared in his office, sat down at his desk, and began to work.
Until nearly midday Willis watched him going through papers, dictating letters, and receiving subordinates. Then about two minutes to the hour he saw him look at his watch, rise, and approach the door from the other office, which was in Willis’s line of vision behind the desk. He stooped over the lock as if turning the key, and then the watcher’s excitement rose as the other disappeared out of sight in the direction of the filing room.
===Chinese===
他的案子总算开始兴旺了。找到杀害科伯恩的凶手自然是一件头等大事，而如今这电话的发现，不仅其本身就极有价值，还很可能从他希望截获的通话里带来大宗情报。确实，他相信自己几乎不可能不从这条渠道，得到关于这场阴谋的性质与规模的明确线索。
八点前后，他从窗口望见一批工人到了蒸馏酒厂，一个钟头后又来了一班文职人员。随后阿彻到了，迈着那副目标坚定的大步，从汽车走进楼里。几乎前后脚，他便出现在自己办公室里，在写字台前坐下，办起公来。
直到近午，威利斯看着他翻阅文件、口授信函、接见下属。随后差两分钟到点的时候，他见他看看表，站起身，朝那扇从另一间办公室通来的门走去——那门在写字台后方，正落在威利斯的视线里。他朝门锁俯下身去，像是在拧钥匙；接着，随着那身影朝档案室方向消失不见，守望者的兴奋陡然升了起来。

===Original===
Willis was not disappointed. Almost immediately he heard the faint call of the tiny buzzer, and then a voice﻿—Archer’s voice, he believed, from what he had heard in the hotel lounge called softly, “Are you there?”
There was an immediate answer. Willis had never heard Benson speak, but he presumed that the reply must be from him.
“Anything to report?” Archer queried.
“No. Everything going on as usual.”
“No strangers poking round and asking questions?”
===Chinese===
威利斯没有失望。几乎紧接着，他就听见那只小蜂鸣器发出微弱的呼叫，随后一个声音——他相信是阿彻的嗓音，凭的是他在旅馆休息室里听过的那一回——轻轻唤道：“喂，在吗？”
立刻有了回音。威利斯从没听过本森说话，但他料定回话的必是他。
“有什么要报告的吗？”阿彻问道。
“没有。一切照常。”
“没有生人在附近转悠、盘问？”

===Original===
“No.”
“And no traces of a visitor while you were away?”
“None.”
“Good. It’s probably a false alarm. Beamish may have been mistaken.”
“I hope so, but he seemed very suspicious of that Scotland Yard man﻿—said he was sure he was out for more than he pretended. He thought he was too easily satisfied with the information he got, and that some of his questions were too foolish to be genuine.”
Inspector Willis sat up sharply. This was a blow to his dignity, and he felt not a little scandalised. But he had no time to consider his feelings. Archer was speaking again.
===Chinese===
“没有。”
“你离开那阵子，也没有来过人的痕迹？”
“没有。”
“好。多半是虚惊一场。比米什也许是弄错了。”
“但愿如此。不过他对那个苏格兰场来的人好像疑心很重——说那人肯定图的不止表面上装出来的那一点，觉得他对到手的消息满足得太痛快了些，有些问题又问得蠢，不像是真心发问。”
威利斯探长猛地坐直了。这对他的尊严是一记打击，他颇觉得受了冒犯。但他顾不上计较自己的感受。阿彻又说话了。

===Original===
“I think we had better be on the safe side. If you have the slightest suspicion don’t wait to report to me. Wire at once to Henri at the clearing this message﻿—take it down so that there’ll be no mistake﻿—‘Six hundred four-foot props wanted. If possible send next cargo.’ Got that? He will understand. It is our code for ‘Suspect danger. Send blank cargoes until further notice.’ Then if a search is made nothing will be found, because there won’t be anything there to find.”
“Very good. It’s a pity to lose the money, but I expect you’re right.”
“We can’t take avoidable risks. Now about yourself. I see you brought no stuff up last night?”
“Couldn’t. I had a rotten bilious attack. I started, but had to go back to bed again. Couldn’t stand.”
===Chinese===
“我看咱们还是稳妥些好。你若起了丝毫疑心，不要等着我来吩咐。立刻拍电报给林中空地的亨利，就发这封电文——记下来，免得出错——‘需四英尺坑木六百根。如可能，随下批货发来。’记下了吗？他会明白的。这是咱们的暗码，意思是‘疑有危险。在另行通知前，一律发空货’。这样即便有人来搜，也搜不出名堂，因为那里根本无物可寻。”
“很好。损失这笔钱可惜了点儿，不过我想你说得对。”
“咱们不能冒可以避开的险。现在说你自己。我看你昨晚没把私货运上来？”
“运不成。我闹了回胆病，难受得很。动身了，可只得又回床上去。站都站不住。”

===Original===
“Better?”
“Yes, all right now, thanks.”
“Then you’ll bring the usual up tonight?”
“Certainly.”
“Very well. Now, what about ten forty-five for tomorrow?”
“Right.”
===Chinese===
“好些了？”
“嗯，这会儿没事了，谢谢。”
“那你今晚照常把货送上来？”
“当然。”
“很好。那么，明天十点三刻，怎么样？”
“好。”

===Original===
The switch snapped, and in a few seconds the watcher saw Archer return to his office, bend for a moment over the lock of the door, then reseat himself at his desk.
“I’ve got them now,” he thought triumphantly. “I’ve got them at last. Tonight I’ll take them red-handed in whatever they’re doing.” He smiled in anticipation. “By Jove,” he went on, “it was lucky they sent nothing up last night, or they would have taken me red-handed, and that might have been the end of me!”
He was greatly impressed by the excellence of the telephone scheme. There was nothing anywhere about it to excite suspicion, and it kept Archer in touch with the illicit undertaking, while enabling him to hold himself absolutely aloof from all its members. If the rest of the organisation was as good, it was not surprising that Hilliard and Merriman had been baffled.
But the puzzle was now solved, the mystery at an end. That night, so Willis assured himself, the truth would be known.
===Chinese===
开关咔哒一声，几秒钟后，守望者看见阿彻回到自己办公室，朝门锁俯身片刻，然后重新在写字台前坐下。
“这下可逮住他们了，”他得意扬扬地想。“我终于逮住他们了。今晚不管他们在干什么，我都要当场将他们人赃并获。”他想着想着笑了。“天哪，”他又想道，“幸亏他们昨晚什么也没送上来，不然被当场抓住的倒是我，那也许就是我的末日了！”
这套电话方案的精妙令他叹服。它浑身上下没有一处惹人疑心的地方，既让阿彻同那桩非法营生声气相通，又使他得以同其所有成员全然不沾。倘若这个组织的其余部分也这般严密，希利亚德和梅里曼一筹莫展，也就不足为奇了。
可如今谜已解开，玄机已到尽头。那天夜里——威利斯这样给自己打保票——真相必将大白。

===Original===
He remained in his hiding place all day, until, indeed, he had watched the workers at the distillery leave and the grey shadows of evening had begun to descend. Then he hid the telephone and wire in a cupboard, stealthily left the house, and after a rapid glance round hurried along the lane towards Ferriby.
He caught the 6:57 train to Hull, and in a few minutes was at the police station. There he saw the superintendent, and after a little trouble got him to fall in with the plan which he had devised.
As a result of their conference a large car left the city shortly before nine, in which were seated Inspector Willis and eight picked constables in plain clothes. They drove to the end of the Ferriby Lane, where the men dismounted, and took cover behind some shrubs, while the car returned towards Hull.
===Chinese===
他在藏身之处守了一整天，直到亲眼看着蒸馏酒厂的工人散尽、黄昏的灰色阴影开始降临。于是他把电话和电线藏进一个柜子，蹑手蹑脚出了屋，飞快地四下一瞥，便匆匆沿着小路往费里比去了。
他赶上了 6:57 开往赫尔的火车，几分钟后到了警察局。他在那里见了警司，费了些周折才让警司赞同他拟定的计划。
会商的结果，是一辆大汽车九点前不久驶出城去，车里坐着威利斯探长和八名挑选出来的便衣警员。他们开到费里比小路的尽头，众人下了车，隐蔽到几丛灌木后面，汽车便折回赫尔去了。

===Original===
It was almost, but not quite dark. There was no moon, but the sky was clear and the stars were showing brightly. A faint air, in which there was already a touch of chill, sighed gently through the leaves, rising at intervals almost to a breeze, then falling away again to nothing. Lights were showing here and there﻿—yellow gleams from unshaded windows, signal lamps from the railway, navigation lights from the river. Except for the sound of the retreating car and the dull roar of a distant train, the night was very still, a night, in fact, preeminently suitable for the inspector’s purpose.
The nine men moved silently down the lane at intervals of a few minutes, their rubber-shod feet making no sound on the hard surface. Willis went first, and as the others reached him he posted them in the positions on which he had previously decided. One man took cover behind the hedge of the lane, a short distance on the distillery side of the wharf, another behind a pile of old material on the railway at the same place, a third hid himself among some bushes on the open ground between the railway and the river, while a fourth crept as near to the end of the wharf as the tide would allow, so as to watch approaches from the water. When they were in position, Willis felt convinced no one could leave the syndicate’s depot for the distillery without being seen.
The other four men he led on to the distillery, placing them in a similar manner on its Ferriby side. If by some extraordinary chance the messenger with the “stuff” should pass the first cordon, the second, he was satisfied, would take him. He left himself free to move about as might appear desirable.
The country was extraordinarily deserted. Not one of the nine men had seen a living soul since they left their motor, and Willis felt certain that his dispositions had been carried out in absolute secrecy.
===Chinese===
天差不多黑了，只是还没有黑透。没有月亮，天空却晴朗，星星亮得很。一缕空气里已带一丝寒意的微风，轻轻叹息着穿过树叶，时而几乎涨成一阵轻风，时而又归于无有。灯火四处可见——无遮无拦的窗户里漏出的黄光、铁路上的信号灯、河上的航行灯。除了渐行渐远的汽车声和远处一列火车的沉闷轰鸣，夜真是静极了——这样一个夜晚，实在最最适合探长的目的。
九个人每隔几分钟下去一个，悄没声儿地沿小路推进，胶底鞋踩在硬路面上一点声响也没有。威利斯打头，其余的人一个个到他跟前，他便把他们安置到事先定好的位置上。一人埋伏在小路的树篱后面、码头靠蒸馏酒厂一侧过去不远的地方；另一人藏在同一地段铁路上一堆废旧物料背后；第三人躲在铁路与河流之间那片开阔地的灌木丛里；第四人则趁潮水允许，尽量爬近码头尽头，好监视水路上来的路径。各人就位之后，威利斯确信，谁也别想从辛迪加货栈往蒸馏酒厂去而不被瞧见。
其余四人，他领到蒸馏酒厂跟前，照同样的方式安置在厂区靠费里比的一侧。倘若那运送“私货”的信使竟会侥幸穿过第一道警戒线，他确信第二道也必将他拿下。他自己则留着自由之身，哪里看来有需要便往哪里去。
乡间冷清得出奇。九个人下了汽车以后，谁也没有见过一个活人，威利斯因此确信，他的布置是在绝对秘密之中完成的。

===Original===
He crossed the fence on to the railway. By climbing halfway up the ladder of a signal he was able to see the windows of the shed over the galvanized fence. All were in darkness, and he wondered if Benson had gone on his customary expedition into Hull.
To satisfy himself on this point he hid beneath a wagon which was standing on the siding close to the gate in the fence. If the manager were returning by his usual train he would be due in a few minutes, and Willis intended to wait and see.
It was not long before a sharp footfall told that someone was coming along the lane. The unknown paused at the stile, climbed over; and, walking more carefully across the rails, approached the door. Willis, whose eyes were accustomed to the gloom, could make out the dim form of a man, showing like a smudge of intensified blackness against the obscurity beyond. He unlocked the door, passed through, slammed it behind him, and his retreating steps sounded from within. Finally another door closed in the distance and silence again reigned.
Willis crawled out from beneath his truck and once more climbed the signal ladder. The windows of Benson’s office were now lighted up, but the blinds being drawn, the inspector could see nothing within.
===Chinese===
他跨过栅栏上了铁路。爬到一座信号机梯子的半腰，便能越过镀锌铁栅栏望见棚屋的窗户。窗内一片漆黑，他不知道本森是不是照例进赫尔去了。
为把这一点弄个踏实，他藏到一辆停在栅栏门近旁侧线上的货车底下。经理若是坐他往常那班火车回来，几分钟后就该到了，威利斯打算等着看个究竟。
不多时，一阵清晰的脚步声表明，有人正沿小路走来。那生人在越篱的踏级前停了停，跨了过去；过路轨时放轻了脚步，向那道门走去。威利斯的眼睛已惯于昏暗，辨得出一个男人模糊的身形，在身后更深的幽暗衬托下，像一团格外浓的黑影。他开了锁，走了进去，砰地带上身后的门，渐渐远去的脚步声在屋里响了一阵。最后，远处又有一扇门关上，寂静重新笼罩了一切。
威利斯从货车底下爬出来，再一次爬上信号机梯子。本森办公室的窗户此刻亮着灯，但窗帘拉得严严实实，探长什么也看不见。

===Original===
After about half an hour he observed the same phenomenon as Hilliard and Merriman had witnessed﻿—the light was carried from the office to the bedroom, and a few minutes later disappeared altogether.
The ladder on which he was standing appearing to Willis to offer as good an observation post as he could hope to get, he climbed to the little platform at the top, and seating himself, leaned back against the timber upright and continued his watch.
Though he was keenly interested by his adventure, time soon began to drag. It was cramped on the little seat, and he could not move freely for fear of falling off. Then to his dismay he began to grow sleepy. He had of course been up all the previous night, and though he had dozed a little during his vigil in the deserted house, he had not really rested. He yawned, stretched himself carefully, and made a determined effort to overcome his drowsiness.
===Chinese===
约莫半个钟头后，他见到了希利亚德和梅里曼曾亲眼目睹的那一幕——灯光从办公室移进卧室，几分钟后便彻底熄灭。
在威利斯看来，他脚下这架梯子已是所能指望的顶好的瞭望哨，于是他爬上梯顶的小平台，坐下来，背靠着木立柱，继续守望。
这番历险虽叫他兴致勃勃，时间一久便开始难熬了。小平台上坐着局促，又怕跌下去，不敢随意挪动。接着，令他沮丧的是，他渐渐犯起困来。他前一天夜里本来就整宿没睡，虽说在无人居住的空屋里守望时打过一小会儿盹，却谈不上真正休息。他打了个哈欠，小心地伸展了一下身子，横下心来要战胜睡意。

===Original===
He was suddenly and unexpectedly successful. He got the start of his life, and for a moment he thought an earthquake had come. The signal post trembled and swayed while with a heavy metallic clang objects moved through the darkness near his head. He gripped the rail, and then he laughed as he remembered that railway signals were movable. This one had just been lowered for a train.
Presently it roared past him, enveloping him in a cloud of steam, which for an instant was lit bright as day by the almost white beam that poured out of the open door of the engine firebox. Then, the steam clearing, there appeared a strip of faintly lit ground on either side of the flying carriage roofs; it promptly vanished; red tail lamps appeared, leaping away; there was a rattle of wheels over siding connections, and with a rapidly decreasing roar the visitation was past. For a moment there remained the quickly moving spot of lighted steam, then it too vanished. Once again the signal post swayed as the heavy mechanism of the arm dropped back into the “on” position, and then all was once more still.
The train had effectually wakened Willis, and he set himself with a renewed vigour to this task. Sharply he watched the dark mass of the shed with its surrounding enclosure, keenly he listened for some sound of movement within. But all remained dark and silent.
===Chinese===
这一战出乎意料地大获全胜。他惊得魂飞魄散，一时间竟以为地震来了。信号柱颤抖着摇晃起来，同时伴着沉重的金属铿锵声，有什么物件在他头边的黑暗里移动。他死死抓住梯栏，随即想起铁路信号机是会动的，不由笑了。这一座方才为放行一列火车落了下去。
顷刻之间，列车从他身边轰隆驶过，把他裹进一团蒸汽，机车敞开的炉门里倾泻出一道近乎白亮的光柱，刹那间把蒸汽照得亮如白昼。随后蒸汽消散，飞掠的客车车顶两侧现出一条条微明的地面，旋即隐没；红色的尾灯跳跃着远去；车轮碾过侧线接茬处哐啷作响，这一场过客便带着迅速衰减的轰鸣过去了。有一瞬，那团被照亮的蒸汽还化作一个迅速移动的光斑留在原处，随后它也无影无踪。信号机沉重的机械臂落回“阻断”位置，信号柱又是一阵摇晃，然后一切复归寂静。
这趟车把威利斯彻底震醒了，他重新抖擞精神投入任务。他目光锐利地盯住棚屋连同四周围场的那团黑影，凝神捕捉里面可能发出的些微动静。然而一切照旧又黑又静。

===Original===
Towards one in the morning he descended from his perch and went the round of his men. All were alert, and all were unanimous that no one had passed.
The time dragged slowly on. The wind had risen somewhat and clouds were banking towards the northwest. It grew colder, and Willis fancied there must be a touch of frost.
About four o’clock he went round his pickets for the second time. He was becoming more and more surprised that the attempt had been delayed so long, and when some two hours later the coming dawn began to brighten the eastern sky and still no sign had been observed, his chagrin waxed keen. As the light increased, he withdrew his men to cover, and about seven o’clock, when it was no longer possible that anything would be attempted, he sent them by ones and twos to await their car at the agreed rendezvous.
He was more disappointed at the failure of his trap than he would have believed possible. What, he wondered, could have happened? Why had the conspirators abandoned their purpose? Had he given himself away? He went over in his mind every step he had taken, and he did not see how any one of them could have become known to his enemies, or how any of his actions could have aroused their suspicions. No; it was not, he felt sure, that they had realised their danger. Some other quite accidental circumstance had intervened to cause them to postpone the transfer of the “stuff” for that night. But what extraordinary hard luck for him! He had obtained his helpers from the superintendent only after considerable trouble, and the difficulty of getting them again would be much greater. And not the least annoying thing was that he, a London man, one, indeed, of the best men at the Yard, had been made to look ridiculous in the eyes of these provincial police!
===Chinese===
凌晨一点前后，他从高座上下来，把部下巡查了一遍。个个警醒，个个异口同声：没有一个人经过。
时间慢吞吞地挨着。风大了些，云正在朝西北积聚。天更冷了，威利斯觉得怕是上了点霜。
四点前后，他第二遍巡了岗哨。行动迟迟不见发生，他越来越惊讶；待到约莫两个钟头后，将至的曙光开始照亮东方天际、却仍不见一点动静时，他的懊恼便强烈起来。天色渐亮，他把人撤到隐蔽处；到七点前后，眼看无论如何不会再有什么举动，他便让他们三三两两到约定的集合地点去等汽车。
这次设伏落空令他大失所望，其程度远超出他自己所能相信。他想，到底出了什么事？那伙同谋为什么放弃了计划？是他露了行藏吗？他把采取过的每一步在心里过了一遍，看不出其中哪一步可能被敌人知晓，也看不出哪个举动会惹起他们的疑心。不，他确信不是他们意识到了危险。是别的什么纯属偶然的情况插了进来，害得他们把那一晚的“私货”运送推迟了。可这对他是多么离奇的背运啊！他费了老大的周折才从警司那里借来这班人手，再想借一回，难处可就大得多了。而最教人窝火的一桩还在于：他这个伦敦来的人，而且是苏格兰场数一数二的干员之一，竟在这些外省警察眼里落了个笑柄！

===Original===
Dog-tired and hungry though he was, he set his teeth and determined that he would return to the cottage in the hope of learning the reason of his failure from the conversation which he expected would take place between Archer and Benson at a quarter to eleven that day.
Repeating, therefore, his proceedings of the previous morning, he regained his point of vantage at the broken window. Again he watched the staff arrive, and again observed Archer enter and take his place at his desk. He was desperately sleepy, and it required all the power of his strong will to keep himself awake. But at last his perseverance was rewarded, and at 10:45 exactly he saw Archer bolt his door and disappear towards the filing room. A moment later the buzzer sounded.
“Are you there?” once again came in Archer’s voice, followed by the astounding phrase, “I see you brought up that stuff last night.”
“Yes, I brought up two hundred and fifty,” was Benson’s amazing reply.
===Chinese===
他虽然累得半死、饿得发慌，还是咬紧牙关打定主意：回到村舍去，指望从当天十点三刻阿彻与本森之间料想会有的通话里，探出他此番失手的原因。
于是他重复头天早晨的做法，重新在破窗跟前占好了有利位置。他又看着员工们到齐，又看着阿彻进来在写字台前落座。他困得要命，全凭坚强意志的全力支撑，才使自己没有睡去。但坚忍终于得了报偿：恰好在 10:45，他看见阿彻闩上房门，朝档案室方向消失了。片刻之后，蜂鸣器响了。
“喂，在吗？”又是阿彻的嗓音，紧跟着是一句叫人瞠目结舌的话：“我看你昨晚把那批私货运上来了。”
“是啊，运上来了二百五十，”本森的回答更是惊人。

===Original===
Inspector Willis gasped. He could scarcely believe his ears. So he had been tricked after all! In spite of his carefully placed pickets, in spite of his own ceaseless watchfulness, he had been tricked. Two hundred and fifty of the illicit somethings had been conveyed, right under his and his men’s noses, from the depot to the distillery. Almost choking with rage and amazement he heard Archer continue:
“I had a lucky deal after our conversation yesterday, got seven hundred unexpectedly planted. You may send up a couple of hundred extra tonight if you like.”
“Right. I shall,” Benson answered, and the conversation ceased.
Inspector Willis swore bitterly as he lay back on the dusty floor and pillowed his head on his hands. And then while he still fumed and fretted, outraged nature asserted herself and he fell asleep.
He woke, ravenously hungry, as it was getting dusk, and he did not delay long in letting himself out of the house, regaining the lane, and walking to Ferriby Station. An hour later he was dining at his hotel in Hull.
===Chinese===
威利斯探长倒抽了一口气。他简直不敢相信自己的耳朵。原来他到底还是被耍了！尽管他布下了周密的岗哨，尽管他自己一刻不停地守望，他还是被耍了。二百五十件非法的什么玩意儿，就在他和他那班人马的鼻子底下，从货栈运进了蒸馏酒厂。他几乎被怒气和惊愕噎住，又听阿彻继续说道：
“昨天通话之后，我做了一笔走运的买卖，意外弄到七百，都已藏置妥当。你愿意的话，今晚可以多送两百上来。”
“好。我会的。”本森答道，通话就此结束。
威利斯探长仰面躺倒在积尘的地板上，双手枕着头，狠狠地咒骂了一场。而他还在那儿又气又恼，被亏待透了的身体天性却自行做主，他睡着了。
他醒来时天色将暮，饿得发慌。他没有多耽搁便出了那座房子，回到小路上，步行到费里比车站。一个钟头后，他已在赫尔的旅馆里用晚饭了。

## List of Illustrations / 插图目录

===Original===
1. A map showing the location of the observation point. It lies on the opposite side of the River Lesque and just upstream from the sawmill, which itself is across the clearing from the manager’s house.
2. A map showing from top to bottom a road, railway lines, and the River Humber. Along the railway lines, from Ferriby towards Hull, there the syndicate’s depot, a cottage, Ackroyd & Holt’s and a signal box.
3. A map of the rail network in the area. The line from London to the north crosses the line between Leeds and Hull at Selby. Between Selby and Hull lies Straddlethorpe, Ferriby and Hessle. There’s also a local line from Straddlethorpe that goes through Goole before meeting the London line at Doncaster.

===Chinese===
1. 一幅地图，标示观察点的位置。它位于莱斯克河对岸、紧挨锯木厂上游；锯木厂则隔着林中空地，与经理住宅相对。
2. 一幅地图，自上而下为一条公路、数条铁道线和亨伯河。沿铁道自费里比往赫尔方向，依次是辛迪加的货栈、一座村舍、阿克罗伊德—霍尔特的厂子和一座信号所。
3. 一幅本地区铁路网的地图。从伦敦北上的干线与利兹至赫尔的线路在塞尔比相交。塞尔比与赫尔之间，坐落着斯特拉德尔索普、费里比和赫斯尔。另有一条地方线路自斯特拉德尔索普引出，途经古尔，在唐卡斯特接入伦敦干线。

## X Merriman Becomes Desperate / 十、梅里曼陷入绝望
===Original===
The failure of the attempt to learn the secret of the Pit-Prop Syndicate affected Merriman more than he could have believed possible. His interest in the affair was not that of Hilliard. Neither the intellectual joy of solving a difficult problem for its own sake, nor the kudos which such a solution might bring, made much appeal to him. His concern was simply the happiness of the girl he loved, and though, to do him justice, he did not think overmuch of himself, he recognised that any barrier raised between them was the end for him of all that made life endurable.
As he lay back with closed eyes in the corner seat of a first-class compartment in the boat train from Calais he went over for the thousandth time the details of the problem as it affected himself. Had Mr. Coburn rendered himself liable to arrest or even to penal servitude, and did his daughter know it? The anxious, troubled look which Merriman had on different occasions surprised on the girl’s expressive face made him fear both these possibilities. But if they were true did it stop there? Was her disquietude due merely to knowledge of her father’s danger, or was she herself in peril also? Merriman wondered could she have such knowledge and not be in peril herself. In the eyes of the law would it not be a guilty knowledge? Could she not be convicted as an accessory?
If it were so he must act at once if he were to save her. But how? He writhed under the terrible feeling of impotence produced by his ignorance of the syndicate’s real business. If he were to help Madeleine he must know what the conspirators were doing.
And he had failed to learn. He had failed, and Hilliard had failed, and neither they nor Leatham had been able to suggest any method by which the truth might be ascertained.
There was, of course, the changing of the number plates. A trained detective would no doubt be able to make something of that. But Merriman felt that without even the assistance of Hilliard, he had neither the desire nor the ability to tackle it.
===Chinese===
探听坑木辛迪加秘密的尝试归于失败，这对梅里曼的打击之大，超出了他自己所能相信的限度。他对这桩事端的兴味，本不同于希利亚德。既非为解难题而解难题的那种智性愉悦，也非解题之后可能博得的赞誉，都不大能打动他。他所系念的，无非是他所爱的那位姑娘的幸福；而且，说句公道话，他倒并不怎么把自己放在心上，可他也明白：两人之间但凡竖起一道屏障，对他而言，一切使人生尚可忍受的东西便都到了头。
他仰身合眼，坐在加来开来的邮船列车头等车厢的角落里，第一千次把这道难题中牵涉自身的种种细节从头捋过。科伯恩先生是否已落得可能被捕、乃至服苦役的地步？他女儿是否知情？梅里曼曾多次在那姑娘富于表情的脸上出其不意地瞥见忧心忡忡的神色，这叫他两种可能都害怕。可倘若属实，事情就止于此吗？她的惶惶不安，仅仅出于知晓父亲的凶险，还是她本人也身处危境？梅里曼暗想，她既知道这样的内情，自己还能不惹祸上身吗？在法律眼里，这岂不成了知情不举的罪证？她岂不是可以被定成从犯？
倘若果真如此，他要救她，就必须立即行动。可是怎么行动？他对辛迪加真正的营生一无所知，那股无能为力的可怕感觉压得他五脏如焚。他要帮玛德琳，就必须知道这伙阴谋家在干些什么。
而他没能探出来。他失败了，希利亚德也失败了，他们两人也好，利瑟姆也好，谁都提不出一个查明真相的法子。
当然，还有换车牌那一节。一个受过科班训练的侦探，无疑能从那上头查出些名堂。可梅里曼觉得，眼下连希利亚德的臂助也指望不上，自己既无那份兴致，也没那份本事去招惹它。

===Original===
He pondered the question, as he had pondered it for weeks, and the more he thought, the more he felt himself driven to the direct course﻿—to see Madeleine, put the problem to her, ask her to marry him and come out of it all. But there were terrible objections to this plan, not the least of which was that if he made a blunder it might be irrevocable. She might not hear him at all. She might be displeased by his suggestion that she and her father were in danger from such a cause. She might decide not to leave her father for the very reason that he was in danger. And all these possibilities were, of course, in addition to the much more probable one that she would simply refuse him because she did not care about him.
Merriman did not see his way clearly, and he was troubled. Once he had made up his mind he was not easily turned from his purpose, but he was slow in making it up. In this case, where so much depended on his decision, he found his doubt actually painful.
Mechanically he alighted at the Gare du Nord, crossed Paris, and took his place in the southern express at the Quai d’Orsay. Here he continued wrestling with his problem, and it was not until he was near his destination that he arrived at a decision. He would not bother about further investigations. He would go out and see Madeleine, tell her everything, and put his fate into her hands.
He alighted at the Bastide Station in Bordeaux, and driving across to the city, put up at the Gironde Hotel. There he slept the night, and next day after lunch he took a taxi to the clearing.
===Chinese===
他反复掂量这个问题，如同几个星期以来一直掂量的那样；而越想越觉得，自己被逼上了一条直截了当的路——去见玛德琳，把难题摊给她，求她嫁给自己，同这一切脱开干系。可这条计策有着可怕的难处，其中不小的一条便是：一旦行差踏错，便再无挽回的余地。她也许根本不肯听他往下说。她也许会因为他居然暗示她父女俩因这样的缘由身陷险境而不悦。她也许恰恰因为父亲身处凶险，反而决意不肯离开他。而所有这些可能，当然，还是叠在那个大得多的可能之上——她根本对他无意，索性一口回绝。
梅里曼看不清自己的路，心里烦乱。他一旦拿定主意，便不容易被劝转；可拿定主意之前，却总是迟缓。这一次，千钧系于一决，他只觉得这份犹疑简直成了煎熬。
他浑浑噩噩地在巴黎北站（Gare du Nord）下了车，穿过巴黎城，到奥赛码头车站登上南行快车，寻了座位坐下。一路上他继续同那道难题角力，直到快抵达目的地时，才算拿定了主意：不再为进一步的查探操心。他要径直去见玛德琳，把一切和盘托出，把自己的命运交到她手里。
他在波尔多的巴斯蒂德车站下车，驱车过河进了城，住进吉伦特旅馆（Gironde Hotel）。当夜在那里安歇，第二天午饭后，雇了辆出租汽车前往林中空地。

===Original===
Leaving the vehicle on the main road, he continued on foot down the lane and past the depot until he reached the manager’s house.
The door was opened by Miss Coburn in person. On seeing her visitor she stood for a moment quite motionless while a look of dismay appeared in her eyes and a hot flush rose on her face and then faded, leaving it white and drawn.
“Oh!” she gasped faintly. “It’s you!” She still stood holding the door, as if overcome by some benumbing emotion.
Merriman had pulled off his hat.
===Chinese===
他把车留在大路上，徒步沿小径走下去，经过货栈，来到经理住宅门前。
开门的正是科伯恩小姐本人。一看来客，她怔怔地立了片刻，眼里现出惊惶，脸上腾起一阵红晕，随即又褪了下去，只留下一片惨白与憔悴。
“哦！”她轻轻地倒抽一口气，“是你！”她仍旧抓着门站在那里，仿佛被某种令人麻木的情绪压倒了。
梅里曼已经摘下了帽子。

===Original===
“It is I, Miss Coburn,” he answered gently. “I have come over from London to see you. May I not come in?”
She stepped back.
“Come in, of course,” she said, making an obvious effort to infuse cordiality into her tone. “Come in here.”
He fumbled with his coat in the hall, and by the time he followed her into the drawing-room she had recovered her composure.
===Chinese===
“是我，科伯恩小姐，”他温和地答道，“我从伦敦赶来看你。可以让我进去吗？”
她退后一步。
“当然，请进，”她说着，明显费了番气力往语气里注入热忱，“请到这边来。”
他在门厅里手忙脚乱地脱大衣，等他跟着她走进客厅时，她已经恢复了镇定。

===Original===
She began rather breathlessly to talk commonplaces. At first he answered in the same strain, but directly he made a serious attempt to turn the conversation to the subject of his call she adroitly interrupted him.
“You’ll have some tea?” she said presently, getting up and moving towards the door.
“Er﻿—no﻿—no, thanks, Miss Coburn, not any. I wanted really﻿—”
“But *I* want some tea,” she persisted, smiling. “Come, you may help me to get it ready, but you must have some to keep me company.”
He had perforce to obey, and during the tea-making she effectually prevented any serious discussion. But when the meal was over and they had once more settled down in the drawing-room he would no longer be denied.
===Chinese===
她微微有些气促地说起家常套话。起初他也顺着这个调子应答，可等他郑重想把话头引到来访的正题上，她便机敏地把话岔开。
“喝点茶好吗？”过了一会儿她开口道，站起身朝门口走去。
“呃——不——不用了，谢谢你，科伯恩小姐，一点儿也不用。我其实想——”
“可*我*要喝点茶，”她微笑着坚持道，“来吧，你可以帮我预备，不过你也得喝一点儿陪陪我。”
他只得从命，而在备茶的工夫，她把任何正经的谈话都滴水不漏地挡了开去。可待茶点用完，两人重新在客厅里坐定，他再不容推诿了。

===Original===
“Forgive me,” he entreated, “forgive me for bothering you, but it’s so desperately important to me. And we may be interrupted. *Do* hear what I’ve got to say.”
Without waiting for permission he plunged into the subject. Speaking hoarsely, stammering, contradicting himself, boggling over the words, he yet made himself clear. He loved her; had loved her from that first day they had met; he loved her more than anything else in the world; he﻿—She covered her face with her hands.
“Oh!” she cried wildly. “Don’t go on! Don’t say it!” She made a despairing gesture. “I can’t listen. I tried to stop you.”
Merriman felt as if a cold weight was slowly descending upon his heart.
===Chinese===
“原谅我，”他恳求道，“原谅我打扰你，可这对我实在性命攸关。咱们说不定会被人搅了。*务必*听听我要说的话。”
不等许可，他便一头扎进了正题。嗓音沙哑，结结巴巴，前言不搭后语，字句绊绊磕磕，可他还是把意思说明白了。他爱她；从两人初见的那一天起就一直爱着她；他爱她胜过世上的一切；他——她双手掩住了脸。
“哦！”她狂乱地喊道，“别往下说了！别说出来！”她做了一个绝望的手势，“我听不下去。我方才就想拦住你的。”
梅里曼只觉一块冰冷的重物正慢慢压上心头。

===Original===
“But I will speak,” he cried hoarsely. “It’s my life that’s at stake. Don’t tell me you can’t listen. Madeleine! I love you. I want you to marry me. Say you’ll marry me. Madeleine! Say it!”
He dropped on his knees before her and seized her hands in his own.
“My darling,” he whispered fiercely. “I love you enough for us both. Say you’ll marry me. Say﻿—”
She wrenched her hands from him. “Oh!” she cried as if heartbroken, and burst into an uncontrollable flood of tears.
===Chinese===
“可我偏要说，”他沙哑地喊道，“押上去的是我的一生。别跟我说你听不下去。玛德琳！我爱你。我要你嫁给我。说你愿意嫁给我。玛德琳！说呀！”
他扑通跪在她面前，双手紧紧攥住她的手。
“我亲爱的，”他狂热地低声道，“我爱得抵得上咱们两个。说你愿意嫁给我。说——”
她把手从他手里猛地抽出来。“哦！”她哭喊一声，仿佛心都碎了，随即泪如泉涌，不可遏止。

===Original===
Merriman was maddened beyond endurance by the sight.
“What a brute I am!” he gasped. “Now I’ve made you cry.”
“For pity’s sake! Do stop it! Nothing matters about anything else if only you stop!”
He was almost beside himself with misery as he pleaded with her. But soon he pulled himself together and began to speak more rationally.
===Chinese===
眼前这一幕让梅里曼五内如焚，再难自持。
“我是个多大的浑人哪！”他喘着气说，“这下把你惹哭了。”
“发发慈悲吧！快别哭了！只要你肯住声，旁的什么都不打紧！”
他苦苦哀求，伤心得几乎失了方寸。但不多时他重新振作起来，说起话来也冷静了些。

===Original===
“At least tell me the reason,” he besought. “I know I’ve no right to ask, but it matters so much. Have pity and tell me, is it someone else?”
She shook her head faintly between her sobs.
“Thank goodness for that anyway. Tell me once again. Is it that you don’t like me?”
Again she shook her head.
===Chinese===
“至少告诉我缘故，”他央求道，“我知道自己无权过问，可这关系太大。行行好告诉我：是不是另有人？”
她抽抽噎噎地，微微摇了摇头。
“无论如何，这一点总该谢天谢地。再告诉我一遍：是不是你不欢喜我？”
她又摇了摇头。

===Original===
“You *do* like me!” he exclaimed breathlessly. “You do, Madeleine. Say it! Say that you do!”
She made a resolute effort for self-control.
“You know I do, but﻿—” she began in a tremulous whisper. In a paroxysm of overwhelming excitement he interrupted her.
“Madeleine,” he cried wildly, again seizing her hands, “you don’t﻿—it couldn’t be possible that you﻿—that you *love* me?”
===Chinese===
“你*确实*欢喜我！”他喘着气喊道，“你确实欢喜，玛德琳。说出来！说你确实是欢喜我的！”
她狠命定神，竭力自制。
“你知道我欢喜，可是——”她颤声低语道。他在一阵排山倒海般的激动里打断了她。
“玛德琳，”他又抓住她的手，狂乱地喊道，“你不会——莫非竟有可能，你——你*爱*着我？”

===Original===
This time she did not withdraw her hands. Slowly she raised her eyes to his, and in them he read his answer. In a moment she was in his arms and he was crushing her to his heart.
For a breathless space she lay, a happy little smile on her lips, and then the moment passed. “Oh!” she cried, struggling to release herself, “what have I done? Let me go! I shouldn’t have﻿—”
“Darling,” he breathed triumphantly. “I’ll never let you go as long as I live! You love me! What else matters?”
“No, no,” she cried again, her tears once more flowing. “I was wrong. I shouldn’t have allowed you. It can never be.”
===Chinese===
这一次她没有抽回手。她缓缓抬眼望着他，他从那双眼睛里读出了答案。转眼间她已在他怀中，被紧紧搂在他心口上。
有一小会儿她依偎着不动，唇边挂着一丝幸福的小小笑意，随后这一刻过去了。“哦！”她挣扎着脱身喊道，“我做了什么呀？放开我！我不该——”
“亲爱的，”他得胜地低语，“只要我活着一天，就永远不放你走！你爱我！旁的还算得了什么？”
“不，不，”她又哭喊起来，眼泪重新涌出，“我错了。我不该容你这样。这是万万不能的。”

===Original===
He laughed savagely.
“Never be?” he repeated. “Why, dear one, it *is*. I’d like to know the person or thing that could stop it now!”
“It can never be,” she repeated in a voice of despair. “You don’t understand. There are obstacles.”
She argued. He scoffed first, then he pleaded. He demanded to be told the nature of the barrier, then he besought, but all to no purpose. She would say no more than that it could never be.
===Chinese===
他狞笑了一声。
“万万不能？”他重复道，“嗳，亲爱的，这已经*是*事实了。我倒要看看，如今还有什么人、什么东西拦得住！”
“这是万万不能的，”她用绝望的声音重复道，“你不明白。有阻碍。”
她申辩着。他先是讥诮，继而恳求。他要求人家告知那道屏障究竟是什么，跟着又苦苦哀告，但统统无济于事。她翻来覆去只说一句话：万万不能。

===Original===
And then﻿—suddenly the question of the syndicate flashed into his mind, and he sat, almost gasping with wonder as he realised that he had entirely forgotten it! He had forgotten this mysterious business which had occupied his thoughts to the exclusion of almost all else for the past two months! It seemed to him incredible. Yet so it was.
There surged over him a feeling of relief, so that once more he all but laughed. He turned to Madeleine.
“I know,” he cried triumphantly, “the obstacle. And it’s just nothing at all. It’s this syndicate business that your father has got mixed up in. Now tell me! Isn’t that it?”
The effect of his words on the girl was instantaneous. She started and then sat quite still, while the colour slowly drained from her face, leaving it bleached and deathlike. A look of fear and horror grew in her eyes, and her fingers clasped until the knuckles showed white.
===Chinese===
接着——辛迪加的问题蓦地闪进他的脑海，他坐在那里，惊得几乎透不过气来：自己竟把它忘了个干净！这两个月来占尽他心思、几乎把其余一切都挤了出去的这桩神秘营生，他竟然忘了！他觉得难以置信。然而事实如此。
一股如释重负之感涌遍全身，他险些又笑出声来。他转向玛德琳。
“我知道了，”他得意地喊道，“那道阻碍。它根本就算不得什么。不外是你父亲搅进去的这桩辛迪加营生。快说！是不是这么回事？”
这番话对姑娘的作用立竿见影。她惊跳了一下，随即僵坐不动，脸上的血色一点点褪尽，白得如同死人脸。她眼里渐渐生出恐惧与惊骇，手指绞紧，指节都攥得发白。

===Original===
“Oh!” she stammered brokenly, “what do you mean by that?”
Merriman tried once more to take her hand.
“Dear one,” he said caressingly, “don’t let what I said distress you. We know the syndicate is carrying on something that﻿—well, perhaps wouldn’t bear too close investigation. But that has nothing to do with us. It won’t affect our relations.”
The girl seemed transfixed with horror.
“*We* know?” she repeated dully. “Who are we?”
===Chinese===
“哦！”她语不成句地讷讷道，“你说这话是什么意思？”
梅里曼想再拉她的手。
“亲爱的，”他温存地说，“别让我说的话吓着你。我们知道辛迪加在经营一桩——嗯，兴许经不起细查的勾当。可那同咱们不相干。它不会影响咱们的关系。”
姑娘仿佛被恐惧钉在了原地。
“*我们*知道？”她木然重复道，“这个我们，都是谁？”

===Original===
“Why, Hilliard; Hilliard and I. We found out quite by accident that there was something secret going on. We were both interested; Hilliard has a mania for puzzles, and besides he thought he might get some kudos if the business was illegal and he could bring it to light, while I knew that because of Mr. Coburn’s connection with it the matter might affect you.”
“Yes?” She seemed hardly able to frame the syllable between her dry lips.
Merriman was profoundly unhappy. He felt it was out of the question for him to tell her anything but the exact truth. Whether she would consider he had acted improperly in spying on the syndicate he did not know, but even at the risk of destroying his own chance of happiness he could not deceive her.
“Dear one,” he said in a low tone, “don’t think any worse of me than you can help, and I will tell you everything. You remember that first day that I was here, when you met me in the lane and we walked to the mill?”
===Chinese===
“喏，希利亚德；希利亚德和我。我们纯属偶然地发觉这里面正进行着什么隐秘勾当。我们俩都起了兴味：希利亚德对谜题素有癖好，再者他还想，倘若这桩营生不合法、又能由他揭发出来，或许能博些赞誉；而我呢，我知道正因科伯恩先生牵涉在内，这事可能会连累到你。”
“是吗？”她干燥的嘴唇似乎连这一个音节都吐不利落。
梅里曼深深陷于不快。他觉得，除了原原本本的实话，自己什么也不能对她说。监视辛迪加一事，她会不会认为他行止失当，他不得而知；但哪怕赔上自己仅有的幸福前程，他也不能欺瞒她。
“亲爱的，”他放低声音说，“请把我想得尽量好些，我会把一切都告诉你。你记得我头一回到这儿来的那天吧，你在小径上迎着我，咱们一路走去锯木厂？”

===Original===
She nodded.
“You may recall that a lorry had just arrived, and that I stopped and stared at it? Well, I had noticed that the number plate had been changed.”
“Ah,” she exclaimed, “I was afraid you had.”
“Yes, I saw it, though it conveyed nothing to me. But I was interested, and one night in London, just to make conversation in the club, I mentioned what I had seen. Hilliard was present, and he joined me on the way home and insisted on talking over the affair. As I said, he has a mania for puzzles, and the mystery appealed to him. He was going on that motorboat tour across France, and he suggested that I should join him and that we should call here on our way, so as to see if we could find the solution. Neither of us thought then, you understand, that there was anything wrong; he was merely interested. I didn’t care about the mystery, but I confess I leaped at the idea of coming back in order to meet you again, and on the understanding that there was to be nothing in the nature of spying, I agreed to his proposal.”
===Chinese===
她点了点头。
“你也许记得，当时刚好到了一辆卡车，我停下来盯着它看了半天？是这样，我当时看出车牌被换过了。”
“啊，”她失声道，“我就怕你看出来了。”
“是的，我看见了，虽说当时并没咂摸出什么名堂。可我上了心，后来有天晚上在伦敦，为着寻话说，我在俱乐部里提起了这桩见闻。希利亚德在场，回家路上他陪我同行，非要聊聊这件事不可。我方才说过，他对谜题素有癖好，这桩疑案正对他胃口。他正要驾机动艇横穿法国出游，便提议我与他同行，顺路在此地停上一站，看能不能寻出谜底。你要明白，我们俩当时谁也没想到里头有什么不轨；他纯粹是感兴趣。至于那桩疑案，我本无所谓，可我承认，一想到能借机回来再见你，我便求之不得；讲定了绝无半点刺探的意思，我才应允了他的提议。”

===Original===
Merriman paused, but the girl, whose eyes were fixed intently on his face, made no remark, and he continued:
“While we were here, Hilliard, who is very observant and clever, saw one or two little things which excited his suspicion, and without telling me, he slipped on board the *Girondin* and overheard a conversation between Mr. Coburn, Captain Beamish, Mr. Bulla, and Henri. He learned at once that something serious and illegal was in progress, but he did not learn what it was.”
“Then there *was* spying,” she declared accusingly.
===Chinese===
梅里曼顿了顿，可姑娘的目光紧盯着他的脸，一言不发，他便接着说下去：
“咱们在此地停留的时候，眼尖心细的希利亚德看出了一两处小节，起了疑心；他没告诉我就悄悄溜上*吉伦丁号*，偷听到了科伯恩先生、比米什船长、布拉先生和亨利之间的一席谈话。他当即明白正在进行的是某种严重的非法勾当，只是没弄清究竟是什么。”
“那么这*就*是刺探了，”她带着责难的口吻断言道。

===Original===
“There was,” he admitted. “I can only say that under the circumstances he thought himself justified.”
“Go on,” she ordered shortly.
“We returned then to England, and were kept at our offices for about a week. But Hilliard felt that we could not drop the matter, as we should then become accomplices. Besides, he was interested. He proposed we should try to find out more about it. This time I agreed, but I would ask you, Madeleine, to believe me when I tell you my motive, and to judge me by it. He spoke of reporting what he had learned to the police, and if I hadn’t agreed to help him he would have done so. I wanted at all costs to avoid that, because if there was going to be any trouble I wanted Mr. Coburn to be out of it first. Believe me or not, that was my only reason for agreeing.”
===Chinese===
“是刺探，”他承认，“我只能说，在那般情形之下，他自认问心无愧。”
“往下说。”她简短地命令道。
“随后我们回了英国，在商行里脱不开身，忙了约莫一个星期。可希利亚德觉得此事再撂不得了，否则我们便成了从犯。何况他兴味正浓。他提议咱们设法再多探出些底细。这一回我答应了，但玛德琳，我求你，听我说出我的动机时，请相信它，并依着它来评判我。他说过要把探得的情况报告警方，倘若我不肯相助，他早就去了。我不惜一切代价要避开这一步，因为万一要出乱子，我指望科伯恩先生能先脱身出来。信不信由你，我应允的理由仅此一条。”

===Original===
“I do believe you,” she said, “but finish what you have to tell me.”
“We learned from *Lloyd’s List* that the *Girondin* put into Hull. We went there and at Ferriby, seven miles upstream, we found the depot where she discharged the props. You don’t know it?”
She shook her head.
“It’s quite like this place; just a wharf and shed, with an enclosure between the river and the railway. We made all the inquiries and investigations we could think of, but we learned absolutely nothing. But that, unfortunately, is the worst of it. Hilliard is disgusted with our failure and appears determined to tell the police.”
===Chinese===
“我信你，”她说，“不过把你要说的说完。”
“我们从《劳氏日报》上得知*吉伦丁号*进了赫尔港。我们赶到那里，在赫尔上游七英里的费里比，找到了她卸坑木的货栈。你没听说过那地方？”
她摇了摇头。
“那地方跟这儿很像：不过一方码头、一间货棚，外加夹在河道与铁路之间的一片围场。凡是想得出的查问探访，我们都做尽了，却一无所获。可不幸的是，最糟的正在这里。希利亚德对我们的失败大为光火，看样子是铁了心要报告警方了。”

===Original===
“Oh!” cried the girl with an impatient gesture. “Why can’t he let it alone? It’s not his business.”
Merriman shrugged his shoulders.
“That’s what he said at all events. I had the greatest difficulty in getting him to promise even to delay. But he has promised, and we have a month to make our plans. I came straight over to tell you, and to ask you to marry me at once and come away with me to England.”
===Chinese===
“哦！”姑娘不耐烦地一挥手，喊道，“他为什么就不能撒手呢？这又不关他的事。”
梅里曼耸了耸肩。
“无论如何，这话他自己也说过。我费了九牛二虎之力，才求得他答应暂缓行事。不过他总算答应了，咱们有一个月的工夫可以从长计议。我便径直赶过来告诉你，求你立刻嫁给我，随我一同去英国。”

===Original===
“Oh, no, no, no!” she cried, putting up her hand as if to shield herself from the idea. “Besides, what about my father?”
“I’ve thought about him too,” Merriman returned. “We will tell him the whole thing, and he will be able to get out before the crash comes.”
For some moments she sat in silence; then she asked had Hilliard any idea of what was being done.
“He suggested brandy smuggling, but it was only a theory. There was nothing whatever to support it.”
“Brandy smuggling? Oh, if it only were!”
Merriman stared in amazement.
===Chinese===
“哦，不，不，不行！”她举起一只手，仿佛要把这个念头挡开，“再说，我父亲怎么办？”
“我也替他盘算过了，”梅里曼答道，“咱们把原委全部告诉他，他来得及在大祸临头之前抽身。”
她默默坐了片刻，然后问，希利亚德是否猜到了他们干的是什么营生。
“他猜是白兰地走私，不过只是个推想。什么凭据也没有。”
“白兰地走私？哦，但愿真是这个就好了！”
梅里曼惊愕地瞪着她。

===Original===
“It wouldn’t be so bad as what I had feared,” the girl added, answering his look.
“And that was﻿—? Do trust me, Madeleine.”
“I do trust you, and I will tell you all I know; it isn’t much. I was afraid they were printing and circulating false money.”
Merriman was genuinely surprised.
“False money?” he repeated blankly.
===Chinese===
“那总不至于糟到我担心的那个地步。”姑娘望着他的眼色，又添了一句。
“你担心的又是——？务请信任我，玛德琳。”
“我信任你，我也愿意把我知道的都告诉你；所知不多。我担心他们在印制、流通假钞。”
梅里曼当真吃了一惊。
“假钞？”他茫然地重复道。

===Original===
“Yes; English Treasury notes. I thought they were perhaps printing them over here, and sending some to England with each trip of the *Girondin*. It was a remark I accidentally overheard that made me think so. But, like you, it was only a guess. I had no proof.”
“Tell me,” Merriman begged.
“It was last winter when the evenings closed in early. I had had a headache and I had gone to rest for a few minutes in the next room, the dining-room, which was in darkness. The door between it and this room was almost but not quite closed. I must have fallen asleep, for I suddenly became conscious of voices in here, though I had heard no one enter. I was going to call out when a phrase arrested my attention. I did not mean to listen, but involuntarily I stayed quiet for a moment. You understand?”
===Chinese===
“是的；英国财政部纸币（English Treasury notes）。我想他们多半是在这边印好，趁*吉伦丁号*每一趟航行动身，夹带一批去英国。是我不小心听见的一句话引我这么想的。不过同你们一样，也只是个猜测。我没有凭据。”
“讲给我听。”梅里曼恳求道。
“那是去年冬天，天黑得早。我有点头疼，便到隔壁的房间——饭厅——去歇几分钟，屋里没有点灯。饭厅和这间屋子之间的门虚掩着，差一点儿才关严。我一定是睡着了，因为我忽然察觉这边有人说话，却没听见有谁进来。我正要出声，有一句话拉住了我的注意。我并不存心偷听，可身不由己地静了一小会儿。你明白吗？”

===Original===
“Of course. It was the natural thing to do.”
“Captain Beamish was speaking. He was just finishing a sentence and I only caught the last few words. ‘So that’s a profit of six thousand, seven hundred and fifty pounds,’ he said; ‘fifty pounds loss on the props, and six thousand seven hundred netted over the other. Not bad for one trip!’ ”
“Lord!” Merriman exclaimed in amazement. “No wonder you stopped!”
===Chinese===
“当然。换了谁都会这么做。”
“说话的是比米什船长。一句话正说到尾上，我只听见最后几个词。‘这么一趟下来，利润是 6,750 英镑，’他说，‘坑木上亏 50 英镑，那一头净落 6,700 英镑。跑一趟，不算白辛苦！’”
“老天！”梅里曼惊叹道，“难怪你收住了脚！”

===Original===
“I couldn’t understand what was meant, and while I sat undecided what to do I heard my father say, ‘No trouble planting the stuff?’ Captain Beamish answered, ‘Archer said not, but then Archer is﻿—Archer. He’s planting it in small lots﻿—ten here, twenty there, fifty in t’other place; I don’t think he put out more than fifty at any one time. And he says he’s only learning his way round, and that he’ll be able to form better connections to get rid of it.’ Then Mr. Bulla spoke, and this was what upset me so much and made me think, ‘Mr. Archer is a wonderful man,’ he said with that horrible fat chuckle of his, ‘he would plant stuff on Old Nick himself with the whole of the C.I.D. looking on.’ I was bewildered and rather horrified, and I did not wait to hear any more. I crept away noiselessly, and I didn’t want to be found as it were listening. Even then I did not understand that anything was wrong, but it happened that the very next day I was walking through the forest near the lane, and I noticed Henri changing the numbers on the lorry. He didn’t see me, and he had such a stealthy surreptitious air, that I couldn’t but see it was not a joke. Putting two and two together I felt something serious was going on, and that night I asked my father what it was.”
“Well done!” Merriman exclaimed admiringly.
“But it was no use. He made little of it at first, but when I pressed him he said that against his will he had been forced into an enterprise which he hated and which he was trying to get out of. He said I must be patient and we should get away from it as quickly as possible. But since then,” she added despondently, “though I have returned to the subject time after time he has always put me off, saying that we must wait a little longer.”
“And then you thought of the false notes?”
“Yes, but I had no reason to do so except that I couldn’t think of anything else that would fit the words I had overheard. Planting stuff by tens or twenties or fifties seemed to﻿—”
===Chinese===
“我听不懂这话是什么意思，正坐着拿不定主意，又听见我父亲说：‘把货撒出去，没遇到麻烦吧？’比米什船长答道：‘阿彻说没有，不过阿彻嘛——就是阿彻。他正一小批一小批地撒：这儿十份，那儿二十份，别处五十份；我看他一次出手从没超过五十。他还说，自己不过是在摸路子，往后能结上更好的关系，把货出脱。’随后布拉先生开了口，正是这句话让我那样心神不宁，起了疑心。‘阿彻先生真是位了不起的人物，’他带着他那副令人厌恶的肥腻笑声说道，‘哪怕刑事侦查部（C.I.D.）全体在场围观，他也能把货塞给老尼克（Old Nick）本人。’我一时茫然，又有些害怕，便没再听下去。我蹑手蹑脚地溜了出去，也不愿让人撞见、仿佛存心偷听似的。即便到了那时，我还不明白有什么不轨；可巧第二天，我正在小径附近的林子里散步，看见亨利在卡车上改号码。他没瞧见我，而他那副鬼鬼祟祟、遮遮掩掩的样子，令我不可能不当真。两下一串，我便觉得正有什么严重的事在进行，当晚就问我父亲那究竟是什么。”
“好样的！”梅里曼赞佩地喊道。
“可是没有用。起先他满不在乎，待我逼紧了，他才说，他迫于无奈，被裹进了一桩自己厌恶、正想脱身的营生。他说我必须耐心，咱们会尽早从中脱身。可是打那以后，”她垂头丧气地补了一句，“尽管我一回又一回重提这事，他总是搪塞我，说咱们还得再等一等。”
“后来你就想到假钞了？”
“是的，可我并无凭据，只是想不出别的什么，能对上我偶然偷听到的那些话。十个、二十个、五十个地撒货，似乎——”

===Original===
There was a sudden noise in the hall and Madeleine broke off to listen.
“Father,” she whispered breathlessly. “Don’t say anything.”
Merriman had just time to nod when the door opened and Mr. Coburn appeared on the threshold. For a moment he stood looking at his daughter’s visitor, while the emotions of doubt, surprise and annoyance seemed to pass successively through his mind. Then he advanced with outstretched hand and a somewhat satirical smile on his lips.
“Ah, it is the good Merriman,” he exclaimed. “Welcome once more to our humble abode. And where is brother Hilliard? You don’t mean to say you have come without him?”
===Chinese===
门厅里忽然起了响动，玛德琳收住话头细听。
“父亲，”她屏息低语，“什么都别说了。”
梅里曼只来得及点点头，门就开了，科伯恩先生出现在门口。他站在那里打量女儿的来客，疑惑、惊讶和不快似乎在他心里相继掠过。随后他迎上前来，伸出手，唇边挂着一丝略带讥诮的笑。
“啊，来的是我们的好梅里曼，”他高声道，“欢迎再度光临寒舍。希利亚德老弟何在？你该不是说，这回没同他一道来吧？”

===Original===
His tone jarred on Merriman, but he answered courteously: “I left him in London. I had business bringing me to this neighbourhood, and when I reached Bordeaux I took the opportunity to run out to see you and Miss Coburn.”
The manager replied suitably, and the conversation became general. As soon as he could with civility, Merriman rose to go. Mr. Coburn cried out in protest, but the other insisted.
Mr. Coburn had become more cordial, and the two men strolled together across the clearing. Merriman had had no opportunity of further private conversation with Madeleine, but he pressed her hand and smiled at her encouragingly on saying goodbye.
===Chinese===
这语调让梅里曼听着刺耳，但他客气地答道：“我把他留在伦敦了。我有些事情要到这一带来，到了波尔多，便趁机跑来看望你和科伯恩小姐。”
经理得体地应答了几句，谈话转入了寻常话题。梅里曼一逮着不失礼数的空当，便起身告辞。科伯恩先生出声挽留，他却执意要走。
科伯恩先生变得热络起来，两人一同穿过空地散步。梅里曼再没有机会同玛德琳私下交谈，但道别时他紧握了握她的手，向她投去鼓励的微笑。

===Original===
As the taxi bore him swiftly back towards Bordeaux, his mind was occupied with the girl to the exclusion of all else. It was not so much that he thought definitely about her, as that she seemed to fill all his consciousness. He felt numb, and his whole being ached for her as with a dull physical pain. But it was a pain that was mingled with exultation, for if she had refused him, she had at least admitted that she loved him. Incredible thought! He smiled ecstatically, then, the sense of loss returning, once more gazed gloomily ahead into vacancy. As the evening wore on his thoughts turned towards what she had said about the syndicate. Her forged note theory had come to him as a complete surprise, and he wondered whether she really had hit on the true solution of the mystery. The conversation she had overheard undoubtedly pointed in that direction. “Planting stuff” was, he believed, the technical phrase for passing forged notes, and the reference to “tens,” “twenties,” and “fifties,” tended in the same direction. Also “forming connections to get rid of it” seemed to suggest the finding of agents who would take a number of notes at a time, to be passed on by ones and twos, no doubt for a consideration.
But there was the obvious difficulty that the theory did not account for the operations as a whole. The elaborate mechanism of the pit-prop industry was not needed to provide a means of carrying forged notes from France to England. They could be secreted about the person of a traveller crossing by any of the ordinary routes. Hundreds of notes could be sewn into the lining of an overcoat, thousands carried in the double bottom of a suitcase. Of course, so frequent a traveller would require a plausible reason for his journeys, but that would present no difficulty to men like those composing the syndicate. In any case, by crossing in rotation by the dozen or so well-patronised routes between England and the Continent, the continuity of the travelling could be largely hidden. Moreover, thought Merriman, why print the notes in France at all? Why not produce them in England and so save the need for importation?
On the whole there seemed but slight support for the theory and several strong arguments against it, and he felt that Madeleine must be mistaken, just as he and Hilliard had been mistaken.
===Chinese===
出租汽车载着他向波尔多飞驰而去，他满脑子只有那姑娘，再容不下别的。倒不是他在明确地想她，而是她仿佛占满了他全部的意识。他只觉麻木，整个身心为她隐隐作痛，像一种钝钝的肉体疼痛。可这疼痛里又搀着狂喜，因为虽说她回绝了他，至少承认了爱他。不可思议的念头！他心醉神迷地笑了；随即失落之感卷土重来，又阴郁地望向前方的虚空，怔怔出神。随着夜色渐深，他的思绪转向她关于辛迪加的那番话。她的假钞之说完全出乎他的意料，他寻思，她是否真的一语道破了谜底。她偷听到的那席话无疑指向那个方向。据他判断，“撒货”正是使用假钞的黑话切口，而“十”“二十”“五十”那些数目也指向同一路。至于“结上关系把货出脱”，似乎暗示物色一些经手人，一次收下若干张钞票，再一张两张地陆续用出去——无疑是有酬劳的。
但这一说法有个显而易见的难处：它解释不了整个运作。要把假钞从法国带到英国，犯不着兴师动众，搞起坑木生意这一整套繁复机关。随便走哪条寻常路线的旅客，把钞票藏在身上就能带过去。几百张钞票可以缝进大衣夹里，几千张可以藏进手提箱的夹层底。当然，跑得这样勤的旅客需要一个说得通的理由，可这对辛迪加那班人来说算不得难事。再说，英国与欧陆之间常走的路线不过十来条，轮换着走，旅行的连贯性大半就掩住了。况且，梅里曼想，何必非在法国印钞票？何不就在英国造，省去偷运入境这一道？
总起来看，支持这一说法的依据寥寥，反对的论据倒有好几条站得住。他觉得玛德琳必定是弄错了，正如他和希利亚德先后弄错一样。

===Original===
Oh! how sick of the whole business he was! He no longer cared what the syndicate was doing. He never wanted to hear of it again. He wanted Madeleine, and he wanted nothing else.
