As Merriman cycled eastward through France his interest in the affair gradually waned, and when, a fortnight later, he reached England, he had ceased to give it a serious thought.
But the image of Miss Coburn did not so quickly vanish from his imagination, and many times he regretted he had not taken an opportunity of returning to the mill to renew the acquaintanceship so unexpectedly begun.

===Chinese===
“但愿如此。”科伯恩小姐应道，随即不动声色却干脆决然地把话头转开了。
两人开始交换起关于伦敦的种种见闻。梅里曼加满了油箱，把摩托车推上公路之后，再也无法不失体面地找到借口留在她身边，心里好生怅惘。他依依不舍地道了别，约莫半个钟头后，已经踏上波尔多一家旅馆的门阶了。
当天晚上，以及后来的许多个时候，他的思绪都会回到卡车那桩事上。科伯恩小姐当时拿弹震症作解释，似乎把司机亨利的举动完全说通了；可如今梅里曼不那么有把握了。他越琢磨，越确信自己在号码牌上没有看错；也越觉得多半是司机猜到了他梅里曼注意到了什么，因而怀恨在心。在他看来，这里面藏着什么秘密，那人唯恐它泄露出去；而梅里曼也不得不暗自承认，科伯恩小姐的种种举动，处处印证着这样一个假设：她也分担着这个秘密和这份恐惧。
然而，假定他没有看错，调换一辆卡车的号码牌又能有什么了不得的名堂——这念头未免荒唐。就算真有其事，那也是区区小事；照他看来，干这事的动机也好，后果也好，必定都无足轻重。这事蹊跷归蹊跷，可谁也想不出它有什么要紧。梅里曼骑车向东横穿法国，对这件事的兴趣渐渐淡薄下去；两个星期后他抵达英国时，已经不再认真去想它了。
只是科伯恩小姐的倩影，却没有这么快从他的想象中消散。多少回，他懊悔自己没找个机会重返锯木厂，续上这段不期而遇的交情。

## IX The Second Cargo / 九、第二批货
===Original===
Merriman was awakened in the early hours of the following morning by a push on the shoulder and, opening his eyes, he was amazed to see Hilliard, dressed only in his pajamas, leaning over him. On his friend’s face was an expression of excitement and delight which made him a totally different man from the gloomy pessimist of the previous day.
“Merriman, old man,” he cried, though in repressed tones﻿—it was only a little after five﻿—“I’m frightfully sorry to stir you up, but I just couldn’t help it. I say, you and I are a nice pair of idiots!”
Merriman grunted.
“I don’t know what you’re talking about,” he murmured sleepily.
===Chinese===
第二天凌晨时分，梅里曼肩上被推了一把，从睡梦中醒来。睁开眼，他惊讶地看见希利亚德只穿着睡衣，正俯身望着自己。朋友脸上那份兴奋与欢喜，使他全然变了个人，跟前一天那个愁眉苦脸的悲观者判若两人。
“梅里曼，老兄，”他喊道，嗓音却压得很低——这才五点刚过——“把你吵醒实在对不住，可我实在憋不住了。我说，咱们俩可真是一对地道的傻瓜！”
梅里曼咕哝了一声。
“我不知道你在说些什么。”他睡意朦胧地嘟囔道。

===Original===
“Talking about?” Hilliard returned eagerly. “Why, this affair, of course! I see it now, but what I don’t see is how we missed it before. The idea struck me like a flash. Just while you’d wink I saw the whole thing!”
Merriman, now thoroughly aroused, moved with some annoyance.
“For Heaven’s sake, explain yourself,” he demanded. “What whole thing?”
“How they do it. We thought it was brandy smuggling but we couldn’t see how it was done. Well, I see now. It’s brandy smuggling right enough, and we’ll get them this time. We’ll get them, Merriman, we’ll get them yet.”
===Chinese===
“说些什么？”希利亚德急切地接话，“自然是这桩事！我现在看明白了，不明白的反倒是咱们先前怎么会错过了它。这念头像闪电一样劈中我。就一眨眼的工夫，整件事我全看清了！”
这下梅里曼彻底醒了，带着几分恼火挪了挪身子。
“看在老天的份上，把话说明白，”他要求道，“什么整件事？”
“他们是怎么干的。咱们原以为是白兰地走私，可看不出是怎么个干法。喏，现在我看见了。千真万确是白兰地走私，这一回咱们要逮住他们了。要逮住他们了，梅里曼，早晚要逮住他们。”

===Original===
Hilliard was bubbling over with excitement. He could not remain still, but began to pace up and down the room. His emotion was infectious, and Merriman began to feel his heart beat quicker as he listened.
Hilliard went on:
“We *thought* there might be brandy, in fact we couldn’t suggest anything else. But we didn’t *see* any brandy; we saw pit-props. Isn’t that right?”
“Well?” Merriman returned impatiently. “Get on. What next?”
===Chinese===
希利亚德兴奋得直往外冒。他站不定，开始在屋里踱来踱去。他的情绪有感染力，梅里曼听着听着，只觉得自己的心跳也快了起来。
希利亚德接着说：
“咱们*想*过可能有白兰地，事实上也提不出别的说法。可咱们并没*看见*白兰地；看见的是坑木。对不对？”
“那又怎样？”梅里曼不耐烦地答道，“往下说。然后呢？”

===Original===
“That’s all,” Hilliard declared with a delighted laugh. “That’s the whole thing. Don’t you see it now?”
Merriman felt his anger rising.
“Confound it all, Hilliard,” he protested. “If you haven’t anything better to do than coming round wakening﻿—”
“Oh, don’t get on your hind legs,” Hilliard interrupted with another ecstatic chuckle. “What I say is right-enough. Look here, it’s perfectly simple. We thought brandy would be unloaded! And what’s more, we both sat in that cursed barrel and watched it being done! But all we saw coming ashore was pit-props, Merriman, *pit-props*! Now don’t you see?”
===Chinese===
“没了，”希利亚德得意地一笑，宣布道，“全部就这些。你如今还不明白？”
梅里曼觉得火气直往上冒。
“真是见鬼，希利亚德，”他抗议道，“你要是没别的正经事，专会跑来吵醒人——”
“哦，别这么暴跳如雷，”希利亚德又乐不可支地笑着打断他，“我说的准没错。听着，事情再简单不过。咱们以为会卸下白兰地！而且咱们俩还坐在那只倒霉的桶里，眼睁睁看着卸货来着！可上岸的净是坑木呀，梅里曼，*坑木*！这还不明白？”

===Original===
Merriman suddenly gasped.
“Lord!” he cried breathlessly. “It was *in* the props?”
“Of course it was in the props!” Hilliard repeated triumphantly. “Hollow props; a few hollow ones full of brandy to unload in their shed, many genuine ones to sell! What do you think of that, Merriman? Got them at last, eh?”
Merriman lay still as he tried to realise what this idea involved. Hilliard, moving jerkily about the room as if he were a puppet controlled by wires, went on speaking.
===Chinese===
梅里曼猛地倒抽一口气。
“老天！”他喘着气喊道，“白兰地就*在*坑木里？”
“当然在坑木里！”希利亚德得意洋洋地重复道，“空心的坑木；几根灌满白兰地的空心货，卸进他们的货栈，大批真货照常出售！你以为如何，梅里曼？总算逮着他们了，嗯？”
梅里曼躺着不动，努力掂量这个想法意味着什么。希利亚德像个被提线操纵的木偶，一顿一顿地在屋里走来走去，嘴里说个不停。

===Original===
“I thought it out in bed before I came along. All they’d have to do would be to cut the props in half and bore them out, attaching a screwed ring to one half and a screwed socket to the other so that they’d screw together like an ordinary gas thimble. See?”
Merriman nodded.
“Then they’d get some steel things like oxygen gas cylinders to fit inside. They’d be designed of such a thickness that their weight would be right; that their weight plus the brandy would be equal to the weight of the wood bored out.”
He paused and looked at Merriman. The latter nodded again.
===Chinese===
“来之前我躺在床上就想透了。他们只消把坑木锯成两截，把芯子掏空，一截装上外螺纹箍，一截装上内螺纹套，拧起来就跟普通的煤气套管接头一个样。明白吗？”
梅里曼点头。
“然后弄些氧气瓶模样的钢筒装进去。筒壁的厚度经过设计，分量恰到好处；钢筒连同白兰地的重量，正好抵得上掏去的那部分木头。”
他顿了顿，望着梅里曼。后者又点了点头。

===Original===
“The rest would be as easy as tumbling off a log. At night Coburn and company would screw off the hollow ends, fill the cylinders with brandy, screw on the end again, and there you have your props﻿—harmless, innocent props﻿—ready for loading up on the *Girondin*. Of course, they’d have them marked. Then when they’re being unloaded that manager would get the marked ones put aside﻿—they could somehow be defective, too long or too short or too thin or too anything you like﻿—he would find some reason for separating them out﻿—and then at night he would open the things and pour out the brandy, screw them up again and﻿—there you are!”
Hilliard paused dramatically, like a conjurer who has just drawn a rabbit from a lady’s vanity bag.
“That would explain that Ferriby manager sleeping in the shed,” Merriman put in.
“So it would. I hadn’t thought of that.”
===Chinese===
“余下的事易如反掌。夜里，科伯恩一伙拧开空心的一头，把白兰地灌进钢筒，再把头子拧上，坑木便成了——无害的、清清白白的坑木——只等装上*吉伦丁号*。当然，他们会做上记号。卸货的时候，那位经理就把做了记号的拣到一边——总可以说它们有这样那样的毛病，太长啦，太短啦，太细啦，随你说什么都行——他总会找出个理由，把这几根单挑出来——然后到了夜里，他打开这些东西，把白兰地倒出来，再一一拧好——成了！”
希利亚德煞有介事地顿住，活像个刚从女士手袋里变出兔子的魔术师。
“这一下就解释了费里比那位经理为什么睡在货栈里。”梅里曼插话道。
“可不是嘛。这层我倒没想到。”

===Original===
“And,” Merriman went on, “there’d be enough genuine props carried on each trip to justify the trade.”
“Of course. A very few faked ones would do all they wanted﻿—say two or three percent. My goodness, Merriman, it’s a clever scheme; they deserve to win. But they’re not going to.” Again he laughed delightedly.
Merriman was thinking deeply. He had recovered his composure, and had begun to weigh the idea critically.
“They mightn’t empty the brandy themselves at all,” he said slowly. “What’s to prevent them running the faked props to the firm who plants the brandy?”
===Chinese===
“还有，”梅里曼接着说，“每趟运上足够数量的真坑木，这门生意也就名正言顺了。”
“当然。掺极少数假的就够他们用了——百分之二三吧。天哪，梅里曼，这计划真高明；他们都配得上赢。可他们赢不了。”他又快活地笑起来。
梅里曼陷入沉思。他已恢复镇定，开始对这个想法加以批判的掂量。
“说不定他们压根儿不亲手倒白兰地，”他慢慢地说，“何妨把假坑木径直运到窝藏白兰地的那家商号去？”

===Original===
“That’s true,” Hilliard returned. “That’s another idea. My eyes, what possibilities the notion has!”
They talked on for some moments, then Hilliard, whose first excitement was beginning to wane, went back to his room for some clothes. In a few minutes he returned full of another side of the idea.
“Let’s just work out,” he suggested, “how much you could put into a prop. Take a prop say nine inches in diameter and nine feet long. Now you can’t weaken it enough to risk its breaking if it accidentally falls. Suppose you bored a six-inch hole down its center. That would leave the sides one and half inches thick, which should be ample. What do you think?”
“Take it at that anyway,” answered Merriman.
===Chinese===
“有道理，”希利亚德答道，“这又是一条。乖乖，这念头牵出的可能性真不少！”
两人又谈了一阵，随后，最初那阵兴奋渐渐消退的希利亚德回自己房间添了些衣服。几分钟工夫他又转回来，满脑子是这个念头的另一面。
“咱们来算算，”他提议道，“一根坑木里能装多少货。就拿直径九英寸、长九英尺的坑木来说。总不能把它削弱到万一失手落地就会折断的地步。假设沿中心钻一个六英寸的孔，两壁各留一英寸半厚，应该绰绰有余。你看呢？”
“就按这个算吧。”梅里曼答道。

===Original===
“Very well. Now how long would it be? If we bore too deep a hole we may split the prop. What about two feet six inches into each end? Say a five-foot tube?”
“Take it at that,” Merriman repeated.
“How much brandy could you put into a six-inch tube, five feet long?” He calculated aloud, Merriman checking each step. “That works out at a cubic foot of brandy, six and a quarter gallons, fifty pints or four hundred glasses﻿—four hundred glasses per prop.”
He paused, looked at his friend, and resumed:
===Chinese===
“好。那么孔钻多深？钻得太深，坑木可能裂开。两头各钻进二英尺六英寸怎么样？合起来算一根五英尺长的筒子？”
“就这么定。”梅里曼又说了一遍。
“六英寸粗、五英尺长的筒子，能灌多少白兰地？”他高声计算，梅里曼逐步核对。“算下来就是一立方英尺白兰地，合六又四分之一加仑，五十品脱，也就是四百杯——一根坑木四百杯。”
他停了停，望了望朋友，又接着说：

===Original===
“A glass of brandy in France costs you sixpence; in England it costs you half-a-crown. Therefore, if you can smuggle the stuff over you make a profit of two shillings a glass. Four hundred glasses at two shillings. There’s a profit of £40 per prop, Merriman!”
Merriman whistled. He was growing more and more impressed. The longer he considered the idea, the more likely it seemed. He listened eagerly as Hilliard, once again excitedly pacing the room, resumed his calculations.
“Now you have a cargo of about seven thousand props. Suppose you assume one percent of them are faked, that would be seventy. We don’t know how many they have, of course, but one out of every hundred is surely a conservative figure. Seventy props means £2,800 profit per trip. And they have a trip every ten days﻿—say thirty trips a year to be on the safe side﻿—£84,000 a year profit! My eyes, Merriman, it would be worth running some risks for £84,000 a year!”
“Risks?” cried Merriman, now as much excited as his friend. “They’d risk hell for it! I bet, Hilliard, you’ve got it at last. £84,000 a year! But look here,”﻿—his voice changed﻿—“you have to divide it among the members.”
===Chinese===
“在法国，一杯白兰地只消你六便士；到了英国，就得半克朗。因此，只要能把货私运过来，每杯净赚两先令。四百杯，每杯两先令。一根坑木就是 40 英镑的利，梅里曼！”
梅里曼打了个呼哨。他越来越折服了。这个想法琢磨得越久，越显得可信。希利亚德再次兴奋地在屋里踱步，继续他的演算，梅里曼听得入了神。
“如今一船货约七千根坑木。假设其中百分之一是假的，那就是七十根。当然，咱们不知道他们到底掺多少，可每一百里挑一总归是保守的估计。七十根就是每趟 2,800 英镑的利。他们每十天跑一趟——往宽裕里算，一年三十趟——一年 84,000 英镑的利！乖乖，梅里曼，为了一年 84,000 英镑，担些风险也值！”
“风险？”梅里曼喊道，此刻他跟朋友一样激动，“为这个，下地狱他们都敢！我敢打赌，希利亚德，你总算逮着真相了。一年 84,000 英镑！不过听着，”——他的口气变了——“这钱得分给各位股东。”

===Original===
“That’s true, you have,” Hilliard admitted, “but even so﻿—how many are there? Beamish, Bulla, Coburn, Henri, the manager here, and the two men they spoke of, Morton and Archer﻿—that makes seven. That would give them £12,000 a year each. It’s still jolly well worthwhile.”
“Worth while? I should just say so.” Merriman lay silently pondering the idea. Presently he spoke again.
“Of course those figures of yours are only guesswork.”
“They’re only guesswork,” Hilliard agreed with a trace of impatience in his manner, “because we don’t know the size of the tubes and the number of the props, but it’s not guesswork that they can make a fortune out of smuggling in that way. We see now that the thing can be done, and how it can be done. That’s something gained anyway.”
===Chinese===
“不错，是要分，”希利亚德承认，“可即便如此——一共几个人？比米什船长、布拉、科伯恩、亨利、这边这位经理，外加他们提过的那两位，莫顿和阿彻——一共七个。每人每年摊 12,000 英镑。仍旧划得来得很。”
“划得来？那还用说。”梅里曼静静躺着，琢磨这件事。过了一会儿他又开口。
“当然，你那些数字不过是瞎猜。”
“不过是瞎猜，”希利亚德应道，神色间带着一丝不耐烦，“因为咱们不知道筒子的粗细和坑木的数目。可他们能用这种法子走私发大财，这就不是瞎猜了。如今咱们知道事情办得成，也知道它是怎么办成的。不管怎样，总算有收获了。”

===Original===
Merriman nodded and sat up in bed.
“Hand me my pipe and baccy out of that coat pocket like a good man,” he asked, continuing slowly:
“It’ll be some job, I fancy, proving it. We shall have to see first if the props are emptied at that depot, and if not we shall have to find out where they’re sent, and investigate. I seem to see a pretty long program opening out. Have you any plans?”
“Not a plan,” Hilliard declared cheerfully. “No time to make ’em yet. But we shall find a way somehow.”
===Chinese===
梅里曼点点头，从床上坐起身。
“好哥们儿，劳驾把我那件外套口袋里的烟斗和烟丝递过来。”他说道，又慢悠悠地接着讲：
“我看，要拿出证据来可不是件轻省事。咱们得先弄清坑木是不是在那座货栈倒空；若不是，还得查清它们被运到哪儿去，再着手调查。我仿佛望见一份老长的计划正在眼前铺开。你有什么打算吗？”
“半条打算也没有，”希利亚德乐呵呵地宣称，“还没工夫去想呢。不过天无绝人之路。”

===Original===
They went on discussing the matter in more detail. At first the testing of Hilliard’s new theory appeared a simple matter, but the more they thought it over the more difficult it seemed to become. For one thing there would be the investigations at the depot. Whatever unloading of the brandy was carried on there would probably be done inside the shed and at night. It would therefore be necessary to find some hiding place within the building from which the investigations could be made. This alone was an undertaking bristling with difficulties. In the first place, all the doors of the shed were locked and none of them opened without noise. How were they without keys to open the doors in the dark, silently and without leaving traces? Observations might be required during the entire ten-day cycle, and that would mean that at some time each night one of these doors would have to be opened and shut to allow the watcher to be relieved. And if the emptying of the props were done at night how were they to ensure that this operation should not coincide with the visit of the relief? And this was all presupposing that a suitable hiding place could be found inside the building in such a position that from it the operations in question could be overlooked.
Here no doubt were pretty serious obstacles, but even were they all successfully overcome it did not follow that they would have solved the problem. The faked props might be loaded up and forwarded to some other depot, and, if so, this other depot might be by no means easy to find. Further, if it were found, nocturnal observation of what went on within would then become necessary.
It seemed to the friends that all they had done up to the present would be the merest child’s play in comparison to what was now required. During the whole of that day and the next they brooded over the problem, but without avail. The more they thought about it the more hopeless it seemed. Even Hilliard’s cheery optimism was not proof against the wave of depression which swept over him.
===Chinese===
两人继续细谈这件事。起初，检验希利亚德的新理论似乎轻而易举，可想得越多，越显得棘手。头一件就是货栈里的侦察。凡是在那儿进行的白兰地卸装，多半是在库房内部、趁着夜里做。因此必须在建筑物里找一个藏身之处，好从那里进行观察。单这一桩就困难重重。首先，库房所有的门都上着锁，没有一扇开起来不出声响。没有钥匙，他们如何在黑暗中开门，既不出声，又不留痕迹？观察或许得贯穿整整十天的周期，这就意味着每天夜里总有一个时刻，得开一次门、关一次门，好换守望的班。倘若倒空坑木恰在夜间进行，他们又如何保证这步操作不与换班撞在一起？而这一切还都先得假定：建筑物内找得到一个位置合宜的藏身处，从那里能把要观察的动静尽收眼底。
这些障碍无疑相当严重，可即便全部顺利克服，也未必就等于解决了问题。假坑木可能被装上船，转运到另一处货栈；倘若如此，那另一处货栈还未必好找。再者，就算找到了，又得对里面进行的勾当实施夜间侦察。
两人都觉得，比起眼下的要求来，他们此前所做的一切不过是小孩子的把戏。那一整天加第二天，他们苦思这个问题，却毫无成效。越想越觉得无望。连希利亚德那乐天派的性子，也挡不住阵阵袭来的消沉。

===Original===
Curiously enough it was to Merriman, the plodding rather than the brilliant, that light first came. They were seated in the otherwise empty hotel lounge when he suddenly stopped smoking, sat motionless for nearly a minute, and then turned eagerly to his companion.
“I say, Hilliard,” he exclaimed. “I wonder if there mightn’t be another way out after all﻿—a scheme for making them separate the faked and the genuine props? Do you know Leatham﻿—Charlie Leatham of Ellerby, somewhere between Selby and Boughton? No? Well, he owns a group of mines in that district. He’s as decent a soul as ever breathed, and is just rolling in money. Now﻿—how would it do if we were to go to Charlie and tell him the whole thing, and ask him to approach these people to see if they would sell him a cargo of props﻿—an entire cargo. I should explain that he has a private wharf for lighters on one of those rivers up beyond Goole, but the approach is too shallow for a seagoing boat. Now, why shouldn’t he tell these people about his wharf, saying he had heard the *Girondin* was shallow in the draught, and might get up? He would then say he would take an entire cargo on condition that he could have it at his own place and so save rail carriage from Ferriby. That would put the syndicate in a hole. They couldn’t let any of the faked props out of their possession, and if they agreed to Leatham’s proposal they’d have to separate out the faked props from the genuine, and keep the faked aboard. On their way back from Leatham’s they would have to call at Ferriby to put these faked ones ashore, and if we are not utter fools we should surely be able to get hold of them then. What do you think, Hilliard?”
===Chinese===
说来奇怪，最先透进光亮的，竟是梅里曼这个论天资不算敏锐、却肯下死功夫的人。两人正坐在旅馆空荡荡的休息室里，他忽然停了烟，一动不动坐了近一分钟，随即热切地转向同伴。
“我说，希利亚德，”他扬声道，“会不会终究还有另一条出路——一个让他们自己把真假坑木分开的计策？你认识利瑟姆（Leatham）吗——埃勒比（Ellerby）的查理·利瑟姆（Charlie Leatham），在塞尔比（Selby）和博顿（Boughton）之间的什么地方？不认识？唔，他在那一带拥有一批矿井。天底下顶厚道的人，钱也多得花不完。这样行不行——咱们去找查理，把事情原原本本告诉他，请他出面跟那伙人接洽，看他们肯不肯卖他一船坑木——整整一船。我该说明一下，他在古尔上游那边的某条河上有一座私家驳船码头，只是进港水道太浅，海船进不去。那么，他何不跟这伙人提起自己的码头，就说听说*吉伦丁号*吃水浅，兴许开得进来？然后他说可以整船全要，条件是把货卸在他自家门口，省下从费里比走的铁路运费。这一来辛迪加可就进退两难了。他们不能让任何假坑木脱手，而一旦接受利瑟姆的提议，就得把假的从真的里头拣出来，把假的留在船上。从利瑟姆那儿回来的路上，他们就得在费里比靠岸，把这几根假货卸下；只要咱们不是十足的傻瓜，届时准能把它们逮个正着。你看怎么样，希利亚德？”

===Original===
Hilliard smote his thigh.
“Bravo!” he cried with enthusiasm. “I think it’s just splendid. But is there any chance your friend would take a cargo? It’s rather a large order, you know. What would it run into? Four or five thousand pounds?”
“Why shouldn’t he? He has to buy props anyway, and these are good props and they would be as cheap as any he could get elsewhere. Taking them at his own wharf would be good business. Besides, 7,000 props is not a big thing for a group of mines. There are a tremendous lot used.”
“That’s true.”
===Chinese===
希利亚德一拍大腿。
“好极了！”他热情地喊道，“我看这主意妙透了。可你那位朋友真有可能吃下一船货吗？这可不是小单子啊。得合多少钱？四五千英镑？”
“为什么不能？他反正要买坑木，这些又是上等坑木，价钱不比别处能拿到的贵。在自家码头收货更是划算买卖。再说，7,000 根坑木对一批矿井来说不算大数。矿上用的坑木海了去了。”
“这倒不假。”

===Original===
“But the syndicate may not agree,” Merriman went on. “And yet I think they will. It would look suspicious for them to refuse so good an offer.”
Hilliard nodded. Then a further idea seemed to strike him and he sat up suddenly.
“But, Merriman, old man,” he exclaimed, “you’ve forgotten one thing. If they sent a cargo of that kind they’d send only genuine props. They wouldn’t risk the others.”
But Merriman was not cast down.
===Chinese===
“不过辛迪加未必肯答应，”梅里曼接着说，“可我想他们会的。这么好的条件都回绝，反倒惹人疑心。”
希利亚德点了头。接着他似乎又想起什么，猛地坐直了身子。
“可是，梅里曼，老兄，”他喊道，“你忘了一件事。他们要是发那样一船货，发来的就全是真货。其余那些，他们不肯冒险。”
梅里曼却并不气馁。

===Original===
“I dare say you’re right,” he admitted, “but we can easily prevent that. Suppose Leatham arranges for a cargo for some indefinite date ahead, then on the day after the *Girondin* leaves France he goes to Ferriby and says some other consignment has failed him, and could they let him have the next cargo? That would meet the case, wouldn’t it?”
“By Jove, Merriman, but you’re developing the detective instinct and no mistake! I think the scheme’s worth trying anyway. How can you get in touch with your friend?”
“I’ll phone him now that we shall be over tomorrow to see him.”
Leatham was just leaving his office when Merriman’s call reached him.
===Chinese===
“就算你说得对，”他承认道，“这也好办。让利瑟姆先定下一船货，日子不定死；等*吉伦丁号*离开法国的第二天，他跑到费里比，就说原来那批货落了空，问他们下一船能不能匀给他？这一下不就堵上了吗？”
“好家伙，梅里曼，你这侦探的直觉真是越发灵光了，错不了！我看这计策值得一试。你怎么联络你那位朋友？”
“我现在就打电话给他，说咱们明天过去看他。”
电话打到时，利瑟姆正要离开办公室。

===Original===
“Delighted to see you and meet your friend,” he answered. “But couldn’t you both come over now and stay the night? You would be a perfect godsend to me, for Hilda’s in London and I have the house to myself.”
Merriman thanked him, and later on the two friends took the 6:35 train to Ellerby. Leatham’s car was waiting for them at the station, and in a few minutes they had reached the mineowner’s house.
Charles Leatham was a man of about five-and-thirty, tall, broad, and of muscular build. He had a strong, clean-shaven face, a kindly though direct manner, and there was about him a suggestion of decision and efficiency which inspired the confidence of those with whom he came in contact.
“This is very jolly,” he greeted them. “How are you, old man? Glad to meet you, Hilliard. This is better than the lonely evening I was expecting.”
===Chinese===
“欢迎你们来，也乐意见见你那位朋友，”他答道，“不过你们俩何不这就过来住一夜？那可真是天上掉下来的救星，希尔达（Hilda）去了伦敦，就我一个人守着宅子。”
梅里曼道了谢。随后两位朋友乘 6 点 35 分的火车去了埃勒比。利瑟姆的汽车已在车站候着，几分钟工夫，两人便到了这位矿主的宅邸。
查尔斯·利瑟姆（Charles Leatham）三十五岁上下，高个子，肩宽背阔，筋骨结实。他脸膛刚毅，胡子刮得干干净净，举止亲切而直率，周身透着一股果决干练的气概，凡与他打过交道的人，无不心生信赖。
“这可真妙，”他招呼道，“你好吗，老兄？幸会，希利亚德。总算强过原来料想的那份孤零零的晚上了。”

===Original===
They went into dinner presently, but it was not until the meal was over and they were stretched in basket chairs on the terrace in the cool evening air that Merriman reverted to the subject which had brought them together.
“I’m afraid,” he began, “it’s only now when I am right up against it that I realise what appalling cheek we show in coming to you like this, and when you hear what we have in our minds, I’m afraid you will think so too. As a matter of fact, we’ve accidentally got hold of information that a criminal organisation of some kind is in operation. For various reasons our hands are tied about going to the police, so we’re trying to play the detectives ourselves, and now we’re up against a difficulty we don’t see our way through. We thought if we could interest you sufficiently to induce you to join us, we might devise a scheme.”
Amazement had been growing on Leatham’s face while Merriman was speaking.
“Sounds like the *New Arabian Nights*!” he exclaimed. “You’re not by any chance pulling my leg?”
===Chinese===
不多时众人入席用饭，但直到饭毕，三人在凉爽的晚风里舒展开身子、坐上露台的藤椅，梅里曼才重新提起把他们引到一处的正题。
“我怕的是，”他开了口，“直到如今走投无路了，我才明白咱们这样找上门来，脸皮厚得有多骇人；等你听完我们的打算，恐怕你也会这么想。实情是，我们无意中得到消息，知道有个什么犯罪组织正在活动。由于种种缘故，我们不便报警，只好自己充当侦探，如今撞上一道难关，看不清路在何方。我们想，倘若能引起你足够的兴趣、请你入伙，或许能合计出一个办法来。”
梅里曼说话的当口，利瑟姆脸上的惊异之色越来越浓。
“听着像《新天方夜谭》（*New Arabian Nights*）！”他叫道，“你们该不会是在诓我吧？”

===Original===
Merriman reassured him.
“The thing’s really a bit serious,” he continued. “If what we suspect is going on, the parties concerned won’t be squeamish about the means they adopt to keep their secret. I imagine they’d have a short way with meddlers.”
Leatham’s expression of astonishment did not decrease, but “By Jove!” was all he said.
“For that reason we can only tell you about it in confidence.”
===Chinese===
梅里曼向他担保并非如此。
“这事确实有几分严重，”他接着说，“倘若我们疑心的勾当当真在进行，当事者为守住秘密，采用起手段来是不会手软的。我想，对插手多事的人，他们的处置一定干脆利落。”
利瑟姆脸上的惊愕丝毫未减，却只迸出一句：“乖乖！”
“正因为如此，我们只能私下里告诉你，务请守密。”

===Original===
Merriman paused and glanced questioningly at the other, who nodded without replying.
“It began when I was cycling from Bayonne to Bordeaux,” Merriman went on, and he told his host about his visit to the clearing, his voyage of discovery with Hilliard and what they had learned in France, their trip to Hull, the Ferriby depot and their adventures thereat, ending up by explaining their hollow pit-prop idea, and the difficulty with which they found themselves faced.
Leatham heard the story with an interest which could hardly fail to gratify its narrator. When it was finished he expressed his feelings by giving vent to a long and complicated oath. Then he asked how they thought he could help. Merriman explained. The mineowner rather gasped at first, then he laughed and slapped his thigh.
“By the Lord Harry!” he cried, “I’ll do it! As a matter of fact I want the props, but I’d do it anyway to see you through. If there’s anything at all in what you suspect it’ll make the sensation of the year.”
===Chinese===
梅里曼顿了顿，探询地望了对方一眼。对方点点头，没有作声。
“事情是从我骑车从巴约讷去波尔多那天起的头，”梅里曼接着说。他把造访林间空地、与希利亚德同船探查以及在法国的种种见闻、赫尔之行、费里比货栈和在那里的历险，一五一十讲给主人听，最后讲到他们的空心坑木设想，以及眼下共同面对的难处。
利瑟姆听这个故事听得兴致盎然，讲故事的人不能不受鼓舞。故事讲完，他抒发感受的方式，是甩出一长串花样百出的咒骂。随后他问，他们想他能帮什么忙。梅里曼说明了一番。矿主起初倒吸一口气，随即放声大笑，一巴掌拍在大腿上。
“老天在上！”他喊道，“我干了！实话说，我本来就要买坑木，可就算不买，为了帮你们成事我也干。只要你们的怀疑沾得上半分边，这案子准能轰动全年。”

===Original===
He thought for a moment, then went on:
“I shall go down to that depot at Ferriby tomorrow, have a look at the props, and broach the idea of taking a cargo. It’ll be interesting to have a chat with that manager fellow, and you may bet I’ll keep my eyes open. You two had better lie low here, and in the evening we’ll have another talk and settle what’s to be done.”
The next day the friends “lay low,” and evening saw them once more on the terrace with their host. It seemed that he had motored to Ferriby about midday. The manager had been polite and even friendly, had seemed pleased at the visit of so influential a customer, and had shown him over the entire concern without the slightest hesitation. He had appeared delighted at the prospect of disposing of a whole cargo of props, and had raised no objection to the *Girondin* unloading at Leatham’s wharf. The price was moderate, but not exceptionally so.
“I must admit,” Leatham concluded, “that everything appeared very sound and businesslike. I had a look everywhere in that shed and enclosure, and I saw nothing even remotely suspicious. The manager’s manner, too, was normal and it seems to me that either he’s a jolly good actor or you two chaps are on a wild goose chase.”
===Chinese===
他想了想，接着说：
“我明天就下费里比那座货栈去，看看坑木，把买一船货的意思挑明。跟那位经理老兄聊聊倒也有意思，你们尽管打赌，我这双眼睛绝不会闲着。你们俩最好在这儿蛰伏不动，晚上咱们再碰头，商量下一步。”
第二天，两位朋友果然“蛰伏”了一天，到晚上，他们又同主人坐上露台。原来他晌午前后开车去了费里比。那位经理客客气气，甚至称得上热络，对这么一位有分量的主顾登门显然颇为受用，毫不迟疑地陪他把整处产业看了个遍。听说有望出手整整一船坑木，他喜形于色，对*吉伦丁号*在利瑟姆的码头卸货也毫无异议。价钱公道，却也不算格外便宜。
“我必须承认，”利瑟姆最后说，“样样都显得规矩本分，一派生意人的做派。那库房和场院我四处看了，连一丝可疑的影子都没见着。经理的态度也属正常。依我看，要么他是个顶呱呱的演员，要么你们两位就是在白费劲瞎追。”

===Original===
“We may be about the hollow props,” Merriman returned, “and we may be about the brandy smuggling. But there’s no mistake at all about something being wrong. That’s certain from what Hilliard overheard.”
Leatham nodded.
“I know all that,” he said, “and when we’ve carried out this present scheme we shall know something more. Now let’s see. When does that blessed boat next leave France?”
“Thursday morning, we reckon,” Hilliard told him.
===Chinese===
“空心坑木一说，我们也许看错了，”梅里曼答道，“白兰地走私一说，也许看错了。可总有什么事不对头，这一点绝错不了。希利亚德偷听来的话就是铁证。”
利瑟姆点了点头。
“这些我都知道，”他说，“等眼下这计策办完，咱们就知道得更多了。来，算算看。那条宝贝船下回几时离开法国？”
“我们估计是星期四早上。”希利亚德告诉他。

===Original===
“Then on Friday afternoon I shall call up those people and pitch my yarn about my consignment of props having gone astray, and ask if they can send their boat direct here. How’s that?”
“Nothing could be better.”
“Then I think for the present you two had better clear out. Our connection should not be known. And don’t go near London either. That chap Morton has lost you once, but he’ll not do it a second time. Go and tramp the Peak District, or something of that kind. Then you’ll be wanted back in Hull on Saturday.”
“What’s that for?” both men exclaimed in a breath.
===Chinese===
“那么星期五下午，我就给那伙人挂电话，编一套说辞，就说原定的那批坑木误了事，问他们的船能不能直接开到这儿来。怎么样？”
“再好不过。”
“那么眼下，你们两位最好避一避。咱们之间的关系不能让人知道。伦敦也别去沾边。莫顿那家伙跟丢过你们一回，可不会再有第二回。去峰区（the Peak District）徒步走走，或者做点类似的事。星期六再回赫尔来听差。”
“那是干什么？”两人异口同声地问。

===Original===
“That blessed barrel of yours. You say the *Girondin* will leave France on Thursday night. That means she will be in the Humber on Sunday night or Monday morning. Now you reckoned she would unload here and put the faked props ashore and load up oil at Ferriby on her way out. But she mightn’t. She might go into Ferriby first. It would be the likely thing to do, in fact, for then she’d get here with nothing suspicious aboard and could unload everything. So I guess you’ll have to watch in your barrel on Sunday, and that means getting into it on Saturday night.”
The two friends swore and Leatham laughed.
“Good heavens,” Hilliard cried, “it means about four more nights of the damned thing. From Saturday night to Sunday night for the arrival; maybe until Monday night if she lies over to discharge the faked props on Monday. Then another two nights or maybe three to cover her departure. I tell you it’s a tall order.”
===Chinese===
“为你那只宝贝桶呗。你们说*吉伦丁号*星期四夜里离开法国，那就是星期天夜里或星期一早上进亨伯河（the Humber）。你们原估计她在这儿卸货，把假坑木弄上岸，出港路上再在费里比装油。可她未必。她可能先进费里比。说实在的，那样才合乎情理——如此一来，她到这儿时船上干干净净，什么可疑的东西都没有，可以一总卸完。所以我看，星期天你们就得在桶里守着，也就是说，星期六夜里就得钻进去。”
两位朋友骂了几句，利瑟姆大笑。
“老天爷，”希利亚德叫道，“那意味着在这鬼东西里再熬大约四个晚上。从星期六夜熬到星期天夜，等船到；要是她耽搁到星期一才卸假坑木，说不定得熬到星期一夜里。然后为盯她离港，还得再守两夜，兴许三夜。我告诉你，这差事可不轻省。”

===Original===
“But think of the prize,” Leatham smiled maliciously. “As a matter of fact I don’t see any other way.”
“There is no other way,” Merriman declared with decision. “We may just set our teeth and go through with it.”
After further discussion it was arranged that the friends would leave early next day for Harrogate. There Leatham would wire them on Friday the result of his negotiations about the *Girondin*. They could then return to Hull and get out their boat on Saturday should that be necessary. When about midnight they turned in, Leatham was quite as keen about the affair as his guests, and quite as anxious that their joint experiment should be crowned with success.
===Chinese===
“可想想那份赢头，”利瑟姆不怀好意地笑道，“说实话，我也想不出别的法子。”
“本来就没有别的法子，”梅里曼斩钉截铁地宣告，“咬紧牙关，熬过去就是。”
又商量了一阵，议定两位朋友次日一早动身去哈罗盖特（Harrogate）。星期五，利瑟姆把关于*吉伦丁号*交涉的结果电告他们。届时如有必要，他们星期六再回赫尔，把船取出来。将近午夜就寝时，利瑟姆对这件事的热心已不亚于两位客人，同样巴望他们共同的试验大功告成。

===Original===
The two friends spent a couple of lazy days amusing themselves in Harrogate, until towards evening on the Friday Merriman was called to the telephone.
“That’ll be Leatham,” he exclaimed. “Come on, Hilliard, and hear what he has to say.”
It was the mineowner speaking from his office.
===Chinese===
两位朋友在哈罗盖特懒散地消磨了两天，到星期五傍晚，梅里曼被叫去听电话。
“准是利瑟姆，”他扬声道，“来，希利亚德，听听他怎么说。”
打电话的正是那位矿主，从他的办公室打来。

===Original===
“I’ve just rung up our friends,” he told them, “and that business is all right. There was some delay about it at first, for Benson﻿—that’s the manager﻿—was afraid he hadn’t enough stock of props for current orders. But on looking up his records he found he could manage, so he is letting the ship come on.”
“Jolly good, Leatham.”
“The *Girondin* is expected about seven tomorrow evening. Benson then asked about a pilot. It seems their captain is a certified pilot of the Humber up to Ferriby, but he could not take the boat farther. I told him I’d lend him the man who acted for me, and what I’ve arranged is this. I shall send Angus Menzies, the master of one of my river tugs, to the wharf at Ferriby about six on Saturday evening. When the *Girondin* comes up he can go aboard and work her on here. Menzies is a good man, and I shall drop a hint that I’ve bought the whole cargo, and to keep his eyes open that nothing is put ashore that I don’t get. That’ll be a still further check.”
===Chinese===
“我刚给咱们那几位朋友挂了电话，”他告诉他们，“事情成了。起初稍耽搁了一下，因为本森（Benson）——就是那位经理——怕坑木库存不够应付手头的订单。可一查底账，发现腾挪得开，他就放船过来了。”
“好极了，利瑟姆。”
“*吉伦丁号*预计明天傍晚七点前后到。本森接着问起领航员。原来他们那位船长是持证的亨伯河领航员，航线到费里比为止，再往里就带不了船了。我告诉他，可以把我代用的那位借给他，我的安排是这样：我派安格斯·门齐斯（Angus Menzies）——我一条河用拖船的船长——星期六傍晚六点前后到费里比码头。*吉伦丁号*开上来时，他就上船，把她一路领到这儿。门齐斯是个可靠的人，我会递个话，说整船货我都买下了，叫他留神，凡我该到手的，一样也不许卸上岸。这又多一道稽察。”

===Original===
The friends expressed their satisfaction at this arrangement, and it was decided that as soon as the investigation was over all three should meet and compare results at Leatham’s house.
Next evening saw the two inquirers back at their hotel in Hull. They had instructed the owner of their hired boat to keep it in readiness for them, and about eleven o’clock, armed with the footstool and the satchel of food, they once more got on board and pulled out on to the great stream. Merriman not wishing to spend longer in the barrel than was absolutely necessary, they went ashore near Hessle and had a couple of hours’ sleep, and it was well past four when they reached the depot. The adventure was somewhat more risky than on the previous occasion, owing to the presence of a tiny arc of moon. But they carried out their plans without mishap, Merriman taking his place in the cask, and Hilliard returning to Hull with the boat.
If possible, the slow passage of the heavily weighted hours until the following evening was even more irksome to the watcher than on the first occasion. Merriman felt he would die of weariness and boredom long before anything happened, and it was only the thought that he was doing it for Madeleine Coburn that kept him from utter collapse.
===Chinese===
两位朋友对这一安排表示满意，并议定调查一结束，三人就在利瑟姆的宅子里碰头，核对各自的结果。
第二天晚上，两位探查者回到了赫尔的旅馆。他们已嘱咐租船的船主把船备好随时候用，十一点来钟，两人带上脚凳和食物袋，再次登船，划进那条大河。梅里曼不愿在桶里多待一刻非必要的光阴，他们在赫斯尔附近上了岸，睡了两个来钟头，到达货栈时早已过了四点。由于天上挂着一弯细细的月牙，这一趟比上回风险略大。但两人把计划办得毫发无损，梅里曼在桶里各就各位，希利亚德驾船回了赫尔。
倘若可以比较，从清晨挨到次日晚间那沉甸甸的时辰，挪动得比头一回更叫守桶人难熬。梅里曼觉得自己只怕等不及出事，就要先叫困倦和无聊折磨死了；支撑他不至于彻底垮掉的，只剩一个念头：这是为了玛德琳·科伯恩。

===Original===
At intervals during the morning, Benson, the manager, or one of the other men came out for a moment or two on the wharf, but no regular work went on there. During the interminable hours of the afternoon no one appeared at all, the whole place remaining silent and deserted, and it was not until nearly six that the sound of footsteps fell on Merriman’s weary ears. He heard a gruff voice saying: “Ah’m no so sairtain o’ it mesel’,” which seemed to accord with the name of Leatham’s skipper, and then came Benson’s voice raised in agreement.
The two men passed out of the shed and moved to the edge of the wharf, pursuing a desultory discussion, the drift of which Merriman could not catch. The greater part of an hour passed, when first Benson and then Menzies began to stare eastwards down the river. It seemed evident to Merriman that the *Girondin* was in sight, and he began to hope that something more interesting would happen. But the time dragged wearily for another half-hour, until he heard the bell of the engine-room telegraph and the wash of the screw. A moment later the ship appeared, drew alongside, and was berthed, all precisely as had happened before.
===Chinese===
上午时不时地，经理本森或是旁的什么人会上码头站上一两分钟，但正经的活计一件没干。下午那没完没了的几个钟头里，一个人影也不见，整处地方静悄悄、空落落。直到将近六点，脚步声才落进梅里曼疲惫的耳朵。他听见一个粗哑的嗓门说：“这事儿，俺自个儿也老不大拿得准哩。”这口音倒正衬得上利瑟姆那位船长的姓氏，紧接着便响起本森高声附和。
两人走出库房，踱到码头边上，有一搭没一搭地谈着，谈的话头偏向哪里，梅里曼听不真切。将近一个钟头过去，本森先、门齐斯后，开始朝下游东面张望。在梅里曼看来，*吉伦丁号*显然已经入眼，他开始盼着有点更有意思的事发生。可时间又磨磨蹭蹭挨了半个钟头，他才听见机舱传令钟的铃声和螺旋桨搅起的水声。片刻之后，船露面了，靠上来，泊好，一切与前次分毫不差。

===Original===
As soon as the gangway was lowered, Benson sprang aboard, and running up the ladder to the bridge, eagerly addressed Captain Beamish. Merriman could not hear what was said, but he could see the captain shaking his head and making little gestures of disapproval. He watched him go to the engine room tube and speak down it. It was evidently a call to Bulla, for almost immediately the engineer appeared and ascended to the bridge, where all three joined in a brief discussion. Finally Benson came to the side of the ship and shouted something to Menzies, who at once went on board and joined the group on the bridge. Merriman saw Benson introduce him to the others, and then apparently explain something to him. Menzies nodded as if satisfied and the conversation became general.
Merriman was considerably thrilled by this new development. He imagined that Benson while, for the benefit of Menzies, ostensibly endeavoring to make the arrangements agreed on, had in reality preceded the pilot on board in order to warn the captain of the proposal, and arrange with him some excuse for keeping the ship where she was for the night. Bulla had been sent for to acquaint him with the situation, and it was not until all three were agreed as to their story that Menzies was invited to join the conclave. To Merriman it certainly looked as if the men were going to fall into the trap which he and his friends had prepared, and he congratulated himself on having adhered to his program and hidden himself in the barrel, instead of leaving the watching to be done by Menzies, as he had been so sorely tempted to do. For it was clear to him that if any secret work was to be done Menzies would be got out of the way until it was over. Merriman was now keenly on the alert, and he watched every movement on the ship or wharf with the sharpness of a lynx. Bulla presently went below, leaving the other three chatting on the bridge, then a move was made and, the engineer reappearing, all four entered the cabin. Apparently they were having a meal, for in about an hour’s time they emerged, and bringing canvas chairs to the boat deck, sat down and began to smoke﻿—all except Bulla, who once again disappeared below. In a few moments he emerged with one of the crew, and began to superintend the coupling of the oil hose. The friends had realised the ship would have to put in for oil, but they had expected that an hour’s halt would have sufficed to fill up. But from the delay in starting and the leisurely way the operation was being conducted, it looked as if she was not proceeding that night.
===Chinese===
舷梯一放下，本森就蹿上船，顺着扶梯跑上驾驶台，急切地对比米什船长说着什么。梅里曼听不见内容，但看得到船长摇头，做着不以为然的小手势。他见他走到机舱传话管前，对着管子讲了几句。那显然是在叫布拉，因为轮机长几乎立刻现身，登上了驾驶台，三人在那里简短地商议了一阵。末了本森走到船舷边，朝门齐斯喊了句什么，门齐斯随即登船，加入了驾驶台上那一伙。梅里曼看见本森把他介绍给众人，接着又像是向他交代了些什么。门齐斯满意似的点点头，谈话便敞开了。
这一新动向令梅里曼心头发热。他想，本森当着门齐斯的面，表面上是在落实谈妥的各项安排，实际上却抢在领航员前头上了船，好把这项提议预先通报船长，并同他商定一个借口，让船在原地过夜。布拉被叫来是为了让他知情，直到三人把说词统一，这才请门齐斯加入密议。在梅里曼看来，这伙人分明正要一头栽进他和朋友们布下的陷阱。他暗自庆幸自己守着计划没变，照旧藏进了桶里，而没有把守望交给门齐斯去办——先前他可着实动过这个念头。因为他看得分明：真要做什么隐秘的活计，门齐斯准会被支开，等做完了再说。此刻梅里曼警觉万分，以山猫般的锐利，盯住船上和码头上的每一个动作。不多时布拉下了舱，留下其余三人在驾驶台上闲谈；随后有了动静，轮机长重新露面，四人一齐进了舱房。看样子是在用饭，因为约莫一个钟头后他们又出来了，搬着帆布椅到艇甲板上坐下，点起了烟——唯独布拉除外，他又一次下舱去了。片刻后他带着一名船员上来，开始督着人接输油软管。两位朋友早知道这船得添油，但原以为停上一个钟头灌满就够。可从迟迟不见开船、这桩活计办得从从容容的样子看，她当夜多半是不走了。

===Original===
In about an hour the oiling was completed, and Bulla followed his friends to the captain’s cabin, where the latter had retired when dusk began to fall. An hour later they came out, said “Good night,” and separated, Benson coming ashore, Bulla and Menzies entering cabins on the main deck, and Captain Beamish snapping off the deck light and reentering his own room.
“Now or never,” thought Merriman, as silence and darkness settled down over the wharf.
But apparently it was to be never. Once again the hours crept slowly by and not a sign of activity became apparent. Nothing moved on either ship or wharf, until about two in the morning he saw dimly in the faint moonlight the figure of Hilliard to relieve him.
===Chinese===
约莫一个钟头后，油加完了，布拉随着伙伴们进了船长舱——暮色四合时，船长已退到那里。一个钟头后，几人出来，道了声“晚安”，各自散去：本森上了岸，布拉和门齐斯进了主甲板上的舱房，比米什船长啪的一声关掉甲板灯，回了自己房间。
“成败在此一举。”梅里曼心想。寂静和黑暗笼罩了码头。
可看情形，这一举注定落空。时辰又一次缓缓爬过，不见半点动静的征兆。船上码头上什么也不动，直到凌晨两点来钟，他才在朦胧月色里隐约望见来换班的希利亚德身影。

===Original===
The exchange was rapidly effected, and Hilliard took up his watch, while his friend pulled back into Hull, and following his own precedent, went to the hotel and to bed.
The following day Merriman took an early train to Goole, returning immediately. This brought him past the depot, and he saw that the *Girondin* had left.
That night he again rowed to the wharf and relieved Hilliard. They had agreed that in spite of the extreme irksomeness of a second night in the cask it was essential to continue their watch, lest the *Girondin* should make another call on her way to sea and then discharge the faked props.
===Chinese===
交接迅速办妥，希利亚德接了守望，他的朋友划船回赫尔，循着上回的老例，进旅馆上床睡觉。
第二天，梅里曼乘早班车去古尔，随即折返。这一路正经过货栈，他看见*吉伦丁号*已经离港。
当晚他又划船去码头，换下希利亚德。两人已商定：尽管在桶里再熬一夜苦不堪言，守望却万万中断不得，生怕*吉伦丁号*出港途中再靠一次岸，把假坑木卸下去。

===Original===
The remainder of the night and the next day passed like a hideous dream. There being nothing to watch for in the first part of his vigil, Merriman tried to sleep, but without much success. The hours dragged by with an incredible deliberation, and during the next day there was but slight movement on the wharf to occupy his attention. And then just before dark he had the further annoyance of learning that his long-drawn-out misery had been unnecessary. He saw out in the river the *Girondin* passing rapidly seawards.
Their plan then had failed. He was too weary to think consecutively about it, but that much at least was clear. When Hilliard arrived some five hours later, he had fallen into a state of partial coma, and his friend had considerable trouble in rousing him to make the effort necessary to leave his hiding place with the requisite care and silence.
The next evening the two friends left Hull by a late train, and reaching Leatham’s house after dusk had fallen, were soon seated in his smoking-room with whiskies and sodas at their elbows and Corona Coronas in their mouths. All three were somewhat gloomy, and their disappointment and chagrin were very real. Leatham was the first to put their thoughts into words.
===Chinese===
那一夜剩下的时辰和第二天，像一场噩梦般挨了过去。守夜的前半程无事可盯，梅里曼试着睡觉，却睡不成多少。时辰以难以置信的从容磨蹭过去；第二天白天，码头上仅有零星动作供他留意。随后，天擦黑前，他又添了一层懊恼：得知自己这场旷日持久的活罪原是白受。他望见*吉伦丁号*正在河上，疾速驶向大海。
他们的计划就这么落空了。他累得没法把这件事连贯地想下去，但至少这一点是清楚的。约莫五个钟头后希利亚德到时，他已陷入半昏迷的状态，朋友费了好一番功夫，才唤醒他打起精神，以必要的谨慎和悄无声息离开藏身之处。
次日晚间，两位朋友乘夜班车离开赫尔，暮色落定后到了利瑟姆家，不多时便坐进他的吸烟室，手边搁着掺苏打水的威士忌，嘴里叼着科罗娜·科罗纳斯（Corona Coronas）雪茄。三人都有些蔫蔫的，失望与懊恼实实在在。利瑟姆第一个把大家的心思说出了口。

===Original===
“Well,” he said, drawing at his cigar, “I suppose we needn’t say one thing and think another. I take it our precious plan has failed?”
“That’s about the size of it,” Hilliard admitted grimly.
“Your man saw nothing?” Merriman inquired.
===Chinese===
“唉，”他吸了口雪茄，说道，“我想咱们不必嘴上一套、心里一套了。我琢磨着，咱们那条宝贝计划，是黄了吧？”
“就是这么回事。”希利亚德冷冷地承认。
“你那个人什么也没看见？”梅里曼问道。

===Original===
“He saw you,” the mineowner returned. “He’s a very dependable chap, and I thought it would be wise to give him a hint that we suspected something serious, so he kept a good watch. It seems when the ship came alongside at Ferriby, Benson told the captain not to make fast as he had to go further up the river. But the captain said he thought they had better fill up with oil first, and he sent to consult the engineer, and it was agreed that when they were in they might as well fill up as it would save a call on the outward journey. Besides, no one concerned was on for going up in the dark﻿—there are sandbanks, you know, and the navigation’s bad. They gave Menzies a starboard deck cabin﻿—that was on the wharf side﻿—and he sat watching the wharf through his porthole for the entire night. There wasn’t a thing unloaded, and there wasn’t a movement on the wharf until you two changed your watch. He saw that, and it fairly thrilled him. After that not another thing happened until the cook brought him some coffee and they got away.”
“Pretty thorough,” Hilliard commented. “It’s at least a blessing to be sure beyond a doubt nothing was unloaded.”
===Chinese===
“他看见你们了。”矿主答道，“他是个十分可靠的家伙，我想还是递个话、说我们疑心的事非同小可来得稳妥，所以他守得很紧。据说船在费里比靠拢时，本森告诉船长先别系缆，因为还得往河上游去。可船长说，不如先把油加满，他派人去问轮机长，几方议定：既然人都到了，索性把油加满，省得出港路上再停一回。再者，船上相关的谁也不肯摸黑上行——那一带有沙洲，你知道，航道难走。他们给了门齐斯一间右舷甲板舱——正对着码头那一面——他整夜坐在舱里，从舷窗盯着码头。一件货也没卸，码头上也纹丝不动，直到你们两位换班才有了动静。这一幕他看得真切，心里着实激荡了一阵。打那以后再没出事，直到厨子给他送来咖啡，船就离了岸。”
“办得挺干净利落，”希利亚德评论道，“至少有一样可算福音：确凿无疑，什么也没卸。”

===Original===
“We’re certain enough of that,” Leatham went on, “and we’re certain of something else too. I arranged to drop down on the wharf when the discharging was about finished, and I had a chat with the captain; superior chap, that. I told him I was interested in his ship, for it was the largest I have ever seen up at my wharf, and that I had been thinking of getting one something the same built. I asked him if he would let me see over her, and he was most civil and took me over the entire boat. There was no part of her we didn’t examine, and I’m prepared to swear there were no props left on board. So we may take it that whatever else they’re up to, they’re not carrying brandy in faked pit-props. Nor, so far as I can see, in anything else either.”
The three men smoked in silence for some time and then Hilliard spoke.
===Chinese===
“这一点咱们够确定了，”利瑟姆接着说，“另有一点也一样确定。我安排在卸货快完时踱到码头上去，跟船长聊了聊；是个自视甚高的家伙。我告诉他，我对他的船很感兴趣，因为那是我码头上见过的最大的船，我还说，一直琢磨着造一条大致相仿的。我问他能不能让我上船看看，他客气得很，领我把整条船走了一遍。船上没有一处我们没查到，我敢起誓，船上没剩下一根坑木。所以咱们可以认定：不管他们还搞别的什么名堂，总不是用假坑木运白兰地。而且依我看，用别的什么运也不是。”
三人闷头抽了一阵烟，随后希利亚德开了口。

===Original===
“I suppose, Leatham, you can’t think of any other theory, or suggest anything else that we should do.”
“I can’t suggest what you should do,” returned Leatham, rising to his feet and beginning to pace the room. “But I know what I should do in your place. I’d go down to Scotland Yard, tell them what I know, and then wash my hands of the whole affair.”
Hilliard sighed.
===Chinese===
“利瑟姆，我想你提不出别的说法，也想不出我们还有什么别的可干了吧。”
“你们该干什么，我提不出，”利瑟姆答道，站起身，在屋里踱起步来，“可我知道换了我会怎么干。我会直奔苏格兰场，把知道的全告诉他们，然后对这整桩事撒手不管。”
希利亚德叹了口气。

===Original===
“I’m afraid we shall have no option,” he said slowly, “but I needn’t say we should much rather learn something more definite first.”
“I dare say, but you haven’t been able to. Either these fellows are a deal too clever for you, or else you are on the wrong track altogether. And that’s what I think. I don’t believe there’s any smuggling going on there at all. It’s some other game they’re on to. I don’t know what it is, but I don’t believe it’s anything so crude as smuggling.”
Again silence fell on the little group, and then Merriman, who had for some time been lost in thought, made a sudden movement.
===Chinese===
“恐怕咱们别无选择，”他慢慢地说，“不过不必说，我们当然更愿意先摸到些更确凿的东西。”
“话是这么说，可你们没能摸到。要么这伙家伙比你们精明得多，要么你们压根儿就追错了方向。我倾向后一种。我不信那儿有什么走私。他们玩的是另一路把戏。是什么我说不上，但我不信会是走私这样粗糙的勾当。”
小小一圈人又静了下来，随后，早已出神多时的梅里曼忽然动了一下。

===Original===
“Lord!” he exclaimed, “but we have been fools over this thing! There’s another point we’ve all missed, which alone proves it couldn’t have been faked props. Here, Hilliard, this was your theory, though I don’t mean to saddle you with more imbecility than myself. But anyway, according to your theory, what happened to the props after they were unloaded?”
Hilliard stared at this outburst.
“After they were unloaded?” he repeated. “Why, returned of course for the next cargo.”
===Chinese===
“老天！”他叫道，“这件事上咱们可真够傻的！还有一层大家都漏了，单凭这一层就能证明，假坑木之说站不住。听着，希利亚德，这理论原本是你的，不过我并非想给你安的蠢名头比我自己的还重。但不管怎样，按你的理论，坑木卸下之后怎么了？”
这番抢白让希利亚德瞪大了眼。
“卸下之后？”他重复道，“嗨，当然是运回去，赶下一船用。”

===Original===
“But that’s just it,” cried Merriman. “That’s just what wasn’t done. We’ve seen that boat unloaded twice, and on neither occasion were any props loaded to go back.”
“That’s a point, certainly; yes,” Leatham interposed. “I suppose they would have to be used again and again? Each trip’s props couldn’t be destroyed after arrival, and new ones made for the next cargo?”
Hilliard shook his head reluctantly.
===Chinese===
“可问题就在这儿，”梅里曼喊道，“恰恰没有这么办。那条船卸货咱们看过两回，哪一回也没见装上一根坑木运回去。”
“这一条确实有分量；是的，”利瑟姆插话道，“我想那些坑木总得一用再用吧？总不能每趟货到了就毁掉，再新做一批赶下一船吧？”
希利亚德勉强摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No,” he declared. “Impossible. Those things would cost a lot of money. You see, no cheap scheme, say of shipping bottles into hollowed props, would do. The props would have to be thoroughly well made, so that they wouldn’t break and give the show away if accidentally dropped. They wouldn’t pay unless they were used several times over. I’m afraid Merriman’s point is sound, and we may give up the idea.”
Further discussion only strengthened this opinion, and the three men had to admit themselves at a total loss as to their next move. The only suggestion in the field was that of Leatham, to inform Scotland Yard, and that was at last approved by Hilliard as a counsel of despair.
“There’s nothing else for it that I can see,” he observed gloomily. “We’ve done our best on our own and failed, and we may let someone else have a shot now. My leave’s nearly up anyway.”
===Chinese===
“不能，”他断言，“不可能。那种东西花销很大。你想，便宜法子——比如把成瓶的货塞进挖空的坑木——行不通。坑木必须做得十分地道，才不至于偶然失手跌落便摔裂、露了馅。非得反复用上多回，否则不够本。恐怕梅里曼这一条站得住，咱们可以放弃这个想法了。”
进一步的讨论只会坐实这个判断，三人不得不承认，下一步怎么办全然没了主意。眼前剩的只有利瑟姆那一条建议——报告苏格兰场——最后被希利亚德认可，当作万不得已的一步棋。
“依我看，也别无他法了，”他郁郁地说道，“我们自己尽力了，败了，如今不妨让别人来试一手。反正我的假期也快到头了。”

===Original===
Merriman said nothing at the time, but next day, when they had taken leave of their host and were in train for King’s Cross, he reopened the subject.
“I needn’t say, Hilliard,” he began, “I’m most anxious that the police should not be brought in, and you know the reason why. If she gets into any difficulty about the affair, you understand my life’s at an end for any good it’ll do me. Let’s wait a while and think over the thing further, and perhaps we’ll see daylight before long.”
Hilliard made a gesture of impatience.
===Chinese===
梅里曼当时没说什么，可第二天，两人辞别主人、坐上开往国王十字车站的火车时，他重新提起了这个话头。
“不必说，希利亚德，”他开了头，“我最不愿的就是把警察扯进来，原因你是知道的。她要是因这桩事陷入什么麻烦，你明白，我这条命留着也没什么益处，等于完了。咱们再等一等，把事情好生掂量掂量，说不定不久就能见着亮儿。”
希利亚德做了个不耐烦的手势。

===Original===
“If you can suggest any single thing that we should do that we haven’t done, I’m ready to do it. But if you can’t, I don’t see that we’d be justified in keeping all that knowledge to ourselves for an indefinite time while we waited for an inspiration. Is not that reasonable?”
“It’s perfectly reasonable,” Merriman admitted, “and I don’t suggest we should wait indefinitely. What I propose is that we wait for a month. Give me another month, Hilliard, and I’ll be satisfied. I have an idea that something might be learned from tracing that lorry number business, and if you have to go back to work I’ll slip over by myself to Bordeaux and see what I can do. And if I fail I’ll see her, and try to get her to marry me in spite of the trouble. Wait a month, Hilliard, and by that time I shall know where I stand.”
Hilliard was extremely unwilling to agree to this proposal. Though he realised that he could not hand over to his superiors a complete case against the syndicate, he also saw that considerable kudos was still possible if he supplied information which would enable their detectives to establish one. And every day he delayed increased the chance of someone else finding the key to the riddle, and thus robbing him of his reward. Merriman realised the position, and he therefore fully appreciated the sacrifice Hilliard was risking when after a long discussion that young man gave his consent.
Two days later Hilliard was back at his office, while Merriman, after an argument with his partner not far removed from a complete break, was on his way once more to the south of France.
===Chinese===
“你要是能提出哪怕一件咱们还没做过又该做的事，我随时去干。可你若提不出，我就看不出，咱们凭什么把满腹的情报无限期攥在自己手里，干等灵光乍现。这难道不合理吗？”
“再合理不过，”梅里曼承认，“我也没说要无限期等下去。我的提议是等一个月。再给我一个月，希利亚德，我就知足。我有个想法：顺着卡车换号码那条线查下去，兴许能查出点东西；你要是得回去上班，我就独自个儿溜到波尔多去，看我能做点什么。若还是不成，我就去见她，设法求她嫁给我，管它这桩祸事。等一个月，希利亚德，到那时我就知道自己站在什么地步了。”
希利亚德极不愿意答应这个提议。他虽明白自己还交不出一份针对辛迪加的完整案卷，却也看得出，倘若他提供的情报能让他们的侦探立起一桩案子，功劳仍颇可观。而他每拖延一天，别人抢先解开谜底、从而夺走他那份奖赏的机会就大一分。梅里曼明白这层处境，因此，当那位年轻人经长久商谈终于点头应允时，他深深领会到希利亚德这是在拿什么作牺牲。
两天后，希利亚德回了办公室；梅里曼则同合伙人吵了一场——离彻底闹翻已不远——再度登上了南下法国的旅途。

## XVI The Secret of the Syndicate / 十六、辛迪加的秘密
===Original===
A night’s rest made Willis once more his own man, and next morning he found that his choking rage had evaporated, and that he was able to think calmly and collectedly over the failure of his plans.
As he reconsidered in detail the nature of the watch he had kept, he felt more than ever certain that his cordons had not been broken through. No one, he felt satisfied, could have passed unobserved between the depot and the distillery.
And in spite of this the stuff had been delivered. Archer and Benson were not bluffing to put him off the scent. They had no idea they were overheard, and therefore had no reason to say anything except the truth.
How then was the communication being made? Surely, he thought, if these people could devise a scheme, he should be able to guess it. He was not willing to admit his brain inferior to any man’s.
===Chinese===
一夜的休息使威利斯又恢复了平日的自己。第二天早晨他发觉，那股噎在喉头的怒火已经散了，他能够冷静沉着地回味这番计划的失败了。
他把自己守望布防的情形细细重想了一遍，比先前任何时候都确信：他的警戒线不曾被人突破。他笃信，谁也不可能神不知鬼不觉地在货栈与蒸馏酒厂之间往来。
然而尽管如此，货还是运过去了。阿彻和本森并不是存心虚张声势、好甩开他的追踪。他们不知道自己的谈话被人听了去，因此没有理由不说真话。
那么，这传递究竟是怎么完成的呢？他想，这伙人既然设计得出这样的方案，他理应也猜得透。他可不愿意承认，自己的脑子比谁的差。

===Original===
He lit his pipe and drew at it slowly as he turned the question over in his mind. And then a possible solution occurred to him. What about a subterranean connection? Had these men driven a tunnel?
Here undoubtedly was a possibility. To drive three hundred yards of a heading large enough for a stooping man to pass through, would be a simple matter to men who had shown the skill of these conspirators. The soil was light and sandy, and they could use without suspicion as much timber as they required to shore up their work. It was true they would have to pass under the railway, but that again was a matter of timbering.
Their greatest difficulty, he imagined, would be in the disposal of the surplus earth. He began to figure out what it would mean. The passageway could hardly be less than four feet by five, to allow for lining, and this would amount to about two yards of material to the yard run, or say six hundred or seven hundred cubic yards altogether. Could this have been absorbed in the filling of the wharf? He thought so. The wharf was a large structure, thirty yards by thirty at least and eight or nine feet high; more than two thousand cubic yards of filling would have been required for it. The disposal of the earth, therefore, would have presented no difficulty. All that came out of the tunnel could have gone into the wharf three times over.
A tunnel seemingly being a practical proposition, he turned his attention to his second problem. How could he find out whether or not it had been made?
===Chinese===
他点燃烟斗，缓缓吸着，把这个问题在心里翻来覆去地掂量。忽然间，一个可能的答案浮上心头：会不会有一条地下通路？这伙人莫非挖了一条隧道？
这无疑是一种可能。对已经亮出这等身手的这伙同谋来说，开一条三百码长、够一个猫着腰的人通过的坑道，不过是小事一桩。土质疏松多沙，支护工程要用多少木料，他们尽可随意取用，不致惹人起疑。诚然，隧道得从铁路底下穿过去，可那也不过是多加支护的事。
他想，他们最大的难处，该在处置挖出来的余土。他开始盘算这其中的分量。那条通道算上衬砌，断面怎么也小不过四英尺乘五英尺，这样每掘进一码约出两码土，统共算下来，大约六七百立方码。这些土，能不能消化在码头的填筑体里？他认为是能的。码头是座大构筑物，至少三十码见方、高八九英尺，填筑它需要的土就不止两千立方码。因此，余土的处置根本不成为难处。隧道里挖出的土，往码头里填三遍都绰绰有余。
隧道既然看来是个行得通的设想，他便把注意力转到第二个问题上：怎样才能查明这条隧道究竟挖没挖？

===Original===
Obviously only from examination at one or other end. If it existed it must connect with cellars at the depot and the distillery. And of these there could be no question of which he ought to search. The depot was not only smaller and more compact, but it was deserted at intervals. If he could not succeed at the syndicate’s enclosure he would have no chance at the larger building.
It was true he had already searched it without result, but he was not then specially looking for a cellar, and with a more definite objective he might have better luck. He decided that if Benson went up to Hull that night he would have another try.
He took an afternoon train to Ferriby, and walking back towards the depot, took cover in the same place that he had previously used. There, sheltered by a hedge, he watched for the manager’s appearance.
The weather had, from the inspector’s point of view, changed for the worse. The sunny days had gone, and the sky was overladen with clouds. A cold wind blew in gustily from the southeast, bringing a damp fog which threatened every minute to turn to rain, and flecking the lead-coloured waters of the estuary with spots of white. Willis shivered and drew up his collar higher round his ears as he crouched behind the wet bushes.
===Chinese===
显然，只能从这一头或那一头的查验入手。隧道若真存在，必定连着货栈和蒸馏酒厂两处的地窖。而这两处之中该搜哪一处，是毫无疑义的：货栈不仅更小更紧凑，而且时常空无一人。若在辛迪加的围场里都不得手，到了那座更大的厂房里就更没有指望了。
不错，他先前已经搜过一回，一无所获，但那时他并不是专门找地窖；这回目标更明确，运气或许会好些。他打定主意：倘若当晚本森进城去赫尔，他就再试一回。
他乘下午的火车到了费里比，朝货栈方向往回走，在上次用过的老地方隐蔽下来。那儿有一道树篱遮挡，他守候着经理露面。
在探长看来，天气是变糟了。晴朗的日子已经过去，天上阴云沉沉。一阵阵冷风从东南方刮来，携着湿漉漉的雾气，眼看随时要化作雨，又把铅灰色的河口水面点出斑斑白痕。威利斯缩在湿透的灌木丛后打了个寒噤，把衣领往耳朵边又拢高了些。

===Original===
“Confound it,” he thought, “when I get into that shed I shall be dripping water all over the floor.”
But he remained at his post, and in due course he was rewarded by seeing Benson appear at the door in the fence, and after locking it behind him, start off down the railway towards Ferriby.
As before, Willis waited until the manager had got clear away, then slipping across the line, he produced his bent wire, opened the door, and five minutes later stood once more in the office.
From the nature of the case it seemed clear that the entrance to the cellar, if one existed, would be hidden. It was therefore for secret doors or moving panels that he must look.
===Chinese===
“该死，”他想，“等我进了那间棚屋，非把水滴得满地都是不可。”
但他仍旧守在岗位上，到时候便得了报偿：本森出现在栅栏门里，反手落了锁，沿着铁路朝费里比方向去了。
同前次一样，威利斯直等到经理走得没了影儿，才闪身越过铁轨，掏出那根弯铁丝开了门，五分钟后又站到了办公室里。
从事情的性质看，地窖的入口——倘若真有一个——必定是藏着的。因此，他要找的就是暗门或活动的镶板。

===Original===
He began by ascertaining the thickness of all the walls, noting the size of the rooms so as to calculate those he could not measure directly. He soon found that no wall was more than six inches thick, and none could therefore contain a concealed opening.
This narrowed his search. The exit from the building could only be through a trapdoor in the floor.
Accordingly he set to work in the office, crawling torch in hand along the boards, scrutinizing the joints between them for any that were not closed with dust, feeling for any that might be loose. But all to no purpose. The boards ran in one length across the floor and were obviously firmly nailed down on fixed joists.
He went to the bedroom, rolling aside the mats which covered the floor and moving the furniture back and forwards. But here he had no better result.
===Chinese===
他先查明所有墙壁的厚度，把各间屋子的大小记下来，好推算那些没法直接丈量的墙。他很快发现，没有一堵墙厚过六英寸，因此哪一堵也容不下一个暗口。
这一下，搜寻的范围缩小了：出口只可能在地坪的一道活板门里。
于是他在办公室里动手，一手握手电，沿地板匍匐爬行，细看板与板之间的拼缝，看哪一条缝不是被积尘封着的，又用手摸有没有哪块板是松的。但全是白费功夫。地板条一顺通铺到头，显然都牢牢钉死在固定的搁栅上。
他又进了卧室，把铺地的席子一一卷开，把家具来回挪动。可在这里他同样一无所获。

===Original===
The remainder of the shed was floored with concrete, and a less meticulous examination was sufficient to show that the surface was unbroken. Nor was there anything either on the wharf itself or in the enclosure behind the shed which could form a cover to a flight of steps.
Sorely disappointed, Willis returned once more to the office, and sitting down, went over once again in his mind what he had done, trying to think if there was a point on the whole area of the depot which he had overlooked. He could recall none except the space beneath a large wardrobe in the next room which, owing to its obvious weight, he had not moved.
“I suppose I had better make sure,” he said to himself, though he did not believe so massive a piece of furniture could have been pulled backwards and forwards without leaving scratches on the floor.
He returned to the bedroom. The wardrobe was divided into two portions, a single deep drawer along the bottom, and above it a kind of large cupboard with a central door. He seized its end. It was certainly very heavy; in fact, he found himself unable to move it.
===Chinese===
棚屋其余部分的地面浇的是混凝土，无须那么仔细，一看便知地面完好无缺。码头本身也好，棚屋后面的围场也好，也没有一样东西能遮住一段台阶。
威利斯大失所望，又回到办公室，坐下来把自己做过的事在心里重新过了一遍，想想货栈整个地面上有没有一处被他漏看了。他想来想去只想起一处：隔壁房间那只大衣柜底下的地方——那柜子一看便知沉得很，他没有搬动过。
“我看还是查实一下的好，”他自言自语道，尽管他并不相信，这么一件笨重的家具能被人拖来拖去而不在地板上留下划痕。
他回到卧室。那衣柜分成上下两部分：底下是通长的一只深抽屉，上面是一种带中开门的大柜。他抓住柜子一端用力搬。柜子确实沉得很；说实在的，他发现自己根本搬不动。

===Original===
He picked up his torch and examined the wooden base. And then his interest grew, for he found it was strongly stitch-nailed to the floor.
Considerably mystified, he tried to open the door. It was locked, and though with his wire he eventually shot back the bolt, the trouble he had, proved that the lock was one of first quality. Indeed, it was not a cupboard lock screwed to the inside of the door as might have been expected, but a small-sized mortice lock hidden in the thickness of the wood, and the keyhole came through to the inside; just the same arrangement as is usual in internal house doors.
The inside of the wardrobe revealed nothing of interest. Two coats and waistcoats, a sweater, and some other clothes were hanging from hooks at the back. Otherwise the space was empty.
“Why,” he wondered as he stood staring in, “should it be necessary to lock up clothes like these?”
===Chinese===
他拿起手电，查看衣柜的木底座。这一看他的兴致就上来了：那底座竟用成对斜穿的铁钉，牢牢地钉死在地板上。
他心里好生纳闷，试着去开柜门。门锁着；虽说他终于用铁丝拨开了门闩，可费的这番周折，恰恰证明那是一把头等好锁。而且，这不是人们意料之中那种用螺丝拧在门板内侧的柜锁，而是一把藏在木料厚度里的小号插锁，锁眼一直通到柜内一侧——同寻常住房内室房门的装法一模一样。
衣柜里面看不出什么名堂。两套外衣带马甲、一件毛衣，还有几件别的衣裳，挂在柜子后壁的挂钩上。除此之外，柜里空空如也。
“怪事，”他站在那儿往柜里瞅着，纳闷道，“这样的衣裳，有什么必要锁起来？”

===Original===
His eyes turned to the drawer below, and he seized the handles and gave a sharp pull. The drawer was evidently locked. Once again he produced his wire, but for the first time it failed him. He flashed a beam from his lamp into the hole, and then he saw the reason.
The hole was a dummy. It entered the wood but did not go through it. It was not connected to a lock.
He passed the light round the edges of the drawer. If there was no lock to fasten it why had he been unable to open it? He took out his penknife and tried to push the blade into the surrounding space. It would not penetrate, and he saw that there was no space, but merely a cut half an inch deep in the wood. There was no drawer. What seemed a drawer was merely a blind panel.
Inspector Willis grew more and more interested. He could not see why all that space should be wasted, as it was clear from the way in which the wardrobe was finished that economy in construction had not been the motive.
===Chinese===
他的目光转向下面那只抽屉。他抓住拉手猛地一拽。抽屉显然锁着。他又掏出那根铁丝，可这一回，它头一次不管用了。他把手电的光射进锁孔，登时看清了缘故。
那孔是唬人的假孔。它钻进木头，却没有钻透，背后也没有连着什么锁。
他把光沿着抽屉四边照了一遭。既然没有锁扣住它，方才怎么拉不开？他掏出铅笔刀，想把刀刃塞进周围的缝里。刀刃塞不进去，他这才看出，那里根本没有缝，只是木头上一道半英寸深的刻槽。根本没有什么抽屉。看着像抽屉的，只是一块装样子的板子。
威利斯探长的兴致越来越高。他不明白，那一整块空间为什么要白白荒着，因为单看这口衣柜完工的做工，就明白省工省料绝不是它的用意。

===Original===
Once again he opened the door of the upper portion, and putting his head inside passed the beam of the lamp over the floor. This time he gave a little snort of triumph. The floor did not fit tight to the sides. All round was a space of some eighth of an inch.
“The trapdoor at last,” he muttered, as he began to feel about for some hidden spring. At last, pressing down on one end of the floor, he found that it sank and the other end rose in the air, revealing a square of inky blackness out of which poured a stream of cold, damp air, and through which he could hear, with the echoing sound peculiar to vaults, the splashing and churning of the sea.
His torch revealed a flight of steps leading down into the darkness. Having examined the pivoted floor to make sure there was no secret catch which could fasten and imprison him below, he stepped on to the ladder and began to descend. Then the significance of the mortice lock in the wardrobe door occurred to him, and he stopped, drew the door to behind him, and with his wire locked it. Descending farther he allowed the floor to drop gently into place above his head, thus leaving no trace of his passage.
He had by this time reached the ground, and he stood flashing his torch about on his surroundings. He was in a cellar, so low in the roof that except immediately beneath the stairs he could not stand upright. It was square, some twelve feet either way, and from it issued two passages, one apparently running down under the wharf, the other at right angles and some two feet lower in level, leading as if towards the distillery. Down the center of this latter ran a tiny tramway of about a foot gauge, on which stood three kegs on four-wheeled frames. In the upper side of each keg was fixed a tun-dish, to the under side a stopcock. Two insulated wires came down through the ceiling below the cupboard in which the telephone was installed, and ran down the tunnel towards the distillery.
===Chinese===
他又一次打开上半部的柜门，把头探进去，将灯光扫过柜底。这一回，他得意地轻轻哼了一声。柜底与四壁并不严丝合缝，四边有一道约莫八分之一英寸宽的缝。
“活板门可算找着了，”他喃喃道，一面伸手摸索暗藏的弹簧。终于，他把柜底的一端往下一按，那一端沉了下去，另一端翘到半空，露出一方墨黑的洞口：一股阴冷潮湿的气流从里头涌出来，他还听见洞里传出大海拍溅翻搅的声音，带着地窖特有的回响。
手电照出一道台阶，通向下面的黑暗。他先查验了那块枢轴地板，确认没有什么暗闩能把它扣死、把他困在底下，这才踏上阶梯往下走。走到半路，他忽然醒悟了衣柜门上那把插锁的深意，便停下脚，回身把柜门掩上，用铁丝从里面锁好。再往下走几级，他让那块地板轻轻落回头顶原位——这样，他下来的痕迹便一点不剩了。
这时他已下到底，站在那儿用手电四下里照看。他置身在一间地窖里，窖顶低矮，除台阶正下方那一小片，他哪儿也直不起腰。地窖四四方方，每边约十二英尺，窖里开出两条通道：一条看样子往码头底下延伸；另一条与它成直角，地面约低两英尺，像是通向蒸馏酒厂。后一条通道正中铺着一道轨距约一英尺的小铁轨，轨上停着三只小桶，各自安在四轮车架上。每只桶的桶口装着一只漏斗，桶底装着一只旋塞。两根绝缘电线从安着电话的那口柜子底下的窖顶穿下来，顺着隧道伸向蒸馏酒厂。

===Original===
The walls and ceiling of both cellar and passages were supported by pit-props, discoloured by the damp and marked by stains of earthy water which had oozed from the spaces between. They glistened with moisture, but the air, though cold and damp, was fresh. That and the noise of the waves which reverberated along the passage under the wharf seemed to show that there was an open connection to the river.
The cellar was empty except for a large wooden tun or cask which reached almost to the ceiling, and a gunmetal hand pump. Pipes led from the latter, one to the tun, the other along the passage under the wharf. On the side of the tun and connected to it at top and bottom was a vertical glass tube protected by a wooden casing, evidently a gauge, as beside it was a scale headed “gallons,” and reading from 0 at the bottom to 2,000 at the top. A dark-coloured liquid filled the tube up to the figure 1,250. There was a wooden spigot tap in the side of the tun at floor level, and the tramline ran beneath this so that the wheeled kegs could be pushed below it and filled.
The inspector gazed with an expression of almost awe on his face.
“Lord!” he muttered. “Is it brandy after all?”
===Chinese===
地窖和两条通道的墙与顶都用坑木支护着，木料被潮气浸得变了色，一道道泥水的渍痕，是从坑木缝里渗漏出来的。木头湿津津地发亮，好在空气虽阴冷潮湿，倒还新鲜。这一点，加上海浪沿码头底下那条通道轰轰回响的声音，似乎表明此处有一条同河面相通的敞口。
地窖里空空的，只有一口几乎顶到窖顶的大木桶和一台炮铜摇泵。泵上接着管子，一根通向大桶，另一根沿码头底下的通道伸过去。大桶侧面竖着一根玻璃管，上下两头与桶身连通，外面罩着木护套，显然是一支量液计，因为管旁立着一柄标尺，上头写着“加仑”，刻度自底部的 0 直读到顶部的 2,000。管里一种深色的液体，正充到刻度 1,250 处。桶身侧面贴近地面处有一只木制放酒塞，铁轨恰从塞下经过，好把带轮的小桶推到塞下灌酒。
探长凝望着，脸上几乎是一副肃然起敬的神情。
“天哪！”他喃喃道。“弄了半天，竟是白兰地？”

===Original===
He stooped and smelled the wooden tap, and the last doubt was removed from his mind.
He gave vent to a comprehensive oath. Right enough it was hard luck! Here he had been hoping to bring off a forged note coup which would have made his name, and the affair was a job for the Customs Department after all! Of course a pretty substantial reward would be due to him for his discovery, and there was his murder case all quite satisfactory, but forged notes were more in his line, and he felt cheated out of his due.
But now that he was so far he might as well learn all he could. The more complete the case he gave in, the larger the reward. Moreover, his own curiosity was keenly aroused.
The cellar being empty save for the tun, the pump, and the small tramway and trucks, he turned, and flashing his light before him, walked slowly along the passage down which ran the pipe. He was, he felt sure, passing under the wharf and heading towards the river.
===Chinese===
他俯身嗅了嗅那只木塞，心里最后一丝疑云散尽了。
他痛痛快快骂出一句包罗万象的粗话。说实在的，这可真是背运！他一直指望破一桩假钞大案，好就此扬名，到头来这案子竟是替海关总署办的差事！当然，凭这一发现，一笔相当可观的赏金是少不了他的，他手上那桩凶案也都办得妥妥帖帖；可假钞才是他的本行，他觉得自己应得的东西被人抢了去。
不过既然到了这一步，索性把能查的都查个明白：呈报上去的案情越齐全，赏金就越丰厚。况且，他自己的好奇心也被大大地勾了起来。
地窖里除了大桶、摇泵和那条带轨道车的小铁轨，再无别物，他便转过身，把手电照在身前，顺着那根管子延伸的方向，沿通道缓缓走去。他确信，自己正走在码头底下，朝河的方向去。

===Original===
Some sixty feet past the pump the floor of the passage came to an abrupt end, falling vertically as by an enormous step to churning waters of the river some six feet below. At first in the semidarkness Willis thought he had reached the front of the wharf, but he soon saw he was still in the cellar. The roof ran on at the same level for some twenty feet farther, and the side walls, here about five feet apart, went straight down from it into the water. Across the end was a wall, sloping outwards at the bottom and made of horizontal pit-props separated by spaces of two or three inches. Willis immediately realised that these props must be those placed behind the inner or raking row of piles which supported the front of the wharf.
Along one side wall for its whole length was nailed a series of horizontal laths twelve inches apart. What their purpose was he did not know, but he saw that they made a ladder twenty feet wide, by which a man could work his way from the passage to the end wall and reach the water at any height of the tide.
Above this ladder was an object which at first puzzled the inspector, then as he realised its object, it became highly illuminating. On a couple of brackets secured to the wall lay a pipe of thin steel covered with thick black baize, and some sixteen feet long by an inch in diameter. Through it ran the light copper pipe which was connected at its other end to the pump. At the end of the passage this pipe had several joints like those of a gas bracket, and was folded on itself concertina-wise.
The inspector stepped on to the ladder and worked his way across it to the other end of the steel pipe, close by the end wall. The copper pipe protruded and ended in a fitting like the half of a union. As Willis gazed he suddenly grasped its significance.
===Chinese===
过了摇泵约莫六十英尺，通道的地面戛然而止，像一级其大无比的台阶，垂直落到约六英尺之下翻腾的河水里。起初在半明半暗里，威利斯以为自己到了码头前沿，但很快便看出他仍在地窖之中：窖顶在同一高度上又往前伸出约二十英尺，两侧的墙在这里相距约五英尺，从窖顶直插进水里。尽头横着一道墙，底部向外张开，由一根根水平坑木拼成，坑木之间留着两三英寸的空当。威利斯立刻醒悟：这些坑木，必定就是打在支撑码头前沿的桩子里靠里那列斜桩后面的坑木。
一面侧墙上，从头到尾钉着一道道水平板条，每道相隔十二英寸。它们的用途何在，他不得而知，但他看出，这些板条凑成了一架二十英尺宽的梯子，人可以攀着它从通道口一路横挪到尽头那道墙，无论潮水涨落到什么高度，都够得着水面。
这架“梯子”上方有一样东西，起初叫探长摸不着头脑，待他悟出它的用途，心里一下子豁然透亮。墙上装着两只托架，架上搁着一根薄钢管，外包厚厚的黑呢，约十六英尺长、一英寸粗。管里穿着那根细铜管，铜管另一头连着摇泵。这根铜管在通道尽头带着几个关节，样子同煤气壁灯架上的关节相仿，能像手风琴似的折拢起来。
探长踏上板条梯，横着挪到钢管的另一头，紧挨着尽头的墙。铜管从钢管里探出来，末端是一个接头，像半个联管节。威利斯凝视着，忽然领会了它的意义。

===Original===
The side of the *Girondin*, he thought, would lie not more than ten feet from where he was standing. If at night someone from within the cellar were to push the end of the steel tube out through one of the spaces between the horizontal timbers of the end wall, it could be inserted into a porthole, supposing one were just opposite. The concertina joints would make it flexible and allow it to extend, and the baize covering would prevent its being heard should it inadvertently strike the side of the ship. The union on the copper tube could then be fixed to some receptacle on board, the brandy being pumped from the ship to the tun.
And no outsider could possibly be any the wiser! Given a dark night and careful operators, the whole thing would be carried out invisibly and in absolute silence.
Now Willis saw the object of the peculiar construction of the front of the wharf. It was necessary to have two lines of piles, so that the deck between might overshadow and screen from view the openings between the horizontal beams at the front of the cellar. He stood marvelling at the ingenuity of the plan. No wonder Hilliard and Merriman had been baffled.
But if he were to finish his investigations, he must no longer delay. He worked back across the side of the cellar, regained the passage, and returned to the pump-room. Then turning into the other passage, he began to walk as quickly as possible along it.
===Chinese===
他想，*吉伦丁号*的船舷，离他站的地方不会超过十英尺。夜里若有人从地窖里把钢管的一端，从尽头那道墙水平坑木的空当里推出去，而正对面恰好有一扇舷窗，管子便可以插进窗里。手风琴式的关节使它柔韧可伸，黑呢包层又保得它万一失手磕上船舷也无声无息。然后，把铜管头上的联管节接到船上的什么容器上，白兰地便从船里泵进大桶。
而任何一个外人，休想看出半点端倪！只要夜色够黑、动手的人够细心，整套动作便做得无影无形，无声无息。
至此，威利斯才明白码头前沿为什么造得那般奇特。非有内外两列桩不可，这样夹在当中的那层甲板才能遮出一片阴影，把地窖前沿水平坑木间的那些开口挡在人的视线之外。他站在那里，惊叹这套设计的巧思。难怪当初希利亚德和梅里曼一头雾水。
但要查个水落石出，就再不能耽搁了。他顺着地窖一侧的板条梯挪了回去，回到通道里，折返泵房。随后拐进另一条通道，尽快朝里走去。

===Original===
The tunnel was barely four feet high by three wide, and he found progress very tiring. After a slight curve at the mouth it ran straight and almost dead level. Its construction was the same as that of the cellar, longitudinal timber lining supported behind verticals and lintels spaced about six feet apart. When he had gone about two hundred yards it curved sharply to the left, ran heavily timbered for some thirty yards in the new direction, and then swung round to the right again.
“I suppose the railway crosses here,” Willis thought, as he passed painfully round the bends.
The sweat stood in drops on his forehead when he reached the end, and he breathed a sigh of relief as he realised he could once more stand upright and stretch his cramped back. He found himself in another cellar, this time about six feet by twelve. The tramway ran along it, stopping at the end wall. The place was otherwise empty, save for a wooden grating or tun-dish with a hinged lid which was fixed between the rails near the entrance. The telephone wires, which had followed the tunnel all the way, here vanished into the roof.
Willis concluded he must be standing beneath some part of the distillery, and a very little thought was required to make clear to him the raison d’être of what he saw. He pictured the kegs being pushed under the tap of the large tun in the pump-room and filled with brandy pumped in from the *Girondin*. In imagination he saw Benson pushing his loaded trucks through the tunnel﻿—a much easier thing to do than to walk without something to step over﻿—stopping them one by one over the grating and emptying the contents therein. No doubt that grating was connected to some vat or tun buried still deeper beneath the distillery, in which the brandy mingled with the other brandy brought there by more legitimate means, and which was sold without documentary evidence of its surprising increase in bulk.
===Chinese===
隧道高不足四英尺、宽不足三英尺，他发觉这一路走得十分吃力。洞口微微一弯之后，便笔直向前，几乎全然水平。构筑法子同地窖一样：纵向的木衬板，背后由立柱和横楣顶住，柱楣每隔约六英尺一架。走出约二百码，隧道猛地向左一折，朝新方向密密地支护着延伸了约三十码，又向右拐了回去。
“想必铁路是从这儿穿过去的，”威利斯艰难地绕过那几道弯，心里想。
走到头时，他额上已沁出成串的汗珠；发觉又能直起身、舒展一下蜷僵的腰背，他长出了一口气。他发现自己置身在又一间地窖里，这间约六英尺乘十二英尺。轻便铁轨顺着窖底铺过去，到尽头的墙前为止。窖中别无他物，只有一副带铰链盖的木格栅——也可以说是一只大漏斗——嵌在入口处的两条轨条之间。那两根一路跟着隧道过来的电话线，到这里便钻进窖顶不见了。
威利斯断定，自己必定站在蒸馏酒厂底下的某处；而眼前这些东西的用途，他只消稍稍一想便明白了。他想象那些小桶被推到泵房大桶的木塞底下，灌进从*吉伦丁号*泵来的白兰地。他想象本森推着满载的小车穿过隧道——这可比空着身子、连个可供跨越的东西都没有容易得多——把车一辆辆停在格栅上，将桶里的东西尽数倒进去。毫无疑问，那格栅下面连着更深埋在蒸馏酒厂底下的什么大池或大桶；白兰地在那里同以更正当的名目运来的白兰地汇作一流，这一池酒的数量惊人地见涨，卖出去时却拿不出任何单据，来交代这凭空多出来的分量。

===Original===
It was probable, thought Willis, that some secret door must connect the chamber in which he stood with the distillery, but a careful search revealed no trace of any opening, and he was forced to the conclusion that none existed. Accordingly, he turned and began to retrace his steps through the tunnel.
The walk back seemed even longer and more irksome than his first transit, and he stopped here and there and knelt down in order to straighten his aching back. As he advanced, the booming sound of the waves, which had died down to a faint murmur at the distillery, grew louder and louder. At last he reached the pump-cellar, and was just about to step out of the tunnel when his eye caught the flicker of a light at the top of the stepladder. Someone was coming down!
Willis instantly snapped off his own light, and for the fraction of a second he stood transfixed, while his heart thumped and his hand slid round to his revolver pocket. Breathlessly he watched a pair of legs step on to the ladder and begin to descend the steps.
Like a flash he realised what he must do. If this was Benson coming to “take up stuff,” to remain in the tunnel meant certain discovery. But if only he could reach the passage under the wharf he might be safe. There was nothing to bring Benson into it.
===Chinese===
威利斯心想，他站的这间窖室，多半有一扇暗门通向蒸馏酒厂；可细细搜寻之下，任何开口的痕迹也没找到，他只得断定暗门并不存在。于是他转过身，沿着隧道往回走。
回程似乎比来时更漫长、更难熬，他走走停停，不时跪直了身子，好舒一舒酸痛的腰背。越往前走，那股在蒸馏酒厂一端已低弱成隐隐絮语的涛声，便越来越响。终于他到了泵房地窖，正要迈出隧道，一眼瞥见梯子顶上有灯光一闪。有人下来了！
威利斯立时掐灭了自己的手电，有一刹那僵在原地，心口怦怦乱跳，手已悄悄摸向揣左轮枪的衣袋。他屏住呼吸，看着一双腿踏上梯子，一级一级地往下走。
电光石火间，他明白了自己该怎么办。若来的是本森，下来“取货”，那么留在隧道里就必被发觉无疑。可只要来得及赶进码头底下那条通道，他兴许就安全了：没有什么会把本森引到那儿去。

===Original===
But to cross the cellar he must pass within two feet of the ladder, and the man was halfway down. For a moment it looked quite hopeless, then unexpectedly he got his chance. The man stopped to lock the wardrobe door. When he had finished, Willis was already across the cellar and hurrying down the other passage. Fortunately the noise of the waves drowned all other sounds.
By the time the unknown had reached the bottom of the ladder, Willis had stepped on to the cross laths and was descending by them. In a moment he was below the passage level. He intended, should the other approach, to hide beneath the water in the hope that in the darkness his head would not be seen.
But the light remained in the cellar, and Willis raised himself and cautiously peeped down the passage. Then he began to congratulate himself on what he had just been considering his misfortune. For, watching there in the darkness, he saw Benson carry out the very operations he had imagined were performed. The manager wheeled the kegs one by one beneath the great barrel, filled them from the tap, and then, setting his lamp on the last of the three, pushed them before him down the tunnel towards the distillery.
Inspector Willis waited until he judged the other would be out of sight, then left his hiding-place and cautiously returned to the pump-room. The gauge now showed 1,125 gallons, and he noted that 125 gallons was put up per trip. He rapidly ascended the steps, passed out through the wardrobe, and regained the bedroom. A few minutes later he was once more out on the railway.
===Chinese===
可要穿过地窖，他就得打离梯子不到两英尺的地方经过，而那人已下到半途。一时间这眼看毫无指望，不想机会竟意外地来了：那人停下来锁衣柜门。等他锁好，威利斯早已穿过地窖，正匆匆奔下另一条通道。幸而涛声大作，把其余一切声响都盖没了。
等那位不速之客下到梯底，威利斯已踏上下面的横板条，攀着它们往下溜。转眼间他已落到通道地面以下。他打定主意：万一那人走过来，就潜进水底，指望黑暗里人家瞧不见他这颗脑袋。
但灯光留在了地窖里。威利斯直起身，小心地朝通道里张望。这一来，他倒开始庆幸起方才还自认是祸事的那件事了。因为他在黑暗里守望着，亲眼看见本森干的，正是他先前凭想象推演过的那套工序：经理把小桶一只只推到大桶底下，从木塞灌满，然后把灯搁在三只桶的末一只上，推着它们沿隧道往蒸馏酒厂去了。
威利斯探长估摸着那人已走得望不见了，便离开藏身之处，小心地回到泵房。量液计此刻显示 1,125 加仑；他记下，每趟装运 125 加仑。他快步登上台阶，从衣柜里钻出去，回到卧室。几分钟之后，他又站到了外面的铁路上。

===Original===
He had glanced at his watch in the building and found that it was but little after ten. Benson must therefore have returned by an earlier train than usual. Again the inspector congratulated himself that events had turned out as they had, for though he would have had no fear of his personal safety had he been seen, premature discovery might have allowed the other members of the gang to escape.
The last train for Hull having left, he started to walk the six miles to the city. The weather had still further changed for the worse, and now half a gale of wind whirled round him in a pandemonium of sound and blew blinding squalls of rain into his eyes. In a few moments he was soaked to the skin, and the buffeting of the wind made his progress slow. But he struggled on, too well pleased by the success of his evening’s work to mind the discomfort.
And as he considered the affair on the following morning he felt even more satisfied. He had indeed done well! Not only had he completed what he set out to do﻿—to discover the murderer of Coburn﻿—but he had accomplished vastly more. He had brought to light one of the greatest smuggling conspiracies of modern times. It was true he had not followed up and completed the case against the syndicate, but this was not his business. Smuggling was not dealt with by Scotland Yard. It was a matter for the Customs Department. But if only it had been forged notes! He heaved a sigh as he thought of the kudos which might have been his.
But when he had gone so far, he thought he might as well make certain that the brandy was discharged as he imagined. He calculated that the *Girondin* would reach Ferriby on the following day, and he determined to see the operation carried out.
===Chinese===
在屋里时他看过表，才十点刚过一点儿。可见本森必是搭了比往常早的一班车回来的。探长又暗自庆幸事情竟是这般结局，因为即便被人撞见，他也不愁自身安全，可若发现得太早，匪帮的其余党羽或许就得以脱身了。
开往赫尔的末班车已经开走，他便迈开步子，徒步六英里进城。天气愈发糟了，此刻风力几乎抵得上半场风暴，裹在一片群魔乱舞般的喧嚣里绕着他打旋，又把一阵阵迷眼的骤雨刮进他眼里。不多时他便浑身湿透，风势的推搡也使他步履迟缓。但他挣扎着往前赶，晚间这趟差办得如此圆满，这点苦楚他全不放在心上。
第二天早晨再回味这桩事，他越发满意了。他干得确实漂亮！不但完成了他着手去办的事——查出杀害科伯恩的凶手——还远远不止这些：他揭出了当代最大的一起走私阴谋之一。诚然，他还没有乘胜追击、办结针对辛迪加的案子，但这本不归他管：走私不在苏格兰场的职分之内，那是海关总署的事。可这案子要是假钞案该有多好！想到那本可能归于他的荣耀，他长叹了一声。
不过，既然走到了这一步，他想索性把白兰地确实按他设想的方式卸载这一点也坐实了。他估算*吉伦丁号*第二天就会抵达费里比，便决意亲眼看看这套作业怎样进行。

===Original===
He followed the plan of Hilliard and Merriman to the extent of hiring a boat in Hull and sculling gently down towards the wharf as dusk fell. He had kept a watch on the river all day without seeing the motor ship go up, but now she passed him a couple of miles above the city. He turned inshore when he saw her coming, lest Captain Beamish’s binoculars might reveal to him a familiar countenance.
He pulled easily, timing himself to arrive at the wharf as soon as possible after dark. The evening was dry, but the southeasterly wind still blew cold and raw, though not nearly so strongly as on the night of his walk.
There were a couple of lights on the *Girondin*, and he steered by these till the dark mass of her counter, looming up out of the night, cut them off. Slipping round her stern, as Hilliard had done in the River Lesque, he unshipped his oars and guided the boat by his hands into the V-shaped space between the two rows of piles fronting the wharf.
