I dare say in summer we could put ten thousand on her if we tried, but she is rather shallow in the draught for it, and we don’t care to run any risks. Hallo, captain! Back again?” he broke off, as a man in a blue pilot cloth coat and a peaked cap emerged from below.
The newcomer was powerfully built and would have been tall, but for rather rounded shoulders and a stoop. He was clean shaven, with a heavy jaw and thin lips which were compressed into a narrow line. His expression was vindictive as well as somewhat crafty, and he looked a man who would not be turned from his purpose by nice points of morality or conscience.
Though Hilliard instinctively noted these details, they did not particularly excite his interest. But his interest was nevertheless keenly aroused. For he saw the man, as his gaze fell on himself and Merriman, give a sudden start, and then flash a quick, questioning glance at Mr. Coburn. The action was momentary, but it was enough to bring back with a rush all Hilliard’s suspicions. Surely, he thought, there must be something if the sight of a stranger upsets all these people in this way.
But he had not time to ponder the problem. The captain instantly recovered himself, pulled off his cap to Miss Coburn and shook hands all round, Mr. Coburn introducing the visitors.
“Good trip, captain?” the manager went on. “You’re ahead of schedule.”
“Not so bad,” the newcomer admitted in a voice and manner singularly cultivated for a man in his position. “We had a good wind behind us most of the way.”
===Chinese===
“她每趟装六七千根坑木，”科伯恩先生告诉他们，“这还是不装甲板货的数目。真要使劲装，我敢说夏天装它一万根也成，只是她吃水浅了些，吃不住这个，我们也不愿冒任何风险。嘿，船长！又回来啦？”他说到一半忽然收住，因为一个穿蓝色海军呢大衣、戴大檐帽的人正从下面走上来。
来人生得魁梧，若不是肩膀溜圆、微微佝背，本可以算个高个子。他脸刮得干干净净，下颌厚重，两片薄唇抿成一道细线。那副神情既凶狠记仇，又透着几分狡诈，一看便知是个不大会被道德良心之类的细讲究绊住、认准了目标便不回头的人。
这些细节希利亚德都本能地看在眼里，倒也没有怎么引起他的注意；可他的兴味还是被狠狠勾了起来。因为他看见，那人目光落到他和梅里曼身上时猛地一怔，随即飞快地、带着询问的意味瞟了科伯恩先生一眼。这个动作只在瞬息之间，却足以让希利亚德的满腹疑心一涌而回。他想，这些人一见生人便这般失态，事情背后必定有鬼。
可他来不及细想这个问题。船长立刻恢复了常态，向科伯恩小姐脱帽致意，又同众人一一握手，科伯恩先生在旁边给双方引见。
“一路顺利吗，船长？”经理接着说，“你这趟可比预定的提前了。”
“还不错，”来人应道；以他那等身份的人而论，他的谈吐举止斯文得出奇。“一路上大半都有顺风相送。”

===Original===
They chatted for a few moments, then started on their tour of inspection. Though Hilliard was once again keenly on the alert, the examination, so far as he could see, left nothing to be desired. They visited every part of the vessel, from the forecastle storerooms to the tunnel of the screw shaft, and from the chart-house to the bottom of the hold, and every question either of the friends asked was replied to fully and without hesitation.
That evening, like the preceding, they passed with the Coburns. The captain and the engineer﻿—a short, thickset man named Bulla﻿—strolled up with them and remained for dinner, but left shortly afterwards on the plea of matters to attend to on board. The friends stayed on, playing bridge, and it was late when they said good night and set out to walk back to the launch.
During the intervals of play Hilliard’s mind had been busy with the mystery which he believed existed in connection with the syndicate, and he had decided that to try to satisfy his curiosity he would go down to the wharf that night and see if any interesting operations went on under cover of darkness. The idea of a midnight loading of contraband no longer appealed to his imagination, but vaguely he wished to make sure that no secret activities were in progress.
===Chinese===
众人闲谈了几句，便开始参观。尽管希利亚德又一次竖起了全部警觉，但依他看，这次查看真可谓无可挑剔。从艏楼的储藏室到螺旋桨轴的轴隧，从海图室到货舱底，船上每一处他们都走到了；两位朋友不论谁提问题，对方都答得完完全全、毫不迟疑。
当晚和前一晚一样，是在科伯恩家度过的。船长和轮机长——一个矮壮敦实、名叫布拉（Bulla）的人——同他们一起踱了过去，留下来吃晚饭，饭后不久便推说船上有事要料理，告辞回船。两位朋友留下打桥牌，直到夜深才互道晚安，步行回艇。
打牌的间隙，希利亚德的脑子一直围着那个他相信缠在辛迪加身上的谜打转，并且打定了主意：为满足好奇心，今夜要下到码头去一趟，看看黑暗的掩护底下有没有什么耐人寻味的举动。午夜偷装禁货的想象，如今已吊不起他的兴致，可他模模糊糊地还想确认一下，没有别的秘密活动正在进行。

===Original===
He was at least certain that none had taken place up to the present﻿—that Mr. Coburn was personally concerned in, at all events. From the moment they had first sighted the ship until they had left the manager’s house at the conclusion of the game of bridge, not five minutes ago, he had been in Mr. Coburn’s company. Next day it was understood they were to meet again, so that if the manager wished to carry out any secret operations they could only be done during the night.
Accordingly when they reached the launch he turned to Merriman.
“You go ahead, old man. I’m going to have a look round before turning in. Don’t wait up for me. Put out the light when you’ve done with it and leave the companion unlatched so that I can follow you in.”
Merriman grunted disapprovingly, but offered no further objection. He clambered on board the launch and disappeared below, while Hilliard, remaining in the collapsible boat, began to row silently upstream towards the wharf.
The night was dark and still, but warm. The moon had not risen, and the sky was overcast, blotting out even the small light of the stars. There was a faint whisper of air currents among the trees, and the subdued murmur of the moving mass of water was punctuated by tiny splashes and gurgles as little eddies formed round the stem of the boat or wavelets broke against the banks. Hilliard’s eyes had by this time become accustomed to the gloom, and he could dimly distinguish the serrated line of the trees against the sky on either side of him, and later, the banks of the clearing, with the faint, ghostly radiance from the surface of the water.
===Chinese===
至少有一点他确凿无疑：到目前为止，凡是有科伯恩先生亲自参与的，这类活动还不曾有过。从望见那条船起，直到五分钟前打完桥牌离开经理的家，他一直同科伯恩先生待在一处。第二天双方说好了还要见面，因此经理若要干点什么秘密勾当，就只能在夜里进行了。
于是到了艇边，他转向梅里曼。
“你先睡吧，老兄。我上床前想出去转一圈。不用等我。灯用完了就熄掉，舱口别闩上，我好跟着进去。”
梅里曼不以为然地哼了一声，却也没有再拦。他爬上机动艇，钻进舱里不见了；希利亚德留在折叠小艇上，开始悄悄地朝上游的码头划去。
夜又黑又静，却不冷，暖洋洋的。月亮还没有升起来，天上覆着云，连星星那一点微光也遮没了。树林里有气流的一丝低语，一大股流动的水发出沉闷的呜咽，其间缀着一星一点的水声——小旋涡绕着船头打转，或是细浪拍上岸畔，泊泊作响。到这时，希利亚德的眼睛已经习惯了昏暗，隐约能分辨出两侧映在天幕上的锯齿般的树影；再后来，凭着水面泛起的幽幽微光，连林间空地的两岸也认得出了。

===Original===
He pulled on with swift, silent strokes, and presently the dark mass of the *Girondin* loomed in sight. The ship, longer than the wharf, projected for several feet above and below it. Hilliard turned his boat inshore with the object of passing between the hull and the bank and so reaching the landing steps. But as he rounded the vessel’s stern he saw that her starboard side was lighted up, and he ceased rowing, sitting motionless and silently holding water, till the boat began to drift back into the obscurity downstream. The wharf was above the level of his head, and he could only see, appearing over its edge, the tops of the piles of pit-props. These, as well as the end of the ship’s navigating bridge and the gangway, were illuminated by, he imagined, a lamp on the side of one of the deckhouses. But everything was very still, and the place seemed deserted.
Hilliard’s intention had been to land on the wharf and, crouching behind the props, await events. But now he doubted if he could reach his hiding place without coming within the radius of the lamp and so exposing himself to the view of anyone who might be on the watch on board. He recollected that the port or river side of the ship was in darkness, and he thought it might therefore be better if he could get directly aboard there from the boat.
Having removed his shoes he rowed gently round the stern and examined the side for a possible way up. The ship being light forward was heavily down in the stern, and he found the lower deck was not more than six or seven feet above water level. It occurred to him that if he could get hold of the mooring rope pawls he might be able to climb aboard. But this after a number of trials he found impossible, as in the absence of someone at the oars to steady the boat, the latter always drifted away from the hull before he could grasp what he wanted.
===Chinese===
他迅捷无声地划着桨，不多时，*吉伦丁号*黑黢黢的船影赫然在望。船比码头长，前后各有几英尺探出码头之外。希利亚德把船往岸边拐，想从船壳和河岸之间穿过去，摸到下码头的小台阶。可他绕过船尾时，看见船的右舷亮着灯，便停了桨，一动不动地坐着，悄悄按住水，任船往下游的暗影里漂回去。码头高过他的头顶，探出码头边缘能望见的，只有一垛垛坑木的顶。这些坑木，连同船尾舰桥的一角和那道跳板，都被——他猜想——某座甲板室侧壁上的一盏灯照着。四下里一片沉寂，整个地方像是空无一人。
希利亚德原先的打算，是登上码头，蜷在坑木后面静候事态。可如今他犯疑了：要摸到那个藏身之处，只怕免不了走进那盏灯的光照范围，从而暴露在船上守望之人的眼里。他记起船的左舷——朝河的一面——黑着，心想与其如此，倒不如索性从船上那一侧直接登船。
他脱下鞋，轻轻绕过船尾，察看那一侧有没有可以上船的去处。船头空着，船尾便深深下沉，他发现下层甲板离水面不过六七英尺。他想到，倘若能抓着系缆桩，也许就能攀上船去。可是试了若干回都没成功：没有人替他把着桨稳住小艇，不等他抓住要抓的东西，艇总要先漂离船壳。

===Original===
He decided he must risk passing through the lighted area, and, having for the third time rowed round the stern, he brought the boat up as close to the hull as possible until he reached the wharf. Then passing in between the two rows of piles and feeling his way in the dark, he made the painter fast to a diagonal, so that the boat would lie hidden should anyone examine the steps with a light. The hull lay touching the vertical piles, and Hilliard, edging along a waling to the front of the wharf, felt with his foot through the darkness for the stern belting. The tide was low and he found this was not more than a foot above the timber on which he stood. He could now see the deck light, an electric bulb on the side of the captain’s cabin, and it showed him the top of the taffrail some little distance above the level of his eyes. Taking his courage in both hands and stepping upon the belting, he succeeded in grasping the taffrail. In a moment he was over it and on deck, and in another moment he had slipped round the deckhouse and out of the light of the lamp. There he stopped, listening for an alarm, but the silence remained unbroken, and he believed he had been unobserved.
He recalled the construction of the ship. The lower deck, on which he was standing, ran across the stern and formed a narrow passage some forty feet long at each side of the central cabin. This cabin contained the galley and mess room as well as the first officer’s quarters. Bulla’s stateroom, Hilliard remembered, was down below beside the engine-room.
From the lower deck two ladders led to the bridge deck at the forward end of which was situated the captain’s stateroom. Aft of this building most of the remaining bridge deck was taken up by two lifeboats, canvas-covered and housed in chocks. On the top of the captain’s cabin was the bridge and chart-house, reached by two ladders which passed up at either side of the cabin.
===Chinese===
他决定豁出去闯那片亮处。第三次绕过船尾之后，他把艇贴着船壳尽量拢上去，一直划到码头跟前。然后他从两排桩柱之间穿进去，在黑暗中摸索着把艇头缆拴牢在一根斜撑上，这样万一有人提灯来查看台阶，艇也不致露馅。船壳紧贴着直桩，希利亚德顺着一道横档蹭到码头前沿，用脚在黑暗里探寻船尾的护舷木。潮水正低，他发现护舷木比他脚下的木料高出不出一英尺。这时他已看得见那盏甲板灯——船长舱侧壁上的一只电灯泡——它照出船尾栏杆的顶端，比他眼睛的水平高出不多的一截。他横下心，踏上护舷木，一把抓住了栏杆。转眼间他已翻过栏杆落上甲板，又一转眼，他已绕过甲板室，闪出了灯光所及的范围。他在那儿停住，听有没有警报，四周依旧寂静无声，他相信自己没有被人瞧见。
他回想这条船的构造。他此刻站着的下层甲板横过船尾，在中央那间舱房的两侧各形成一条四十英尺来长的窄过道。那间舱房里是厨房、餐室，还有大副的住舱。至于布拉的舱房，希利亚德记得，在下面机舱旁边。
从下层甲板有两架梯子通上舰桥甲板，甲板前端是船长的住舱。住舱后面的舰桥甲板，大部分地方摆着两条救生艇，盖着帆布，安放在艇架上。船长舱顶上便是舰桥和海图室，沿舱房两侧各有一架梯子通上去。

===Original===
Hilliard, reconnoitering, crept round to the port side of the ship. The lower deck was in complete darkness, and he passed the range of cabins and silently ascended the steps to the deck above. Here also it was dark, but a faint light shone from the window of the captain’s cabin. Stealthily Hilliard tiptoed to the porthole. The glass was hooked back, but a curtain hung across the opening. Fortunately, it was not drawn quite tight to one side, and he found that by leaning up against the bridge ladder he could see into the interior. A glance showed him that the room was empty.
As he paused irresolutely, wondering what he should do next, he heard a door open. There was a step on the deck below, and the door slammed sharply. Someone was coming to the ladder at the top of which he stood.
Like a shadow Hilliard slipped aft, and, as he heard the unknown ascending the steps, he looked round for cover. The starboard boat and a narrow strip of deck were lighted up, but the port boat was in shadow. He could distinguish it merely as a dark blot on the sky. Recognizing that he must be hidden should the port deck light be turned on, he reached the boat, felt his way round the stern, and, crouching down, crept as far underneath it as he could. There he remained motionless.
The newcomer began slowly to pace the deck, and the aroma of a good cigar floated in the still air. Up and down he walked with leisurely, unhurried footsteps. He kept to the dark side of the ship, and Hilliard, though he caught glimpses of the red point of the cigar each time the other reached the stern, could not tell who he was.
===Chinese===
希利亚德一面侦察，一面匍匐着绕到船的左舷。下层甲板一团漆黑，他走过那一排舱房，悄然登上通上一层甲板的梯级。上面也是黑的，只有船长舱的窗户透出一点微光。希利亚德蹑手蹑脚地挨到舷窗前。窗玻璃钩开着，窗洞上却挂着帘子。所幸帘子没有完全拉严到一边，他发觉，只要倚住舰桥的梯子，就能望进里面。一眼看去，舱里没有人。
他正踌躇着下一步该怎么办，忽听得一扇门开了。下面甲板响起脚步，跟着门砰的一声关上。有人朝梯子走来——他就站在这架梯子的顶端。
希利亚德影子似的溜向船尾，一面听着那生人踏上梯级，一面四下里寻找掩蔽。右舷的救生艇和一条窄甲板被照亮着，左舷的艇却罩在暗影里，只认得出是映在天幕上的一团黑影。他明白，一旦左舷的甲板灯拧亮，自己就非藏起来不可，于是摸到艇边，绕过艇尾，猫下身子，尽力往艇底下钻，随即一动不动。
来人开始缓缓地在甲板上踱步，上等雪茄的香气在凝滞的空气里浮动。他迈着从容不迫的步子走来走去，专拣船的暗侧走；希利亚德只能在那人每次走到船尾时瞥见雪茄的红点，却认不出他是谁。

===Original===
Presently other footsteps announced the approach of a second individual, and in a moment Hilliard heard the captain’s voice.
“Where are you, Bulla?”
“Here,” came in the engineer’s voice from the first-comer. The captain approached and the two men fell to pacing up and down, talking in low tones. Hilliard could catch the words when the speakers were near the stern, but lost them when they went forward to the break of the poop.
“Confound that man Coburn,” he heard Captain Beamish mutter. “What on earth is keeping him all this time?”
“The young visitors, doubtless,” rumbled Bulla with a fat chuckle, “our friends of the evening.”
“Yes, confound them, too,” growled Beamish, who seemed to be in an unenviable frame of mind. “Damned nuisance their coming round. I should like to know what they are after.”
===Chinese===
不多时，另一串脚步声宣告又来了一个人，随即希利亚德听见了船长的嗓音。
“你在哪儿呢，布拉？”
“在这儿，”先来的那个人用轮机长的嗓音应道。船长走近，两人便来回踱着，低声交谈。说话的人在船尾近旁时，希利亚德还听得清字句，待他们走到船尾楼前端，声音便丢掉了。
“科伯恩这家伙真见鬼，”他听见比米什船长（Captain Beamish）咕哝道，“到底什么事把他绊了这么久？”
“多半是那两位年轻客人，”布拉呵呵一笑，嗓音瓮声瓮气，“咱们今晚上的朋友嘛。”
“是啊，这两个也一并见鬼去，”比米什咆哮道，他那副心绪可实在说不上好，“他们转来转去，讨厌透顶。我倒想知道，他们到底图个什么。”

===Original===
“Nothing particular, I should fancy. Probably out doing some kind of a holiday.”
They passed round the deckhouse and Hilliard could not hear the reply. When they returned Captain Beamish was speaking.
“﻿—thinks it would about double our profits,” Hilliard heard him say. “He suggests a second depot on the other side, say at Swansea. That would look all right on account of the South Wales coalfields.”
“But we’re getting all we can out of the old hooker as it is,” Bulla objected. “I don’t see how she could do another trip.”
“Archer suggests a second boat.”
===Chinese===
“我看没什么特别的。多半是出门度什么假罢了。”
两人绕到甲板室那一头去了，回答的话希利亚德没有听见。再绕回来时，开口的是比米什船长。
“——认为这样差不多能把利润翻上一番，”希利亚德听见他说，“他提议在对岸再设一处货栈，比如斯旺西（Swansea）。有南威尔士（South Wales）煤田摆在那里，这一着看上去就名正言顺了。”
“可咱们这条老船，眼下的油水已经榨到头了，”布拉反对道，“我看她哪还挤得出一趟来。”
“阿彻（Archer）提议再添一条船。”

===Original===
“Oh.” The engineer paused, then went on: “But that’s no new suggestion. That was proposed before ever the thing was started.”
“I know, but the circumstances have changed. Now we should﻿—”
Again they passed out of earshot, and Hilliard took the opportunity to stretch his somewhat cramped limbs. He was considerably interested by what he had heard. The phrase Captain Beamish had used in reference to the proposed depot at Swansea﻿—“it would look all right on account of the coalfields”﻿—was suggestive. Surely that was meaningless unless there was some secret activity﻿—unless the pit-prop trade was only a blind to cover some more lucrative and probably more sinister undertaking? At first sight it seemed so, but he had not time to think it out then. The men were returning.
===Chinese===
“哦。”轮机长顿了顿，又说：“可这不算什么新提议。事情还没开张的时候，早就有人提过。”
“我知道，可如今情形变了。现在咱们应该——”
两人又走出了听觉范围，希利亚德趁机舒展了一下蜷得发麻的四肢。方才听到的话使他大感兴趣。比米什船长提到拟议中的斯旺西货栈时说的那句——“有煤田摆在那里，看上去就名正言顺”——颇可玩味。倘若没有什么秘密活动——倘若坑木生意只是一个幌子，底下盖着一桩更赚钱、多半也更凶险的营生——这话岂不成了空话？乍看确是如此，可他来不及细想，那两个人走过来了。

===Original===
Bulla was speaking this time, and Hilliard soon found he was telling a somewhat improper story. As the two men disappeared round the deckhouse he heard their hoarse laughter ring out. Then the captain cried: “That you, Coburn?” The murmur of voices grew louder and more confused and immediately sank. A door opened, then closed, and once more silence reigned.
To Hilliard it seemed that here was a chance which he must not miss. Coming out from his hiding place, he crept stealthily along the deck in the hope that he might find out where the men had gone, and learn something from their conversation.
The captain’s cabin was the probable meeting place, and Hilliard slipped silently back to the window through which he had glanced before. As he approached he heard a murmur of voices, and he cautiously leaned back against the bridge ladder and peeped in round the partly open curtain.
Three of the four seats the room contained were now occupied. The captain, engineer, and Mr. Coburn sat round the central table, which bore a bottle of whisky, a soda siphon and glasses, as well as a box of cigars. The men seemed preoccupied and a little anxious. The captain was speaking.
===Chinese===
这一回说话的是布拉，希利亚德很快听出，他在讲一个不大正经的故事。两人绕过甲板室不见了，随后传来他们嘶哑的笑声。接着船长喊道：“是你吗，科伯恩？”人语的嗡嗡声先是变响、变乱，随即沉了下去。一扇门开了，又关上，四下重归寂静。
在希利亚德看来，这是个万万不可错过的良机。他从藏身处钻出来，蹑手蹑脚地沿甲板摸过去，指望探明那几个人去了哪里，再从他们的谈话里听出点东西。
聚头的地点多半是船长舱，希利亚德悄没声儿地溜回方才偷看过的那扇窗前。走近时听得里面一片嗡嗡人语，他小心翼翼地倚上舰桥的梯子，从半开的帘边往里窥探。
舱里四把椅子，此刻坐了三把。船长、轮机长和科伯恩先生围坐在中间的桌旁，桌上摆着一瓶威士忌、一只苏打水瓶和几只玻璃杯，还有一盒雪茄。几个人都像心里有事，略显焦灼。船长正在说话。

===Original===
“And have you found out anything about them?” he asked Mr. Coburn.
“Only what I have been able to pick up from their own conversation,” the manager answered. “I wrote Morton asking him to make inquiries about them, but of course there hasn’t been time yet for a reply. From their own showing one of them is Seymour Merriman, junior partner of Edwards & Merriman, Gracechurch Street, Wine Merchants. That’s the dark, square-faced one﻿—the one who was here before. The other is a man called Hilliard. He is a clever fellow, and holds a good position in the Customs Department. He has had this launch for some years, and apparently has done the same kind of trip through the Continental rivers on previous holidays. But I could not find out whether Merriman had ever accompanied him before.”
“But you don’t think they smell a rat?”
“I don’t think so,” he said slowly, “but I’m not at all sure. Merriman, we believe, noticed the number plate that day. I told you, you remember. Henri is sure that he did, and Madeleine thinks so too. It’s just a little queer his coming back. But I’ll swear they’ve seen nothing suspicious this time.”
===Chinese===
“他们的底细，你打听到什么了吗？”他问科伯恩先生。
“也只是从他们自己的言谈里零零碎碎听来的，”经理答道，“我写了信给莫顿（Morton），托他去打听这两个人，不过回信自然还没那么快。照他们自己的说法，其中一个叫西摩·梅里曼，是格雷斯丘奇街爱德华兹—梅里曼酒商行的初级合伙人，就是那个黑皮肤、方脸盘的——从前来过的那一个。另一个叫希利亚德，是个聪明人，在海关总署担任着不错的职位。这条机动艇他置了几年了，看样子从前的假期里也做过同一路的大陆江河之旅。只是梅里曼从前可曾同他结过伴，我就打听不出来了。”
“可你不觉得他们嗅出了味儿？”
“我想还没有，”他慢吞吞地说，“可我全无把握。我们相信，梅里曼那一天注意到了号码牌。我告诉过你们的，记得吧。亨利拿准他看见了，玛德琳也这么想。他这回又回来，多少有点蹊跷。不过我可以起誓，这一回他们什么可疑的东西都没瞧见。”

===Original===
“You can’t yourself account for his coming back?”
Again Mr. Coburn hesitated.
“Not with any certainty,” he said at last, then with a grimace he continued: “But I’m a little afraid that it’s perhaps Madeleine.”
Bulla, the engineer, made a sudden gesture.
“I thought so,” he exclaimed. “Even in the little I saw of them this evening I thought there was something in the wind. I guess that accounts for the whole thing. What do you say, skipper?”
===Chinese===
“他为什么回来，你自己也说不出个所以然？”
科伯恩先生又迟疑了一下。
“说不确切，”他终于开口，随即做了个苦脸，接着说，“只是我有点担心，这或许是因为玛德琳。”
轮机长布拉猛地做了个手势。
“我早料到是这么回事！”他叫道，“今晚我统共才见了他们那么一小会儿，就觉得这里头有名堂。我看这一下就全解释通了。你说呢，船长？”

===Original===
The big man nodded.
“I should think so,” he admitted, with a look of relief. “I think it’s a mare’s nest, Coburn. I don’t believe we need worry.”
“I’m not so sure,” Coburn answered slowly. “I don’t think we need worry about Merriman, but I’m hanged if I know what to think about Hilliard. He’s pretty observant, and there’s not much about this place that he hasn’t seen at one time or another.”
“All the better for us, isn’t it?” Bulla queried.
“So far as it goes, yes,” the manager agreed, “and I’ve stuffed him with yarns about costs and about giving up the props and going in for paving blocks and so on which I think he swallowed. But why should he want to know what we are doing? What possible interest can the place have for him﻿—unless he suspects?”
===Chinese===
大个子点了点头。
“我看也是，”他承认道，脸上露出如释重负的神情，“我看这是一场虚惊，科伯恩。我不信咱们有什么可担心的。”
“我可不敢这么笃定，”科伯恩慢吞吞地答道，“梅里曼那边倒不必担心，可对希利亚德该怎么办，我委实说不上来。他眼力很尖，这地方有什么名堂，几乎没有他前后没看在眼里的。”
“那对咱们不是更有利吗？”布拉问。
“就这一层说，是的，”经理表示同意，“我还拿成本啦、放弃坑木改做铺路块啦之类的一套瞎话把他灌了个饱，我想他是囫囵吞下了。可他为什么还想知道我们在干什么？这地方对他能有什么了不得的兴味——除非他起了疑心？”

===Original===
“They haven’t done anything suspicious themselves?”
“Not that I have seen.”
“Never caught them trying to pump any of the men?”
“Never.”
Captain Beamish moved impatiently.
“I don’t think we need worry,” he repeated with a trace of aggression in his manner. “Let’s get on to business. Have you heard from Archer?”
===Chinese===
“他们自己没干过什么可疑的事吧？”
“至少我没有看见。”
“也没撞见他们向手下人套过话？”
“从来没有。”
比米什船长不耐烦地动了动。
“我说用不着担心，”他又说了一遍，神态里带着几分咄咄逼人，“说正事吧。阿彻那儿有信来吗？”

===Original===
Mr. Coburn drew a paper from his pocket, while Hilliard instinctively bent forward, believing he was at last about to learn something which would throw a light on these mysterious happenings. But alas for him! Just as the manager began to speak he heard steps on the gangway which passed on board and a man began to climb the starboard ladder to the upper deck.
Hilliard’s first thought was to return to his hiding place under the boat, but he could not bring himself to go so far away from the center of interest, and before he had consciously thought out the situation he found himself creeping silently up the ladder to the bridge. There he believed he would be safe from observation while remaining within earshot of the cabin, and if anyone followed him up the ladder he could creep round on the roof of the cabin to the back of the chart-house, out of sight.
The newcomer tapped at the captain’s door and, after a shout of “Come in,” opened it. There was a moment’s silence, then Coburn’s voice said:
“We were just talking of you, Henri. The skipper wants to know﻿—” and the door closed.
Hilliard was not long in slipping back to his former position at the porthole.
“By Jove!” Bulla was saying. “And to think that two years ago I was working a little coaster at twenty quid a month! And you, Coburn; two years ago you weren’t much better fixed, if as well, eh?”
===Chinese===
科伯恩先生从口袋里掏出一份文件；希利亚德不由自主地朝前凑去，心想这回总该能听到些什么，给这些神秘的勾当照出点亮了。可惜他运气不济！经理刚要开口，他就听见跳板上有脚步声上了船，一个人开始顺着右舷的梯子往上爬。
希利亚德的第一个念头是回到艇底下那个藏身处去，可他舍不得离这台好戏的中心那么远；还没等有意识地想清楚眼前的处境，他发觉自己已经悄然顺着梯子往舰桥上爬了。在那儿，他想，既听得见舱里的动静，又不致被人看见；万一有人跟着上梯子，他还可以顺着舱顶爬到海图室背后，躲出视界之外。
来人敲了敲船长的门，里面喊了一声“进来”，他便推门而入。片刻的静默之后，只听科伯恩的声音说：
“我们正说你呢，亨利。船长想知道——”门随即关上了。
希利亚德不多时便溜回舷窗边原来的位置。
“嗬！”布拉正在说话，“想想看，两年前我还开着一条小近海货船，挣每月二十镑的薪水呢！还有你，科伯恩——两年前你手头也阔不到哪儿去，至多跟我半斤八两，嗯？”

===Original===
Coburn ignored the question.
“It’s good, but it’s not good enough,” he declared. “This thing can’t run for ever. If we go on too long somebody will tumble to it. What we want is to try to get our piles made and close it down before anything happens. We ought to have that other ship running. We could double our income with another ship and another depot. And Swansea seems to me the place.”
“Bulla and I were just talking of that before you came aboard,” the captain answered. “You know we have considered that again and again, and we have always come to the conclusion that we are pushing the thing strongly enough.”
“Our organisation has improved since then. We can do more now with less risk. It ought to be reconsidered. Will you go into the thing, skipper?”
===Chinese===
科伯恩没有理会这个问题。
“日子是好，可还不够好，”他宣称，“这营生不能永远干下去。拖得太久，总会有人看出破绽。我们要的是趁没出乱子，把各自的大钱挣足，然后收摊关张。那条新船早就该让它跑起来。多一条船、多一处货栈，咱们的进项就能翻上一番。我看斯旺西就是个合适的地方。”
“你上船之前，我和布拉正说这事呢，”船长答道，“你知道，这个我们翻来覆去考虑过多少回，回回的结论都是：摊子已经铺得够大了。”
“可如今咱们的组织比那时强了。眼下的咱们，能干得更多，担的风险更小。这事应该重新考虑。你就把它研究一下吧，船长？”

===Original===
“Certainly. I’ll bring it before our next meeting. But I won’t promise to vote for it. In our business it’s not difficult to kill the goose, etcetera.”
The talk drifted to other matters, while Hilliard, thrilled to the marrow, remained crouching motionless beneath the porthole, concentrating all his attention on the conversation in the hope of catching some word or phrase which might throw further light on the mysterious enterprise under discussion. While the affair itself was being spoken of he had almost ceased to be aware of his surroundings, so eagerly had he listened to what was being said, but now that the talk had turned to more ordinary subjects he began more or less subconsciously to take stock of his own position.
He realised in the first place that he was in very real danger. A quick movement either of the men in the cabin or of some member of the crew might lead to his discovery, and he had the uncomfortable feeling that he might pay the forfeit for his curiosity with his life. He could imagine the manner in which the “accident” would be staged. Doubtless his body, showing all the appearance of death from drowning, would be found in the river with alongside it the upturned boat as evidence of the cause of the disaster.
And if he should die, his secret would die with him. Should he not then be content with what he had learned and clear out while he could, so as to ensure his knowledge being preserved? He felt that he ought, and yet the desire to remain in the hope of doing still better was overpowering. But as he hesitated the power of choice was taken away. The men in the cabin were making a move. Coburn finished his whisky, and he and Henri rose to their feet.
===Chinese===
“可以。下次开会我把它提出来。不过我不保证投它的赞成票。干咱们这一行，把下金蛋的鹅宰掉并不难——诸如此类。”
谈话转到了别的题目上；希利亚德兴奋彻骨，一直蜷在舷窗下面一动不动，全副心神都聚在那席话上，指望再捕捉到只言片语，给这桩正在议论的神秘营生多照出些亮光。谈那件事本身时，他听得那样专注，几乎忘了周遭的一切；如今话题转上寻常轨道，他才多少下意识地掂量起自身的境地来。
他首先意识到，自己身临的凶险实实在在。舱里的人也好，船上哪一名船员也好，只消动作快上一步，他就可能败露；他有一种很不舒服的感觉：这好奇心的代价，也许得拿命来付。那场“意外”会怎样布置，他都想象得出。毫无问题，人们会在河里发现他的尸身，一应溺亡的迹象俱全，旁边翻着那条小艇，作为这场灾祸的明证。
而他若死了，他掌握的秘密也就随他一同死去。那么，他是不是该见好就收，趁还走得脱赶紧撤出去，好保全已经到手的情报？他觉得理应如此，可是想留下再搏一把的欲望压倒了一切。就在他迟疑不决的当口，选择权被剥夺了：舱里的人起身要走。科伯恩喝干杯中的威士忌，同亨利一齐站了起来。

===Original===
“Well,” the former said, “There’s one o’clock. We must be off.”
The others stood up also, and at the same moment Hilliard crept once more up the ladder to the bridge and crouched down in the shadow of the chart-house. Hardly was he there when the men came out of the cabin to the deck beneath the bridge, then with a brief exchange of “Good nights,” Coburn and the lorry driver passed down the ladder, crossed the gangway and disappeared behind a stack of pit-props on the wharf. Bulla with a grunted “ ’Night” descended the port steps and Hilliard heard the door leading below open and shut; the starboard deck lamp snapped off, and finally the captain’s door shut and a key turned in the lock. Some fifteen minutes later the faint light from the porthole vanished and all was dark and silent.
But for more than an hour Hilliard remained crouching motionless on the bridge, fearing lest some sound that he might make in his descent should betray him if the captain should still be awake. Then, a faint light from the rising moon appearing towards the east, he crept from his perch, and crossing the gangway, reached the wharf and presently his boat.
Ten minutes later he was on board the launch.
===Chinese===
“好了，”前者说，“一点钟了。咱们该走了。”
其余的人也站起身来；与此同时，希利亚德再次爬上通舰桥的梯子，蜷进海图室的暗影里。他刚待稳，那几个人便走出舱房，来到舰桥下面的甲板上，草草互道了几声“晚安”，科伯恩和那个卡车司机便下了梯子，走过跳板，消失在码头上一垛坑木后面。布拉咕哝了一声“晚安”，走下左舷的梯级，希利亚德听见通往下层的门开了又关上；右舷的甲板灯啪地熄了；最后，船长的门关上，钥匙在锁里转了一圈。约莫一刻钟后，舷窗里的微光也消失了，四下一片黑暗和寂静。
可希利亚德在舰桥上足足蜷伏了一个多钟头，一动也不敢动，生怕船长还没有睡着，自己下去时弄出半点声响坏了事。后来，东方升起一弯月亮，透出淡淡的清光，他才从高处爬下来，过了跳板，上了码头，不多时便摸到了自己的小艇。
十分钟后，他已经回到机动艇上了。

## XIX Willis Spreads His Net / 十九、威利斯撒下罗网
===Original===
Though Inspector Willis had spent so much time out of London in his following up of the case, he had by no means lost sight of Madeleine Coburn and Merriman. The girl, he knew, was still staying with her aunt at Eastbourne, and the local police authorities, from whom he got his information, believed that her youth and health were reasserting themselves, and that she was rapidly recovering from the shock of her father’s tragic death. Merriman haunted the town. He practically lived at the George, going up and down daily to his office, and spending as many of his evenings and his Sundays at Mrs. Luttrell’s as he dared.
But though the young man had worn himself almost to a shadow by his efforts, he felt that the realisation of his hopes was as far off as ever. Madeleine had told him that she would not marry him until the mystery of her father’s murder was cleared up and the guilty parties brought to justice, and he was becoming more and more afraid that she would keep her word. In vain he implored her to consider the living rather than the dead, and not to wreck his life and her own for what, after all, was but a sentiment.
But though she listened to his entreaties and was always kind and gentle, she remained inflexible in her resolve. Merriman felt that his only plan, failing the discovery of Mr. Coburn’s assassin, was unobtrusively to keep as much as possible in her company, in the hope that she would grow accustomed to his presences and perhaps in time come to need it.
===Chinese===
威利斯探长虽为追办此案，久已不在伦敦，对玛德琳·科伯恩和梅里曼二人却丝毫没有放松注意。他知道，姑娘仍住在伊斯特本她姨母家；他消息的来源——当地警方——认为，青春与健康正在她身上重新占了上风，她正从父亲惨死的震动中迅速恢复过来。梅里曼则在城里流连不去。他简直就住在乔治旅馆，每天上办事处去，晚间和星期日，凡是他敢去的，都尽量泡在卢特雷尔太太（Mrs. Luttrell）家里。
不过，这年轻人虽然操劳得几乎形销骨立，却觉得自己的希望实现起来，仍旧同从前一样遥遥无期。玛德琳告诉过他，在父亲的命案水落石出、罪魁祸首被绳之以法之前，她是不会嫁给他的；而他越来越担心，她说到就会做到。他恳求她顾念活人而不要顾念死者，不要为了一件说到底不过是感情用事的事，毁掉他的一生、也毁掉她自己的一生——可这都是徒劳。
然而她虽听他苦苦恳求，待他也始终温婉亲切，自己的决心却纹丝不动。梅里曼觉得，在找到杀害科伯恩先生的凶手这一条路之外，他唯一的办法，便是不动声色地尽量多陪在她身边，指望她渐渐习惯有他在旁，久而久之，或许竟至于离不开他。

===Original===
Under these circumstances his anxiety as to the progress of the case was very great, and on several occasions he had written to Willis asking him how his inquiry was going on. But the inspector had not been communicative, and Merriman had no idea how matters actually stood.
It was therefore with feelings of pleasurable anticipation that he received a telephone call from Willis at Scotland Yard.
“I have just returned from Bordeaux,” the inspector said, “and I am anxious to have a chat with Miss Coburn on some points that have arisen. I should be glad of your presence also, if possible. Can you arrange an interview?”
===Chinese===
在这样的情形下，他对案情的进展忧心如焚，已经不止一次写信给威利斯，打听他的侦查办到了哪一步。可是探长口风很紧，梅里曼根本不知道事情究竟到了什么田地。
正因如此，接到苏格兰场的威利斯打来的电话时，他心里不禁升起一阵愉快的期待。
“我刚从波尔多回来，”探长说，“有几件新冒出来的事，我想同科伯恩小姐谈一谈。要是可能的话，我也希望您在场。您能安排一次会面吗？”

===Original===
“Do you want her to come to town?”
“Not necessarily; I will go to Eastbourne if more convenient. But our meeting must be kept strictly secret. The syndicate must not get to know.”
Merriman felt excitement and hope rising within him.
“Better go to Eastbourne then,” he advised. “Come down with me tonight by the 5:20 from Victoria.”
“No,” Willis answered, “we mustn’t be seen together. I shall meet you at the corner of the Grand Parade and Carlisle Road at nine o’clock.”
===Chinese===
“您要她进城来吗？”
“不一定；要是更方便，我可以去伊斯特本。不过我们的会面必须严守秘密，绝不能让辛迪加知道。”
梅里曼觉得兴奋和希望在自己胸中涌了起来。
“那就去伊斯特本好，”他提议道，“今晚跟我一起搭维多利亚（Victoria）开出的 5 点 20 分车来吧。”
“不行，”威利斯答道，“咱们不能让人看见走在一起。九点钟，我在大帕雷德（Grand Parade）和卡莱尔路（Carlisle Road）的拐角上同你碰头。”

===Original===
This being agreed on, both men began to make their arrangements. In Merriman’s case these consisted in throwing up his work at the office and taking the first train to Eastbourne. At five o’clock he was asking for Miss Coburn at Mrs. Luttrell’s door.
“Dear Madeleine,” he said, when he had told her his news, “you must not begin to expect things. It may mean nothing at all. Don’t build on it.”
But soon he had made her as much excited as he was himself. He stayed for dinner, leaving shortly before nine to keep his appointment with Willis. Both men were to return to the house, when Madeleine would see them alone.
===Chinese===
事情就这样讲定，两人分头安排起来。轮到梅里曼，安排不外是把办事处的工作一撂，搭上去伊斯特本的头班火车。五点钟，他已经在卢特雷尔太太门前求见科伯恩小姐了。
“亲爱的玛德琳，”他把消息讲完之后说，“你可不能这就指望起什么来。这也许根本什么也算不上。别把它当指望。”
可是不大工夫，他已经撩得她同他自己一样兴奋了。他留下吃了晚饭，快到九点时动身去赴威利斯的约。两人到时要一同回到这所房子里，由玛德琳单独见他们。

===Original===
Inspector Willis did not travel by Merriman’s train. Instead he caught the 5:35 to Brighton, dined there, and then slipping out of the hotel, motored over to Eastbourne. Dismissing his vehicle at the Grand Hotel, he walked down the Parade and found Merriman at the rendezvous. In ten minutes they were in Mrs. Luttrell’s drawing-room.
“I am sorry, Miss Coburn,” Willis began politely, “to intrude on you in this way, but the fact is, I want your help and indirectly the help of Mr. Merriman. But it is only fair, I think, to tell you first what has transpired since we last met. I must warn you, however, that I can only do so in the strictest confidence. No whisper of what I am going to say must pass the lips of either of you.”
“I promise,” said Merriman instantly.
“And I,” echoed Madeleine.
“I didn’t require that assurance,” Willis went on. “It is sufficient that you understand the gravity of the situation. Well, after the inquest I set to work,” and he briefly related the story of his investigations in London and in Hull, his discoveries at Ferriby, his proof that Archer was the actual murderer, the details of the smuggling organisation and, finally, his suspicion that the other members of the syndicate were privy to Mr. Coburn’s death, together with his failure to prove it.
His two listeners heard him with eager attention, in which interest in his story was mingled with admiration of his achievement.
===Chinese===
威利斯探长没有乘梅里曼那班车。他改搭 5 点 35 分去布莱顿（Brighton）的火车，在那里用了晚餐，随后悄悄溜出旅馆，乘汽车越过原野到伊斯特本去。他在格兰德旅馆（Grand Hotel）门前打发走车子，沿着帕雷德大道走去，在约好的地点找到了梅里曼。十分钟后，两人已经坐在卢特雷尔太太家的客厅里了。
“很抱歉，科伯恩小姐，”威利斯客气地开了腔，“这样冒昧登门打扰。实情是，我需要您的帮助，间接地也需要梅里曼先生的帮助。不过我想，先把我上次见面以来办的事向二位交代清楚，才算公道。然而我必须提醒你们，我讲的话只能以最严格的机密相待。我即将说出的话，绝不能从你们哪一位嘴里漏出半个字。”
“我保证。”梅里曼立刻说。
“我也保证。”玛德琳应道。
“这层保证我本不必求，”威利斯接着说，“你们了解局势的严重，这就足够了。是这样，验尸讯问过后我就着手干了。”于是他扼要叙述了自己在伦敦和赫尔的侦查经过，在费里比的种种发现，证实阿彻正是凶手的证据，走私组织的内情细节，末了讲到他怀疑辛迪加的其余成员都参与了科伯恩先生之死，以及他无法证实这一点的苦恼。
两位听的人全神贯注，既听得出神，又对他这一番作为暗暗佩服。

===Original===
“So Hilliard was right about the brandy after all!” Merriman exclaimed. “He deserves some credit for that. I think he believed in it all the time, in spite of our conclusion that we had proved it impossible. *By* Jove! *How* you can be had!”
Willis turned to him.
“Don’t be disappointed about your part in it, sir,” he advised. “I consider that you and Mr. Hilliard did uncommonly well. I may tell you that I thought so much of your work that I checked nothing of what you had done.”
Merriman coloured with pleasure.
“Jolly good of you to say so, I’m sure, inspector,” he said; “but I’m afraid most of the credit for that goes to Hilliard.”
“It was your joint work I was speaking of,” Willis insisted. “But now to get on to business. As I said, my difficulty is that I suspect the members of the syndicate of complicity in Mr. Coburn’s death, but I can’t prove it. I have thought out a plan which may or may not produce this proof. It is in this that I want your help.”
===Chinese===
“这么说，希利亚德关于白兰地的判断到底是对的！”梅里曼失声道，“这一点上他该得几分功劳。我看他自始至终都是相信的，尽管咱们当时得出结论，说已经证明那不可能。天哪！咱们竟会上这么大的当！”
威利斯朝他转过身去。
“别为自己在这一段里的份儿难过，先生，”他劝道，“我认为您和希利亚德先生干得出奇地好。不妨告诉您，我对你们的工作看重到什么地步——你们做过的事，我一样都没有复核。”
梅里曼高兴得脸都红了。
“您能这样说，真是太好了，探长，”他说，“不过恐怕那多半是希利亚德的功劳。”
“我说的正是你们两人的共同功劳，”威利斯坚持道，“不过现在谈正事。如我所说，我的难处在于：我怀疑辛迪加的成员们是科伯恩先生之死的同谋，却拿不出证据。我想出了一个计划，它也许能、也许不能拿出这个证据。我要请你们帮的，就是这个忙。”

===Original===
“Mr. Inspector,” cried Madeleine reproachfully, “need you ask for it?”
Willis laughed.
“I don’t think so. But I can’t very well come in and command it, you know.”
“Of course you can,” Madeleine returned. “You know very well that in such a cause Mr. Merriman and I would do anything.”
“I believe it, and I am going to put you to the test. I’ll tell you my idea. It has occurred to me that these people might be made to give themselves away. Suppose they had one of their private meetings to discuss the affairs of the syndicate, and that, unknown to them, witnesses could be present to overhear what was said. Would there not at least be a sporting chance that they would incriminate themselves?”
===Chinese===
“探长先生，”玛德琳嗔怪地叫道，“这还用您开口求吗？”
威利斯笑了。
“我也觉得不用。不过您知道，我总不能闯进门来征用吧。”
“您当然可以，”玛德琳答道，“您很清楚，为了这样的事业，梅里曼先生和我什么都肯做。”
“这我相信，而且我正要考一考你们。我把想法说出来。我想到的是：可以让这伙人自己招供，露出马脚。假使他们开一次内部会议，商议辛迪加的事务，而在他们毫无察觉的情况下，有证人到场，偷听他们说的话——那么，他们说出的话把自己牵连进去，至少颇有几分胜算，是不是？”

===Original===
“Yes!” said Merriman, much interested. “Likely enough. But I don’t see how you could arrange that.”
Willis smiled slightly.
“I think it might be managed,” he answered. “If a meeting were to take place we could easily learn where it was to be held and hear what went on. But the first point is the difficulty—the question of the holding of the meeting. In the ordinary course there might be none for months. Therefore we must take steps to have one summoned. And that,” he turned to Madeleine, “is where I want your help.”
===Chinese===
“对呀！”梅里曼大感兴趣地说，“很可能如此！可我看不出您怎么能把这一层安排妥当。”
威利斯淡淡一笑。
“我想还是有办法安排的，”他答道，“只要会议开得起来，我们不难打听出开在哪里，也不难听到会上讲了些什么。难就难在头一环——会议能不能开得起来。照常理，也许几个月都开不了一次。所以我们必须设法促使他们召集一次。而这个，”他转向玛德琳，“正是我要请您帮忙的地方。”

===Original===
His hearers stared, mystified, and Willis resumed.
“Something must happen of such importance to the welfare of the syndicate that the leaders will decide that a full conference of the members is necessary. So far as I can see, you alone can cause that something to happen. I will tell you how. But I must warn you that I fear it will rake up painful memories.”
Madeleine, her lips parted, was hanging on his words.
“Go on,” she said quickly, “we have settled all that.”
“Thank you,” said Willis, taking a sheet of paper from his pocket. “I have here the draft of a letter which I want you to write to Captain Beamish. You can phrase it as you like; in fact I want it in your own words. Read it over and you will understand.”
===Chinese===
两位听的人怔怔地望着他，满腹狐疑，威利斯接着说了下去。
“必须出一件大事，大到关系辛迪加的安危，叫那几个头领觉得非开一次全体成员大会不可。依我看，只有您一个人，能叫这件大事发生。怎么做，我这就告诉您。但我必须先提醒您，只怕这会把痛苦的往事重新翻出来。”
玛德琳微微张着嘴唇，一字不漏地盼着他往下讲。
“说下去吧，”她急急地说，“这些我们都讲定了。”
“谢谢，”威利斯说着，从口袋里掏出一页纸来，“我这儿有一封信的底稿，要您写给比米什船长。措辞随您的意；说实话，我要的就是用您自己的口气写。您先读一遍，就明白了。”

===Original===
The draft ran as follows:
> “Dear Captain Beamish—In going over some papers belonging to my late father, I learn to my surprise that he was not a salaried official of your syndicate, but a partner. It seems to me, therefore, that as his heir I am entitled to his share of the capital of the concern, or at all events to the interest on it. I have to express my astonishment that no recognition of this fact has as yet been made by the syndicate.
> “I may say that I have also come on some notes relative to the business of the syndicate, which have filled me with anxiety and dismay, but which I do not care to refer to in detail in writing.
> “I think I should like an interview with you to hear your explanation of these two matters, and to discuss what action is to be taken with regard to them. You could perhaps find it convenient to call on me here, or I could meet you in London if you preferred it.
> “Yours faithfully,
> “Madeleine Coburn.”
===Chinese===
底稿如下：
> “亲爱的比米什船长——在整理先父的文件时，我惊讶地得知，他并不是贵辛迪加的受薪雇员，而是一名合伙人。因此，在我看来，作为他的继承人，我有权取得他在该企业资本中的份额，退一步说，至少也有权取得这份份额的利息。而辛迪加至今没有对这一事实作出任何表示，对此我不得不表示惊愕。
> “我还可以奉告：我又见到了一些关于辛迪加业务的笔记，它们使我满心忧虑与惊骇，但我不愿在信中细谈。
> “我想同您面谈一次，听您对这两件事作个解释，并商讨该采取何种处置。您若觉得方便，或许可以屈尊到舍下来见我；您若愿意，我也可以去伦敦同您会面。
> “您忠实的，
> “玛德琳·科伯恩”

===Original===
Madeleine made a grimace as she read this letter.
“Oh,” she cried, “but how could I do that? I didn’t find any notes, you know, and besides—it would be so dreadful—acting as a decoy—”
“There’s something more important than that,” Merriman burst in indignantly. “Do you realise, Mr. Inspector, that if Miss Coburn were to send that letter she would put herself in very real danger?”
===Chinese===
玛德琳读着这封信，做了个苦脸。
“哦，”她叫道，“可我怎么能做这种事？您知道，我根本没发现什么笔记，再说——那也太可怕了——当诱饵——”
“还有比这更要紧的呢，”梅里曼愤然插言道，“探长先生，您可曾想到，科伯恩小姐若是发出那封信，她就把自己置于实实在在的危险之中了？”

===Original===
“Not at all,” Willis answered quietly. “You have not heard my whole scheme. My idea is that when Beamish gets that letter he will lay it before Archer, and they will decide that they must find out what Miss Coburn knows, and get her quieted about the money. They will say: ‘We didn’t think she was that kind, but it’s evident she is out for what she can get. Let’s pay her a thousand or two a year as interest on her father’s alleged share—it will be a drop in the bucket to us, but it will seem a big thing to her—and that will give us a hold on her keeping silence, if she really does know anything.’ Then Beamish will ask Miss Coburn to meet him, probably in London. She will do so, not alone, but with some near friend, perhaps yourself, Mr. Merriman, seeing you were at the clearing and know something of the circumstances. You will be armed, and in addition I shall have a couple of men from the Yard within call—say, disguised as waiters, if a restaurant is chosen for the meeting. You, Miss Coburn, will come out in a new light at that meeting. You will put up a bluff. You will tell Captain Beamish you know he is smuggling brandy, and that the money he offers won’t meet the case at all. You must have £25,000 down paid as the value of your father’s share in the concern, and in such a way as will raise no suspicion that you knew what was in progress. The interview we can go into in detail later, but it must be so arranged that Beamish will see Mr. Merriman’s hand in the whole thing. On the £25,000 being paid the incriminating notes will be handed over. You will explain that as a precautionary measure you have sent them in a sealed envelope to your solicitor, together with a statement of the whole case, with instructions to open the same that afternoon if not reclaimed before that by yourself in person. Now with regard to your objection, Miss Coburn. I quite realise what an exceedingly nasty job this will be for you. In ordinary circumstances I should not suggest it. But the people against whom I ask you to act did not hesitate to lure your father into the cab in which they intended to shoot him. They did this by a show of friendliness, and by playing on the trust he reposed in them, and they did it deliberately and in cold blood. You need not hesitate from nice feeling to act as I suggest in order to get justice for your father’s memory.”
Madeleine braced herself up.
“I know you are right, and if there is no other way I shall not hesitate,” she said, but there was a piteous look in her eyes. “And you will help me, Seymour?” She looked appealingly at her companion.
Merriman demurred on the ground that, even after taking all Willis’s precautions, the girl would still be in danger, but she would not consider that aspect of the question at all, and at last he was overborne. Madeleine with her companion’s help then rewrote the letter in her own phraseology, and addressed it to Captain Beamish, c/o Messrs. The Landes Pit-Prop Syndicate, Ferriby, Hull. Having arranged that he would receive immediate telephonic information of a reply, Willis left the house and was driven back to Brighton. Next morning he returned to London.
===Chinese===
“完全没有的事，”威利斯平静地答道，“你们还没听完我的全盘方案。我的设想是：比米什一接到那封信，就会把它呈给阿彻，两人会商定，必须查明科伯恩小姐到底知道些什么，并在钱的问题上让她安下心来。他们会说：‘想不到她是这种人，可看样子她分明是奔着能捞一把来的。那就每年付她一千两千，算作她父亲那笔所谓股份的利息——这在我们不过是沧海一粟，在她眼里可就成了个大数目——倘若她当真知道些什么，这也正好攥住她，叫她守口如瓶。’然后比米什就会请科伯恩小姐同他见面，多半是在伦敦。她赴约时不会独自一人，而是带着一位亲近的朋友，也许就是您，梅里曼先生——毕竟您到过那片林间空地，对种种情由略知一二。你们要带上武器，此外我还会派苏格兰场的两个人守在近旁听候召唤——譬如，会面若定在一家餐馆，就让他们扮成侍者。科伯恩小姐，在那次会面上，您要以一副全新的面目出场。您要唱一出空城计。您要告诉比米什船长，您知道他在走私白兰地，他出的那点钱根本摆不平这件事；必须当场付清 25,000 英镑，作为令尊在该企业中股份的代价，而且交款的方式要不露一点痕迹，叫人看不出您早已知情。会面的情形我们以后可以细商，但必须安排得让比米什看出，整件事背后有梅里曼先生的手笔。25,000 英镑一到手，那些足以定罪的笔记便交出来。您就解释说：为防不测，您已把它们连同全案的书面陈述，装进一只封好的信封，送交了您的律师，并吩咐他，若当天下午之前不蒙您亲自取回，便将信封拆阅。至于您的顾虑，科伯恩小姐，我完全明白，这对您将是一桩多么别扭难堪的差事。换在寻常情况下，我是绝不会提这个主意的。可是，我请您对付的这伙人，当初把你父亲诱进那辆他们打算就在里面毙掉他的出租汽车时，可曾有过半分迟疑？他们是装出一副友好的面孔，利用他对他们的信任做下的，而且是蓄意为之、冷酷为之。为了替您父亲的在天之灵讨回公道，您不必因为一时的仁心体恤，就对我提议的做法踌躇不前。”
玛德琳挺直了身子。
“我知道您是对的，只要别无他路，我就不会迟疑，”她说，可眼里含着一副哀恳的神情。“您会帮着我的吧，西摩？”她向同伴投去求恳的目光。
梅里曼推托说，即便把威利斯的防范措施一一用上，姑娘仍会身处险境；可她对问题的这一面根本不予考虑，最后他终于被说服了。于是玛德琳在同伴的协助下，用自己的措辞重写了那封信，写明由费里比、赫尔的朗德坑木辛迪加转交比米什船长收。一切安排妥当，只等回信一到，威利斯便会立刻得到电话通知；随后他离开那所宅子，乘车回布莱顿去了。第二天早晨，他返回伦敦。

===Original===
The *Girondin*, he reckoned, would reach Ferriby on the following Friday, and on the Thursday he returned to Hull. He did not want to be seen with Hunt, as he expected the latter’s business would by this time be too well known. He therefore went to a different hotel, ringing up the Excise man and arranging a meeting for that evening.
Hunt turned up about nine, and the two men retired to Willis’s bedroom, where the inspector described his doings at Bordeaux. Then Hunt told of his discoveries since the other had left.
“I’ve got all I want at last,” he said. “You remember we both realised that those five houses were getting in vastly more brandy than they could possibly sell? Well, I’ve found out how they are getting rid of the surplus.”
Willis looked his question.
“They are selling it round to other houses. They have three men doing nothing else. They go in and buy anything from a bottle up to three or four kegs, and there is always a good reason for the purchase. Usually it is that they represent a publican whose stock is just out, and who wants a quantity to keep him going. But the point is that all the purchases are perfectly in order. They are openly made and the full price is paid. But, following it up, I discovered that there is afterwards a secret rebate. A small percentage of the price is refunded. This pays everyone concerned and ensures secrecy.”
Willis nodded.
===Chinese===
他估计*吉伦丁号*将在下星期五抵达费里比，星期四那天他便回到了赫尔。他不愿被人看见同亨特在一起，因为他料想后者此番的差事，到这时多半已闹得人尽皆知。于是他换了一家旅馆住下，打电话给那位消费税局的官员，约好当晚见面。
九点前后亨特来了，两人进了威利斯的卧室，探长把自己在波尔多的经过讲了一遍。随后亨特也讲了对方走后自己的发现。
“你要的东西我终于全弄到手了，”他说，“你还记得吧，咱们俩都看出来，那五家酒馆进的白兰地，多得根本卖不完？嗯，我查出了他们是怎么打发那批余货的。”
威利斯的眼光替他问出了这个问题。
“他们转手卖给别的酒馆。有三个人专干这一件事。他们上门买货，少则一瓶，多则三四小桶，而每一笔买卖总有个说得过去的因由。通常的说法是，他们代表某位存货刚告罄的酒馆老板，要进一批货应急。但妙处在于，所有这些买卖全都规规矩矩：明来明去地成交，十足照价付款。可是顺着往下追查，我发现事后有一笔回扣是私下给的——货款的一小成会退回去。这一手把经手人个个喂饱，也保住了秘密。”
威利斯点了点头。

===Original===
“It’s well managed all through,” he commented. “They deserved to succeed.”
“Yes, but they’re not going to. All the same my discoveries won’t help you. I’m satisfied that none of these people know anything of the main conspiracy.”
Early on the following morning Willis was once more at work. Dawn had not completely come when he motored from the city to the end of the Ferriby lane. Ten minutes after leaving his car he was in the ruined cottage. There he unearthed his telephone from the box in which he had hidden it, and took up his old position at the window, prepared to listen in to whatever messages might pass.
He had a longer vigil than on previous occasions, and it was not until nearly four that he saw Archer lock the door of his office and move towards the filing-room. Almost immediately came Benson’s voice calling: “Are you there?”
They conversed as before for a few minutes. The *Girondin*, it appeared, had arrived some hours previously with a cargo of “1375.” It was clear that the members of the syndicate had agreed never to mention the word “gallons.” It was, Willis presumed, a likely enough precaution against eavesdroppers, and he thought how much sooner both Hilliard and himself would have guessed the real nature of the conspiracy, had it not been observed.
Presently they came to the subject about which Willis was expecting to hear. Beamish, the manager explained, was there and wished to speak to Archer.
===Chinese===
“从头到尾办得滴水不漏，”他评论道，“他们本来理应成功。”
“是啊，可他们成功不了啦。不过话说回来，我的发现帮不上你的忙。我确信，这些人没有一个知道那桩大阴谋的内情。”
第二天一清早，威利斯又干了起来。天还没有完全亮，他就乘汽车出城，开到费里比那条小巷的尽头。下车十分钟，他已身在破败的村舍。他从藏电话的小箱子里把话机起了出来，在窗口重新占好老位置，准备收听线路上可能传过的一切消息。
这一回守候的时辰比前几次都长，直到将近四点，他才看见阿彻锁上办公室的门，朝档案室走去。几乎紧接着，本森的声音呼叫起来：“喂，在吗？”
两人像先前那样谈了几分钟。看情形，*吉伦丁号*几小时前已经抵港，运来了一批“1375”。显然，辛迪加的成员们有约在先，绝不提“加仑”二字。威利斯揣想，这多半是防人窃听的一个不难想到的预防措施；他心想，倘若没有这条规矩，不论希利亚德还是他自己，本该早得多就猜破这桩阴谋的真相。
不多时，他们谈到了威利斯正等着听的那个话题。经理说，比米什就在那边，想同阿彻讲话。

===Original===
“That you, Archer?” came in what Willis believed he recognised as the captain’s voice. “I’ve had rather a nasty jar, a letter from Madeleine Coburn. Wants Coburn’s share in the affair, and hints at knowledge of what we’re really up to. Reads as if she was put up to it by someone, probably that ——— Merriman. Hold on a minute and I’ll read it to you.” Then followed Madeleine’s letter.
Archer’s reply was short but lurid, and Willis, not withstanding the seriousness of the matter, could not help smiling.
There was a pause, and then Archer asked:
“When did you get that?”
===Chinese===
“是你吗，阿彻？”一个嗓音传来，威利斯相信自己听出那是船长的声音。“我挨了一记不小的闷棍——玛德琳·科伯恩来了封信。要科伯恩在那桩买卖里的份额，还暗示她知道咱们暗地里干的勾当。看那口气，像是有人撺掇着她写的，多半是那个——的梅里曼。等一下，我念给你听。”接下去便是玛德琳那封信。
阿彻的答话简短而粗野，威利斯虽明知此事非同小可，也忍不住露出了笑容。
停了片刻，阿彻问道：
“你什么时候收到信的？”

===Original===
“Now, when we got in; but Benson tells me the letter has been waiting for me for three days.”
“You might read it again.”
Beamish did so, and presently Archer went on:
===Chinese===
“就刚才，我们进港的时候收到的；不过本森告诉我，这封信已经等了我三天了。”
“你不妨再念一遍。”
比米什照办了，随后阿彻接着说：

===Original===
“In my opinion, we needn’t be unduly alarmed. Of course she may know something, but I fancy it’s what you say; that Merriman is getting her to put up a bluff. But it’ll take thinking over. I have an appointment presently, and in any case we couldn’t discuss it adequately over the telephone. We must meet. Could you come up to my house tonight?”
“Yes, if you think it wise?”
“It’s not wise, but I think we must risk it. You’re not known here. But come alone; Benson shouldn’t attempt it.”
“Right. What time?”
“What about nine? I often work in the evenings, and I’m never disturbed. Come round to my study window and I shall be there. Tap lightly. The window is on the right-hand side of the house as you come up the drive, the fourth from the corner. You can slip round to it in the shadow of the bushes, and keep on the grass the whole time.”
“Right. Nine o’clock, then.”
===Chinese===
“依我看，咱们用不着过分慌张。她当然可能知道一点什么，不过我想正像你说的，是梅里曼撺掇她摆的唬人把戏。可这事得细细琢磨。我眼下还有个约会，再说电话里也谈不透。咱们得碰个头。今晚你能上我家来一趟吗？”
“行，只要你觉得稳妥？”
“谈不上稳妥，可我看只好冒这个险。这里没人认得你。不过要一个人来；本森就别试了。”
“好。几点？”
“九点怎么样？我晚上常常做事，从来没人来打扰。你绕到书房窗前来，我就在那儿。轻轻敲两下。你从车道那头过来时，那扇窗在房子的右手一侧，从墙角数起是第四扇。你可以借灌木丛的暗影溜过去，一路只管踩着草皮走。”
“好。那就九点。”

===Original===
The switch of the telephone clicked, and presently Willis saw Archer reappear in his office.
The inspector was disappointed. He had hoped that the conspirators would have completed their plans over the telephone, and that he would have had nothing to do but listen to what they arranged. Now he saw that if he were to gain the information he required, it would mean a vast deal more trouble, and perhaps danger as well.
He felt that at all costs he must be present at the interview in Archer’s study, but the more he thought about it, the more difficult the accomplishment of this seemed. He was ignorant of the plan of the house, or what hiding-places, if any, there might be in the study, nor could he think of any scheme by which he could gain admittance. Further, there was but little time in which to make inquiries or arrangements, as he could not leave his present retreat until dark, or say six o’clock. He saw the problem would be one of the most difficult he had ever faced.
But the need for solving it was paramount, and when darkness had set in he let himself out of the cottage and walked the mile or more to Archer’s residence. It was a big square block of a house, approached by a short winding drive, on each side of which was a border of rhododendrons. The porch was in front, and the group of windows to the left of it were lighted up—the dining-room, Willis imagined. He followed the directions given to Beamish and moved round to the right, keeping well in the shadow of the shrubs. The third and fourth windows from the corner on the right side were also lighted up, and the inspector crept silently up and peeped over the sill. The blinds were drawn down, but that on the third window was not quite pulled to the bottom, and through the narrow slit remaining he could see into the room.
===Chinese===
电话开关咔哒一响，不多时威利斯便看见阿彻重新在办公室里露面。
探长失望了。他本指望这伙阴谋家会在电话里把计划敲定，那样他便只需坐着听他们安排妥当就是。现在他看明白了，要想弄到所需的情况，还须再费不知多少周折，说不定还得担风险。
他觉得，无论如何也要到场旁听阿彻书房里的这次密谈；可是越琢磨，越觉得此事难成。他不知道那所宅子的格局，不知道书房里有没有藏身之处，也想不出什么法子混进门去。再说，能用来打听和布置的时间也少得可怜——不到天黑，比方说不到六点钟，他不能离开眼下这个藏身处。他看得出，这将是他生平所遇到的最棘手的难题之一。
然而这个问题非解决不可，压倒一切。天一擦黑，他便出了村舍，走了一英里多路，来到阿彻的住宅。那是一座四四方方的大宅，一条短短的盘旋车道直通门前，车道两侧各栽着一溜杜鹃。门廊在正前方，门廊左边那一组窗户亮着灯——威利斯猜那是餐室。他按照阿彻指点比米什的路线，绕向宅子右侧，始终藏在灌木的阴影里。右墙从角上数第三扇和第四扇窗也亮着灯，探长悄没声儿地爬近窗台，从窗沿上探看。百叶窗都放了下来，只是第三扇窗的没有拉到底，透过底下剩下的那道窄缝，他可以望进房间里去。

===Original===
It was empty, but evidently only for the time being, as a cheerful fire burned in the grate. Furnished as a study, everything bore the impress of wealth and culture. By looking from each end of the slot in turn, nearly all the floor area and more than half of the walls became visible, and a glance showed the inspector that nowhere in his purview was there anything behind which he might conceal himself, supposing he could obtain admission.
But could he obtain admission? He examined the sashes. They were of steel, hinged and opening inwards in the French manner, and were fastened by a handle which could not be turned from without. Had they been the ordinary English sashes fastened with snibs he would have had the window open in a few seconds, but with these he could do nothing.
He moved round the house examining the other windows. All were fitted with the same type of sash, and all were fastened. The front door also was shut, and though he might have been able to open it with his bent wire, he felt that to adventure himself into the hall without any idea of the interior would be too dangerous. Here, as always, he was hampered by the fact that discovery would mean the ruin of his case.
Having completed the circuit of the building, he looked once more through the study window. At once he saw that his opportunity was gone. At the large desk sat Archer busily writing.
Various expedients to obtain admission to the house passed through his brain, all to be rejected as impracticable. Unless some unexpected incident occurred of which he could take advantage, he began to fear he would be unable to accomplish his plan.
===Chinese===
屋里空无一人，但显然只是暂时的，因为炉膛里燃着一炉欢快的火。这屋子布置成书房的模样，处处带着富裕与教养的印记。从缝隙的两端轮流看过去，几乎整个地面和一多半墙壁都收入眼底；探长一眼便看出，在他视野所及的范围内，没有任何一件东西可以容他藏身——倘若他进得去的话。
可他进得去吗？他察看窗扇。窗扇是钢制的，照法国式样装着铰链、向内开启，靠一个从外面拧不动的把手关牢。若是英国寻常那种用插销扣住的上下推拉窗，他几秒钟就能把窗打开，可对着这种窗，他毫无办法。
他绕着宅子查看其余的窗户。所有的窗都装着同一种窗扇，全都关得严严实实。正门也关着；虽然他未必不能用那根弯铁丝把门捅开，但他觉得，对宅内情形一无所知就贸然闯进门厅，未免太过凶险。在这里，也同往常一样，有一件事缚住他的手脚：一旦被人发现，全案便告完蛋。
绕宅一周之后，他又回到书房窗前看了一眼。他立刻看出，机会已经没有了。大写字台前坐着阿彻，正奋笔疾书。
种种混进宅子的招数在他脑子里过了一遍，又一一批掉，全行不通。他开始担心，除非天上掉下一件可供利用的意外，否则他的计划怕是要落空了。

===Original===
As by this time it was half past eight, he withdrew from the window and took up his position behind a neighbouring shrub. He did not wish to be seen by Beamish, should the latter come early to the rendezvous.
He had, however, to wait for more than half an hour before a dark form became vaguely visible in the faint light which shone through the study blinds. It approached the window, and a tap sounded on the glass. In a moment the blind went up, the sash opened, the figure passed through, the sash closed softly, and the blind was once more drawn down. In three seconds Willis was back at the sill.
The slot under the blind still remained, the other window having been opened. Willis first examined the fastening of the latter in the hope of opening the sash enough to hear what was said, but to his disappointment he found it tightly closed. He had therefore to be content with observation through the slot.
He watched the two men sit down at either side of the fire, and light cigars. Then Beamish handed the other a paper, presumably Madeleine’s letter. Archer having read it twice, a discussion began. At first Archer seemed to be making some statement, to judge by the other’s rapt attention and the gestures of excitement or concern which he made. But no word of the conversation reached the inspector’s ears.
He watched for nearly two hours, getting gradually more and more cramped from his stooping position, and chilled by the sharp autumn air. During all that time the men talked earnestly, then, shortly after eleven, they got up and approached the window. Willis retreated quickly behind his bush.
The window opened softly and Beamish stepped out to the grass, the light shining on his strong, rather lowering face. Archer leaned out of the window after him, and Willis heard him say in low tones, “Then you’ll speak up at eleven?” to which the other nodded and silently withdrew. The window closed, the blind was lowered, and all remained silent.
===Chinese===
这时已八点半，他从窗前退下来，在近旁一丛灌木后面占了位置。万一比米什提前到达约会的地点，他不愿被对方瞧见。
然而他等了半个多小时，才有一个黑色的身影，在书房百叶窗透出的微光里隐约显现。那身影挨近窗口，玻璃上响起轻轻的叩击。转眼之间，百叶窗升起，窗扇打开，那身影闪了进去，窗扇轻轻合上，百叶窗又放了下来。三秒钟后，威利斯已回到了窗台跟前。
由于开的是另一扇窗，百叶底下那道缝还在。威利斯先去查看那扇窗的关锁，指望把窗扇支开一点，好听清里面说的话，可叫他失望的是，窗扇关得死紧。他只得满足于从缝隙里观望。
他看着两人在炉火两边落座，点起雪茄。随后比米什递给对方一张纸，想来就是玛德琳的信。阿彻读了两遍，讨论开始了。起初似乎是阿彻在陈述什么，这从对方凝神屏息的注意和时而激动、时而忧虑的手势上看得出来。但是谈话的内容，一个字也传不进探长的耳朵。
他守望了将近两个钟头，蜷伏的姿势叫他越蜷越麻木，料峭的秋风也把他吹透了。这一整段时间里，两人谈得恳切，随后，十一点刚过，他们起身朝窗口走来。威利斯赶紧缩回灌木后面。
窗子轻轻打开，比米什跨步站到草地上，灯光照在他那张强悍而颇阴沉的脸上。阿彻探身出窗，威利斯听见他低声说：“那么，十一点你会打过来？”对方点点头，悄然退去。窗子合上，百叶放下，四下里归于沉寂。

===Original===
Willis waited for some minutes to let the captain get clear away, then leaving his hiding-place and again keeping on the grass, he passed down the drive and out on to the road. He was profoundly disappointed. He had failed in his purpose, and the only ray of light in the immediate horizon was that last remark of Archer’s. If it meant, as he presumed it did, that the men were to communicate by the secret telephone at eleven in the morning, all might not yet be lost. He might learn then what he had missed tonight.
It seemed hardly worthwhile returning to Hull. He therefore went to the Raven Bar in Ferriby, knocked up the landlord, and by paying four or five times the proper amount, managed to get a meal and some food for the next day. Then he returned to the deserted cottage, he let himself in, closed the door behind him, and lying down on the floor with his head on his arm, fell asleep.
Next morning found him back at his post at the broken window, with the telephone receiver at his ear. His surmise at the meaning of Archer’s remark at the study window proved to be correct, for precisely at eleven he heard the familiar: “Are you there?” which heralded a conversation. Then Beamish’s voice went on:
“I have talked this business over with Benson, and he makes a suggestion which I think is an improvement on our plan. He thinks we should have our general meeting in London immediately after I have interviewed Madeleine Coburn. The advantage of this scheme would be that if we found she possessed really serious knowledge, we could immediately consider our next move, and I could, if necessary, see her again that night. Benson thinks I should fix up a meeting with her at say 10:30 or 11, that I could then join you at lunch at 1:30, after which we could discuss my report, and I could see the girl again at 4 or 5 o’clock. It seems to me a sound scheme. What do you say?”
“It has advantages,” Archer answered slowly. “If you both think it best, I’m quite agreeable. Where then should the meetings be held?”
===Chinese===
威利斯等了几分钟，让船长走远，然后离开藏身之处，照旧踩着草皮，顺着车道走出去，到了大路上。他失望至极。他的目的落了空，眼前地平线上唯一的一线光亮，便是阿彻临别那句话。倘若如他所料，那句话的意思是两人明天上午十一点将通过那条秘密电话线通话，那么一切都还未必输光。到时候，他兴许能把今晚错过的情况补回来。
回赫尔去似乎不值得。于是他去了费里比的“渡鸦”酒吧，敲起店主，靠着付出四五倍的价钱，弄到一顿饭和第二天的一些吃食。随后他回到那座无人居住的村舍，开了锁进去，掩上身后的门，往地上一躺，头枕着手臂，便睡着了。
第二天早晨，他又回到了破窗跟前的岗位上，耳边贴着电话听筒。他对阿彻在书房窗口那句话含义的揣测证明是对的：恰好在十一点，他听到了那句熟悉的“喂，在吗？”——一场谈话的前奏。随后比米什的声音说了下去：
“这件事我同本森商量过了，他提了个建议，我看比咱们的原计划更进一层。他认为，我见过玛德琳·科伯恩之后，应当紧接着就在伦敦开全体大会。这个方案的好处是：倘若我们发现她当真掌握了要命的情况，咱们可以立刻商量下一步棋，必要时，我当晚还可以再见她一面。本森的意思是，我同她的会面约在 10 点 30 分或 11 点，随后我 1 点 30 分同你们一起用午餐，饭后咱们商量我的报告，四五点钟我再去见那姑娘。在我看来，这是个稳妥的方案。你看怎么样？”
“有它的好处，”阿彻慢吞吞地答道，“既然你们两个都觉得这样最好，我没有异议。那么，各场会面定在哪里举行？”

===Original===
“In the case of Miss Coburn there would be no change in our last night’s arrangement; a private sitting-room at the Gresham would still do excellently. If you’re going to town you could fix up some place for our own meeting—preferably close by.”
“Very well, I’m going up on Tuesday in any case, and I’ll arrange something. I shall let Benson know, and he can tell you and the others. I think we should all go up by separate trains. I shall probably go by the 5:03 from Hull on the evening before. Let’s see, when will you be in again?”
“Monday week about midday, I expect. Benson could go up that morning, Bulla and I separately by the 4, and Fox, Henri, and Raymond, if he comes, by the first train next morning. How would that do?”
“All right, I think. The meetings then will be on Tuesday at 11 and 1:30, Benson to give you the address of the second. We can arrange at the meeting about returning to Hull.”
“Righto,” Beamish answered shortly, and the conversation ended.
===Chinese===
“科伯恩小姐这一场，还照昨晚商定的不变；格雷沙姆旅馆的一间私人客厅，照旧再合适不过。你既然要进城，可以替咱们自己的会把地方订好——最好就在近旁。”
“很好，我星期二反正要上去，我会把地方安排好。我会通知本森，让他转告你和其他人。我看咱们都该分头搭不同的火车去。我多半在头天傍晚搭赫尔开出的 5 点 03 分那班。我想想，你下一次什么时候再过来？”
“估计是下下周一中午前后。本森可以当天早晨上去，布拉和我分头搭 4 点那班车，福克斯、亨利和雷蒙——要是他来的话——搭次日一早的头班车。这样安排如何？”
“我看可以。那么两场会面就定在星期二 11 点和 1 点 30 分，第二场的地址由本森告诉你。回赫尔的事，咱们开会时再安排。”
“好极了。”比米什简短地应了一声，谈话便结束了。

===Original===
Willis for once was greatly cheered by what he had overheard. His failure on the previous evening was evidently not going to be so serious as he had feared. He had in spite of it gained a knowledge of the conspirators’ plans, and he chuckled with delight as he thought how excellently his ruse was working, and how completely the gang were walking into the trap which he had prepared. As far as he could see, he held all the trump cards of the situation, and if he played his hand carefully he should undoubtedly get not only the men, but the evidence to convict them.
To learn the rendezvous for the meeting of the syndicate he would have to follow Archer to town, and shadow him as he did his business. This was Saturday, and the managing director had said he was going on the following Tuesday. From that there would be a week until the meeting, which would give more than time to make the necessary arrangements.
Willis remained in the cottage until dark that evening, then, making his way to Ferriby station, returned to Hull. His first action on reaching the city was to send a letter to Madeleine, asking her to forward Beamish’s reply to him at the Yard.
On Monday he began his shadowing of Archer, lest the latter should go to town that day. But the distiller made no move until the Tuesday, travelling up that morning by the 6:15 from Hull.
At 12:25 they reached King’s Cross. Archer leisurely left the train, and crossing the platform, stepped into a taxi and was driven away. Willis, in a second taxi, followed about fifty yards behind. The chase led westwards along the Euston Road until, turning to the left down Gower Street, the leading vehicle pulled up at the door of the Gresham Hotel in Bedford Square. Willis’s taxi ran on past the other, and through the backlight the inspector saw Archer alight and pass into the hotel.
===Chinese===
威利斯这一回可是大受鼓舞。昨晚的失手，看来不至像他担心的那样严重。尽管出了岔子，他还是摸到了阴谋家们的计划；想到自己的计策运转得如此之妙，想到这伙人正一脚踏进他布好的陷阱，他得意得直乐。就他看来，局面里所有的王牌全攥在他手里；只要出牌谨慎，不但人能到手，定他们罪的证据也必定能到手。
要探得辛迪加开会的地点，他就得跟阿彻进城，在他办事时盯他的梢。这天是星期六，那位总经理说过要在下星期二动身。从那时到开会还有一个星期，必要部署的时间绰绰有余。
当晚威利斯在村舍里一直待到天黑，随后摸到费里比车站，回到赫尔。一进城，他头一件事就是给玛德琳去信，请她把比米什的回信转寄到苏格兰场他手中。
星期一，他就开始盯阿彻的梢，生怕对方当天进城。可那位蒸馏酒商到星期二才动身，那天早晨搭的是赫尔 6 点 15 分的车。
12 点 25 分，他们抵达国王十字车站。阿彻不慌不忙下了车，穿过站台，钻进一辆出租汽车走了。威利斯坐着第二辆出租车，跟在后面约五十码处。追逐的路线沿尤斯顿路（Euston Road）一路向西，到高尔街（Gower Street）口向左一拐，前车便停在了贝德福德广场（Bedford Square）格雷沙姆旅馆（Gresham Hotel）门前。威利斯的出租车从旁边赶过头里那辆，借着车尾的灯光，探长看见阿彻下车走进了旅馆。

===Original===
Stopping at a door in Bloomsbury Street, Willis sat watching. In about five minutes Archer reappeared, and again entering his taxi, was driven off southwards. Willis’s car slid once more in behind the other, and the chase recommenced. They crossed Oxford Street, and passing down Charing Cross Road stopped at a small foreign restaurant in a narrow lane off Cranbourne Street.
Willis’s taxi repeated its previous maneuver, and halted opposite a shop from where the inspector could see the other vehicle through the backlight. He thought he had all the information he needed, but there was the risk that Archer might not find the room he required at the little restaurant and have to try elsewhere.
This second call lasted longer than the first, and a quarter of an hour had passed before the distiller emerged and reentered his taxi. This time the chase was short. At the Trocadero Archer got out, dismissed his taxi, and passed into the building. Willis, following discreetly, was in time to see the other seat himself at a table and leisurely take up the bill of fare. Believing the quarry would remain where he was for another half hour at least, the inspector slipped unobserved out of the room, and jumping once more into his taxi, was driven back to the little restaurant off Cranbourne Street. He sent for the manager and drew him aside.
“I’m Inspector Willis from Scotland Yard,” he said with a sharpness strangely at variance with his usual easygoing mode of address. “See here.” He showed his credentials, at which the manager bowed obsequiously. “I am following that gentleman who was in here inquiring about a room a few minutes ago. I want to know what passed between you.”
The manager, who was a sly, evil-looking person seemingly of Eastern blood, began to hedge, but Willis cut him short with scant ceremony.
“Now look here, my friend,” he said brusquely, “I haven’t time to waste with you. That man that you were talking to is wanted for murder, and what you have to decide is whether you’re going to act with the police or against them. If you give us any trouble you may find yourself in the dock as an accomplice after the fact. In any case it’s not healthy for a man in your position to run up against the police.”
===Chinese===
威利斯把车停在布卢姆斯伯里街（Bloomsbury Street）一处门前，坐着守望。约莫五分钟后，阿彻重新露面，又上了那辆出租车，向南驶去。威利斯的车重新滑到前车后面，追逐再开。两车穿过牛津街，沿查令十字路（Charing Cross Road）驶下去，停在克兰伯恩街旁一条窄巷里的一家外国小餐馆门前。
威利斯的出租车故技重施，停在一家铺子对面，探长从那里能借着尾灯望见前一辆车。他以为需要的情报已经到手，只是还有一层风险：万一在那家小餐馆里订不着合意的房间，阿彻说不定得另找地方。
这第二趟停留的工夫比第一趟长，过了一刻钟，那位蒸馏酒商才出来，重新坐进出租车。这一回的追逐很短。到特罗卡德罗（Trocadero），阿彻下了车，打发走出租车，进了那座大楼。威利斯小心地跟进去，正好看见对方拣了张桌子坐下，慢条斯理地拿起菜单。他估计这只猎物至少还会在原地待上半个钟头，便趁人不备溜出餐厅，重新跳上自己的出租车，赶回克兰伯恩街旁那家小餐馆。他把经理请来，拉到一边。
“我是苏格兰场的威利斯探长，”他说道，语气干脆，同他平日随和的谈吐大异其趣。“你看。”他出示了证件，经理见了，点头哈腰。“方才有位先生来这儿打听房间，我正在盯他的梢。我要知道你们之间谈了些什么。”
这经理是个狡诈的、面目不善的家伙，看样子带着东方血统，开口便想搪塞，威利斯毫不客气地打断了他。
“你听着，我的朋友，”他直截了当地说，“我没工夫跟你磨牙。刚才同你说话的那个人，是一桩命案的通缉犯；你要拿定主意的，是站在警察这一边，还是同警察作对。你要是给我们找麻烦，说不定会站到被告席上去，当个事后从犯。再说了，像你这种身份的人，同警察过不去，终究没有什么好处。”

===Original===
His bluff had more effect that it might have had with an Englishman in similar circumstances, and the manager became polite and anxious to assist. Yes, the gentleman had come about a room. He had ordered lunch in a private room for a party of seven for 1:30 on the following Tuesday. He had been very particular about the room, had insisted on seeing it, and had approved of it. It appeared the party had some business to discuss after lunch, and the gentleman had required a guarantee that they would not be interrupted. The gentleman had given his name as Mr. Hodgson. The price had been agreed on.
Willis in his turn demanded to see the room, and he was led upstairs to a small and rather dark chamber, containing a fair-sized oval table surrounded by red plush chairs, a red plush sofa along one side, and a narrow sideboard along another. The walls supported tawdry and dilapidated decorations, in which beveled mirrors and faded gilding bore a prominent part. Two large but quite worthless oil paintings hung above the fireplace and the sideboard respectively, and the window was covered with gelatine paper simulating stained glass.
Inspector Willis stood surveying the scene with a frown on his brow. How on earth was he to secrete himself in this barely furnished apartment? There was not room under the sofa, still less beneath the sideboard. Nor was there any adjoining room or cupboard in which he could hide, his keen ear pressed to the keyhole. It seemed to him that in this case he was doing nothing but coming up against one insoluble problem after another. Ruefully he recalled the conversation in Archer’s study, and he decided that, whatever it cost in time and trouble, there must be no repetition of that fiasco.
===Chinese===
这番唬人的话，比用在同样情形下的英国人身上收效更大，经理顿时客气起来，一心巴望效劳。是这么回事：那位先生是为订房间来的。他订了一间私室，供七个人下星期二 1 点 30 分用午餐。他对那间屋子十分讲究，坚持要亲眼看一看，看过之后表示满意。看样子，那一行人午饭后要谈些事情，那位先生要求保证他们不受打扰。他留下的名字是霍奇森先生（Hodgson）。价钱也讲定了。
威利斯随即要求看看那间屋子，便被人领上楼，进了一间狭小而相当晦暗的屋子：一张不小的椭圆形餐桌，四周围着红色长毛绒椅子，一面墙边靠着一张红色长毛绒长沙发，另一面摆着一张窄长的餐具柜。墙上挂满俗气而陈旧的装饰，斜边镜面和褪色的贴金占着显眼的位置。两幅大而无当、一文不值的油画，分悬在壁炉和餐具柜上方，窗上蒙着一层仿彩色玻璃的明胶纸。
威利斯探长皱着眉头，站在那里把屋里的情形打量了一番。在这间陈设寥寥的屋子里，他究竟怎样才能把自己藏起来？沙发底下钻不进人，餐具柜底下更不必说。也没有什么隔壁的房间或柜橱，可以让他贴着锁眼、竖起耳朵躲藏。他觉得，在这件案子上，自己简直是一个难题接着一个难题地碰壁。他懊恼地想起阿彻书房里那场谈话，于是拿定主意：无论费多少工夫、多少周折，那样的惨败决不容重演。

===Original===
He stood silently pondering over the problem, the manager obsequiously bowing and rubbing his hands. And then the idea for which he was hoping flashed into his mind. He walked to the wall behind the sideboard and struck it sharply. It rang hollow.
“A partition?” he asked. “What is behind it?”
“Anozzer room, sair. A private room, same as dees.”
“Show it to me.”
===Chinese===
他默默站着盘算这个难题，经理在一旁点头哈腰，搓着双手。就在这时，他盼着的那个主意闪进了脑海。他走到餐具柜后面的墙跟前，猛敲了一下。墙里空空作响。
“是隔墙？”他问，“后面是什么？”
“另一间房间，先生。一间私室，跟这间一样。”
“带我去看看。”

===Original===
The “ozzer room” was smaller, but otherwise similar to that they had just left. The doors of the two rooms were beside each other, leading on to the same passage.
“This will do,” Willis declared. “Now look here, Mr. Manager, I wish to overhear the conversation of your customers, and I may or may not wish to arrest them. You will show them up and give them lunch exactly as you have arranged. Some officers from the Yard and myself will previously have hidden ourselves in here. See?”
The manager nodded.
“In the meantime I shall send a carpenter and have a hole made in that partition between the two rooms, a hole about two feet by one, behind the upper part of that picture that hangs above the sideboard. Do you understand?”
===Chinese===
那间“另一间房”比先前那间小些，此外别无二致。两间屋的门紧挨着，开向同一条走廊。
“这就行了，”威利斯宣布道，“你听着，经理先生，我要偷听你这几位顾客的谈话，至于要不要逮捕他们，也许要，也许不要。你只管照原定的安排，把他们领上楼，给他们开午餐。我和苏格兰场的几名警官会事先藏进这间屋。明白？”
经理点了点头。
“与此同时，我会派个木匠来，在两间屋中间那道隔墙上开一个洞，大约两英尺长、一英尺宽，位置就藏在餐具柜上方那幅画的上半幅后头。你懂了吗？”

===Original===
The manager wrung his hands.
“Ach!” he cried. “But *meine Zimmern*! Mine rooms, zey veel pe deestroyed!”
“Your rooms will be none the worse,” Willis declared. “I will have the damage made good, and I shall pay you reasonably well for everything. You’ll not lose if you act on the square, but if not—” he stared aggressively in the other’s face—“if the slightest hint of my plan reaches any of the men—well, it will be ten years at least.”
“It shall be done! All shall happen as you say!”
“It had better,” Willis rejoined, and with a menacing look he strode out of the restaurant.
“The Gresham Hotel,” he called to his driver, as he reentered his taxi.
===Chinese===
经理绞着双手。
“Ach！”他叫道，“可我的房间（*meine Zimmern*）！我的房间，要统统毁掉的呀！”
“你的房间一点也不会糟，”威利斯宣称，“弄坏的地方我会叫人修好，各样事情我也会付你一笔说得过去的钱。你规规矩矩照办，就吃不了亏；要不然——”他咄咄逼人地瞪着对方的脸——“我的计划若有一丝半点风声漏到那伙人耳朵里——哼，少说要吃十年官司。”
“一定照办！一切都按您说的办！”
“最好是这样，”威利斯回了一句，带着一脸威吓，大步走出了餐馆。
“去格雷沙姆旅馆。”他重新坐进出租车时，对司机吩咐道。

===Original===
His manner to the manageress of the Bedford Square hotel was very different from that displayed to the German. Introducing himself as an inspector from the Yard, he inquired the purpose of Archer’s call. Without hesitation he was informed. The distiller had engaged a private sitting-room for a business interview which was to take place at eleven o’clock on the following Tuesday between a Miss Coburn, a Mr. Merriman, and a Captain Beamish.
“So far so good,” thought Willis exultingly, as he drove off. “They’re walking into the trap! I shall have them all. I shall have them in a week.”
At the Yard he dismissed his taxi, and on reaching his room he found the letter he was expecting from Madeleine. It contained that from Beamish, and the latter ran:
===Chinese===
他对贝德福德广场这家旅馆女经理的态度，同方才对那德国人的做派判若两人。他自称是苏格兰场的探长，打听阿彻来此的用意。对方毫不迟疑地照实相告：那位蒸馏酒商订了一间私人客厅，为一场事务性会晤，定在下一个星期二上午十一点钟，会晤双方是一位科伯恩小姐、一位梅里曼先生和一位比米什船长。
“到这一步，一切顺利，”威利斯乘车离去时得意地想道，“他们正往陷阱里走！我要把他们一网打尽。不出一个星期，他们一个也跑不了。”
到了苏格兰场，他打发走出租车；一进自己的房间，就看见了玛德琳那封他正等着的信息。信里装着比米什的回信，全文如下：

===Original===
> “Dear Miss Coburn,—I have just received your letter of 25th inst., and I hasten to reply.
> “I am deeply grieved to learn that you consider yourself badly treated by the members of the syndicate, and I may say at once that I feel positive that any obligations which they may have contracted will be immediately and honorably discharged.
> “It is, however, news to me that your late father was a partner, as I always imagined that he held his position as I do my own, namely, as a salaried official who also receives a bonus based on the profits of the concern.
> “With regard to the notes you have found on the operations of the syndicate, it is obvious that these must be capable of a simple explanation, as there was nothing in the operations complicated or difficult to understand.
> “I shall be very pleased to fall in with your suggestion that we should meet and discuss the points at issue, and I would suggest 11 a.m. on Tuesday, 10th prox., at the Gresham Hotel in Bedford Square, if this would suit you.
> “Walter Beamish.”
===Chinese===
> “亲爱的科伯恩小姐——刚刚收到您本月 25 日的来信，我急急作复。
> “获悉您认为自己受了辛迪加成员们的亏待，我深感痛心；我可以立刻奉告：他们可能承担的任何义务，都必将立即而体面地履行，这一点我确信不疑。
> “不过，令先尊生前竟是一位合伙人，这于我是新闻；我一向以为，他的职位同我的一样，即是一名受薪雇员，此外按企业的利润另领花红。
> “至于您发现的那份关于辛迪加业务的笔记，它们显然必定能有一个简单的解释，因为那些业务中并没有什么复杂难解之处。
> “您提议双方会面、商讨争议各点，我非常乐意遵办；如蒙俯允，我谨建议下月 10 日星期二上午 11 点，在贝德福德广场的格雷沙姆旅馆晤面。
> “沃尔特·比米什”

===Original===
Willis smiled as he read this effusion. It was really quite well worded, and left the door open for any action which the syndicate might decide on. “Ah, well, my friend,” he thought grimly, “you’ll get a little surprise on Tuesday. You’ll find Miss Coburn is not to be caught as easily as you think. Just you wait and see.”
For the next three or four days Willis busied himself in preparing for his great coup. First he went down again to Eastbourne via Brighton, and coached Madeleine and Merriman in the part they were to play in the coming interview. Next he superintended the making of the hole through the wall dividing the two private rooms at the Cranbourne Street restaurant, and drilled the party of men who were to occupy the annex. To his unbounded satisfaction, he found that every word uttered at the table in the larger room was audible next door to anyone standing at the aperture. Then he detailed two picked men to wait within call of the private room at the Gresham during the interview between Madeleine and Beamish. Finally, all his preparations in London complete, he returned to Hull, and set himself, by means of the secret telephone, to keep in touch with the affairs of the syndicate.
===Chinese===
威利斯读着这封信，微微一笑。信写得实在得体，无论辛迪加随后决定采取什么行动，都给它留足了回旋余地。“好啊，我的朋友，”他冷冷地想道，“星期二你就要吃一小惊了。你会发现科伯恩小姐可不像你想的那么好对付。你且等着瞧吧。”
此后三四天，威利斯一心一意筹备他那场大手笔。他先取道布莱顿再次南下伊斯特本，把玛德琳和梅里曼在即将到来的会面中要扮演的角色细细辅导了一番。接着，他督办克兰伯恩街那家餐馆两间私室之间隔墙上开洞的工程，又把要进驻邻室的那一队人马操练了一番。令他无限满意的是，大屋里餐桌旁说出的每一个字，站在隔壁洞口跟前的人都能听见。随后，他派了两名精选的人员，在玛德琳同比米什会晤期间，守在格雷沙姆旅馆那间私人客厅近旁听候召唤。最后，伦敦的各项准备一一停当，他便回到赫尔，凭借那条秘密电话线，继续掌握辛迪加的事态。
